<!-- === 来源: api_reference/concat/concatenate_blocks.md === -->

# concatenate\_blocks[](#concatenate-blocks "Link to this heading")

concatenate\_blocks(*blocks: Iterable[[Block](../main/block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block")]*, *\**, *varname\_mismatch: Literal['raise', 'null'] = 'raise'*, *metadata\_mismatch: Literal['raise', 'first'] = 'raise'*) → [Block](../main/block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block")[](#keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.concatenate_blocks "Link to this definition")
:   Concatenate multiple Blocks into a single Block.

    The concatenation is performed along the observations (axis 0 for all
    data).

    Parameters:
    :   * **blocks** (*iterable of Block*) – An iterable of Blocks to concatenate.
        * **varname\_mismatch** (*{“raise”, “null”}, default “raise”*) – Determines how to handle variable name mismatches between the
          Blocks. If `"raise"`, a ValueError is raised if the variable
          names are not exactly the same for all input Blocks. If
          `"null"`, missing variable(s) are handled by inserting null
          values at the proper observations.
        * **metadata\_mismatch** (*{“raise”, “first”}, default “raise”*) – Determines how to handle metadata mismatches between the Blocks.
          If `"raise"`, a ValueError is raised if any metadata is
          mismatched. If `"first"`, the metadata from the first Block is
          used for the output Block and metadata mismatches are ignored.

    Returns:
    :   A new Block that is the concatenation of the input Blocks along
        the observations (axis 0 for all data).

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](../main/block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Examples

    Let’s demonstrate by concatenating two identical Blocks.

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Dim, DimScale, Block, Var, concatenate_blocks
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> freq_var = Var(
            [1e9, 2e9, 3e9],
            name="freq",
            role="frequency",
            unit="Hz",
            attrs={"foo": np.array([1, 2, 3])},
        )
    >>> portz_var = Var(
            np.array([50, 75, 100, 125, 150, 175]).reshape(3, 2),
            name="PortZ",
            dims=[Dim(), Dim(DimScale(["P1", "P2"], name="portname"), name="i")],
            unit="Ohm",
            role="impedance.port",
            attrs={"spam": 42},
        )
    >>> block1 = Block(
            [freq_var, portz_var],
            name="myblock",
            ivarnames=("freq",),
            attrs={"python": 3.10},
        )
    >>> block2 = Block(
            [freq_var, portz_var],
            name="myblock",
            ivarnames=("freq",),
            attrs={"python": 3.10},
        )
    >>> block_concat = concatenate_blocks([block1, block2])
    >>> block_concat.name
    'myblock'
    >>> block_concat.attrs["python"]
    3.10
    >>> block_concat.ivarnames
    ('freq',)
    >>> block_concat["freq"].to_numpy_ndarray()
    array([1.e+09, 2.e+09, 3.e+09, 1.e+09, 2.e+09, 3.e+09])
    ```

    Now, let’s add a variable to one of the Blocks and see what happens.

    ```
    >>> block2["spam"] = Var([1, 2, 3], name="spam")
    >>> block_concat = concatenate_blocks([block1, block2])
    <raises ValueError>
    ```

    By default, a ValueError is raised if there is a variable name
    mismatch. However, we can use the `varname_mismatch` parameter to
    handle the mismatch differently.

    ```
    >>> block_concat = concatenate_blocks([block1, block2], varname_mismatch="null")
    >>> block_concat["spam"].to_numpy_ndarray()
    masked_array(data=[--, --, --, 1, 2, 3],
                 mask=[ True,  True,  True, False, False, False],
           fill_value=999999)
    ```

    See also

    `concatenate_vars()`, `concatenate_loadpullblocks()`


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/concat/concatenate_loadpullblocks.md === -->

# concatenate\_loadpullblocks[](#concatenate-loadpullblocks "Link to this heading")

concatenate\_loadpullblocks(*loadpullblocks: Iterable[[LoadPullBlock](../loadpull/loadpullblock/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")]*, *\**, *varname\_mismatch: Literal['raise', 'null'] = 'raise'*, *metadata\_mismatch: Literal['raise', 'first'] = 'raise'*) → [LoadPullBlock](../loadpull/loadpullblock/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.concatenate_loadpullblocks "Link to this definition")
:   Concatenate multiple LoadPullBlocks into a single LoadPullBlock.

    The concatenation is performed along the observations (axis 0 for all
    data).

    Parameters:
    :   * **loadpullblocks** (*iterable of LoadPullBlock*) – An iterable of LoadPullBlocks to concatenate.
        * **varname\_mismatch** (*{“raise”, “null”}, default “raise”*) – Determines how to handle variable name mismatches between the
          LoadPullBlocks. If `"raise"`, a ValueError is raised if the
          variable names are not exactly the same for all input
          LoadPullBlocks. If `"null"`, missing variable(s) are handled
          by inserting null values at the proper observations.
        * **metadata\_mismatch** (*{“raise”, “first”}, default “raise”*) – Determines how to handle metadata mismatches between the
          LoadPullBlocks. If `"raise"`, a ValueError is raised if any
          metadata is mismatched. If `"first"`, the metadata from the
          first LoadPullBlock is used for the output LoadPullBlock and
          metadata mismatches are ignored.

    Returns:
    :   A new LoadPullBlock that is the concatenation of the input
        LoadPullBlocks along the observations (axis 0 for all data).

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](../loadpull/loadpullblock/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Examples

    Let’s assume we have already created LoadPUllBlocks by using the
    function
    `keysight.pwdatatools.examples.loadpull.make_swept_gamma_loadpullblock()`
    and demonstrate by concatenating two identical LoadPullBlocks. They
    are stored in Python variables called lpblock1 and lpblock2.

    ```
    >>> import keysight.pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock1
    LoadPullBlock(
        <'Gain', 'DrainEff', ... with 9 observations>,
        name='example',
        gamma_ivarname='GammaLoad',
        attrs={},
    )
    >>> lpblock2
    LoadPullBlock(
        <'Gain', 'DrainEff', ... with 9 observations>,
        name='example',
        gamma_ivarname='GammaLoad',
        attrs={},
    )
    ```

    Note that each LoadPullBlock has 9 observations. We can concatenate
    them into a single LoadPullBlock with 18 observations.

    ```
    >>> lpblock_concat = pwdt.concatenate_loadpullblocks([lpblock1, lpblock2])
    >>> lpblock_concat
    LoadPullBlock(
        <'Gain', 'DrainEff', ... with 18 observations>,
        name='example',
        gamma_ivarname='GammaLoad',
        attrs={},
    )
    ```

    Now, let’s add a variable to one of the LoadPullBlocks and see what
    happens.

    ```
    >>> lpblock1["new_var"] = pwdt.Var([1], name="new_var")
    >>> lpblock_concat = pwdt.concatenate_loadpullblocks([lpblock1, lpblock2])
    <raises ValueError>
    ```

    By default, a ValueError is raised if there is a variable name
    mismatch. However, we can use the `varname_mismatch` parameter to
    handle the mismatch differently.

    ```
    >>> lpblock_concat = pwdt.concatenate_loadpullblocks(
    ...     [lpblock1, lpblock2], varname_mismatch="null"
    ... )
    >>> lpblock_concat["new_var"].to_numpy_ndarray()
    masked_array(data=[1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, --, --, --, --, --, --, --,
                       --, --],
                 mask=[False, False, False, False, False, False, False, False,
                       False,  True,  True,  True,  True,  True,  True,  True,
                       True,  True],
           fill_value=999999)
    ```

    See also

    `concatenate_vars()`, `concatenate_blocks()`


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/concat/concatenate_vars.md === -->

# concatenate\_vars[](#concatenate-vars "Link to this heading")

concatenate\_vars(*vars: Iterable[[Var](../main/var/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")]*, *\**, *metadata\_mismatch: Literal['raise', 'first'] = 'raise'*) → [Var](../main/var/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")[](#keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.concatenate_vars "Link to this definition")
:   Concatenate multiple Vars into a single Var.

    The data is concatenated along axis 0. The new Var’s name, dims, role,
    and unit are taken from the first Var in the iterable.

    Parameters:
    :   * **vars** (*iterable of Var*) – Iterable of Vars to concatenate.
        * **metadata\_mismatch** (*{‘raise’, ‘first’}, default ‘raise’*) – If ‘raise’, raise an error if any of the Vars have inconsistent
          metadata. If ‘first’, use the metadata from the first Var in the
          iterable and ignore any metadata mismatches.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](../main/var/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If any of the Vars have inconsistent metadata and metadata\_mismatch
        is set to ‘raise’.

    See also

    `concatenate_blocks()`, `concatenate_loadpullblocks()`


---

# keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.\_\_init\_\_[](#keysight-pwdatatools-datafile-init "Link to this heading")

DataFile.\_\_init\_\_(*path\_or\_obj: str | PathLike | BaseDataFile*, *format\_override: str | None = None*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.__init__ "Link to this definition")
:   Initialize with a file path or object, and optional format override.

    The file may or may not exist on the filesystem.

    Parameters:
    :   * **path\_or\_obj** – A str, path object, or file object that points to a file
          location. The file may or may not exist.
        * **format\_override** – Optionally override the format that would be inferred by the
          file extension. Also allows to assign a format to files with
          missing file extensions. If `None`, the format is
          determined by the file extension on `path_or_obj`. Defaults
          to `None`.

    Raises:
    :   * **ValueError** – If `path_or_obj` is a directory.
          If invalid `format_override` value.
        * **TypeError** – If `path_or_obj` is wrong type.

DataFile.\_\_repr\_\_()[](#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.__repr__ "Link to this definition")

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/fileio/datafile/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.copy.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.copy[](#keysight-pwdatatools-datafile-copy "Link to this heading")

DataFile.copy(*dst: str | PathLike | BaseDataFile*, *dst\_mode: Literal['x', 'w'] = 'x'*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.copy "Link to this definition")
:   Copy file to another location.

    Parameters:
    :   * **dst** – The destination as a str path, a `pathlib.Path`, or a
          `pwdatatools.BaseDataFile`.
        * **dst\_mode** – The mode to use when opening `dst`. If `dst` does not yet
          exist, both modes yield the same behavior. However, if
          attempting to copy to a pre-existing file, `'w'` overwrites
          the existing file, and `'x'` results in a FileExistsError.
          Defaults to `'x'`.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If `dst_mode` is not `'x'` or `'w'`.
        If `dst_mode='x'` and `dst` already exists.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/fileio/datafile/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.read_as_block.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.read\_as\_block[](#keysight-pwdatatools-datafile-read-as-block "Link to this heading")

DataFile.read\_as\_block(*loc: str = '/'*, *\**, *data: bool = True*, *format\_specific: FormatSpecificReadOptions | None = None*, *idxs: IdxsOption | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*, *varnames: Mapping[str, str] | None = None*) → [Block](../../../main/block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.read_as_block "Link to this definition")
:   Read the DataFile and return a new instance of Block.

    If the DataFile has a missing or unknown file extension, you
    must specify the `Datafile.format_override` parameter. Known
    readable file extensions are stored in
    `options.reading.extentions`. Known readable file formats
    are stored in `options.reading.formats`.

    Parameters:
    :   * **loc** – The location within the DataFile to return as a Block. If
          `'/'` (the “root”), the entire DataFile is read. Otherwise,
          it represents some portion of the DataFile. This may be the
          name of a single datablock (for example, in an MDIF or ADS
          dataset), or the full HDF5 path to a block (an IvITrace) for
          a .pwdt file. Defaults to `'/'`.
        * **data** – A bool that determines whether to read the data. If `False`,
          only the metadata is read. Reading only metadata can be useful
          for quickly examining information about the variables. Defaults
          to `True`.
        * **format\_specific** – By default, the global option
          `options.reading.format_specific` sets all format-
          specific reading options, but this parameter can override the
          global options. If provided, it must be an instance of
          `FormatSpecificReadOptions`. See the docs for a list of
          all classes that can act as format-specific read options. Some
          examples are `ADSReadOptions`,
          `LoadPullReadOptions`, and `LoadPullDerivedVars`.
        * **idxs** (*{‘auto’, True, False}, optional*) – If `True`, create index variables (idxs) when reading the
          source file. If `False`, do not create them. If `'auto'`,
          use the format-specific defaults. If `None`, use the global
          option `options.reading.idxs`.
        * **iffs** – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing. Defaults to `False`.
        * **varnames** – A mapping of variable roles to their names. Defaults to
          `options.reading.varnames`.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](../../../main/block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If data cannot be represented as a single Block.

    Examples

    The below examples assume the following import:

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    ```

    If the DataFile has a known file extension, and you want to read
    the entire file, you can call `read_as_block` with any arguments.
    Known file extensions are stored in
    `options.reading.extentions`.

    ```
    >>> # Using a path with known ext (.lpd)
    >>> datafile = pwdt.DataFile('/home/device.lpd')
    >>> block = datafile.read_as_block()
    ```

    If the DataFile has a missing or unknown file extension, you must
    specify the DataFile’s `format_override` parameter.

    ```
    >>> datafile = pwdt.DataFile('/data/noise.txt', format_override='mdif')
    >>> block = datafile.read_as_block()
    ```

    If you want to perform a partial read, use the `loc` parameter.
    You have to know the name of the part that you want to read. A
    Block is always returned. If the contents of the part cannot be
    represented by a single Block, a ValueError is raised.

    ```
    >>> block = datafile.read_as_block(loc='/SP1.SP')
    ```

    See also

    `from_file()`, [`Block`](../../../main/block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block"), [`Group`](../../../main/group/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools._api.group.Group")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/fileio/datafile/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.read_as_group.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.read\_as\_group[](#keysight-pwdatatools-datafile-read-as-group "Link to this heading")

DataFile.read\_as\_group(*loc: str = '/'*, *\**, *data: bool = True*, *format\_specific: FormatSpecificReadOptions | None = None*, *idxs: IdxsOption | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*, *varnames: Mapping[str, str] | None = None*) → [Group](../../../main/group/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.read_as_group "Link to this definition")
:   Read the DataFile and return a new instance of Group.

    If the DataFile file has a missing or unknown file extension, you
    must specify the `DataFile.format_override`. Known readable file
    extensions are stored in `options.reading.extentions`. Known
    readable file formats are stored in
    `options.reading.formats`.

    Parameters:
    :   * **loc** – The location within the source file to return as a
          [`Group`](../../../main/group/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group"). If `'/'` (the “root”), the entire DataFile
          is read. Otherwise, it represents some portion of the file.
          This may be the name of a single datablock (for example in an
          MDIF or ADS dataset). Or, it may be the full HDF5 path in a
          .pwdt file. Defaults to `'/'`, which results in the full
          file contents returned as a [`Group`](../../../main/group/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group").
        * **dtypes** – Datatypes used for numpy ndarrays. Defaults to `None`, which
          results in automatic determination of the dtypes.
        * **data** – A bool that determines whether to read the data. If `False`,
          only the metadata is read. Reading only metadata can be useful
          for quickly examining the variable names and hierarchy.
          Defaults to `True`.
        * **format\_specific** – Defines the format-specific reading options. By default, the
          global option `options.reading.format_specific` sets
          all format-specific reading options, but this parameter can
          override the global options. If provided, it must be an
          instance of `FormatSpecificReadOptions`. See the docs
          for a list of all classes that can act as format-specific read
          options. Some examples are `ADSReadOptions`,
          `LoadPullReadOptions`, and `LoadPullDerivedVars`.
        * **idxs** (*{‘auto’, True, False}, optional*) – If `True`, create index variables (idxs) when reading the
          source file. If `False`, do not create them. If `'auto'`,
          use the format-specific defaults. If `None`, use the global
          option `options.reading.idxs`.
        * **iffs** – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing. Defaults to `False`.
        * **varnames** – A mapping of variable roles to their names. Defaults to
          `options.reading.varnames`.

    Return type:
    :   [*Group*](../../../main/group/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group")

    Examples

    The below examples assume the following import:

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    ```

    If the DataFile has a known file extension, and you want to read
    the entire file, you can simply call `read_as_group` without any
    arguments. Known file extensions are stored in
    `pwdatatools.options.reading.extentions`.

    ```
    >>> # Using a path with known ext (.ds)
    >>> datafile = pwdt.DataFile('/home/s_param.ds')
    >>> group = datafile.read_as_group()
    ```

    If the file has a missing or unknown file extension, specify the
    DataFile’s `format_override` parameter.

    ```
    >>> datafile = pwdt.DataFile('/noise.txt', format_override='mdif')
    >>> group = datafile.read_as_group()
    ```

    If you want to perform a partial read, use the `loc` parameter. You
    have to know the name of the part that you want to read from `src`.
    A Group will always be returned, even if the contents could be
    contained within a single Block (the Block is placed into a Group and
    the Group is returned).

    ```
    >>> # partial read of ADS dataset returns Group containing one Block
    >>> datafile = pwdt.DataFile('/home/s_param.ds')
    >>> group = datafile.read_as_group(loc='/SP1.SP')
    ```

    See also

    `from_file()`, [`Group`](../../../main/group/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools._api.group.Group"), [`Block`](../../../main/block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/fileio/datafile/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.read_as_loadpullblock.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.read\_as\_loadpullblock[](#keysight-pwdatatools-datafile-read-as-loadpullblock "Link to this heading")

DataFile.read\_as\_loadpullblock(*\*\*kwargs*) → [LoadPullBlock](../../../loadpull/loadpullblock/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.read_as_loadpullblock "Link to this definition")
:   Read the DataFile and return a LoadPullBlock.

    The Datafile must have a known file extension that is associated
    with Keysight, Focus, or Maury load pull files. Known readable load
    pull file extensions are stored in
    `options.reading.format_to_extension_map['loadpull']`.

    Parameters:
    :   * **format\_specific** (*FormatSpecificReadOptions*) – Defines the format-specific reading options. By default, the
          global option `options.reading.format_specific.loadpull`
          sets format-specific loadpull reading options, but this
          parameter can override the global options. If provided, it
          must be an instance of `FormatSpecificReadOptions`. See
          the docs for a list of all classes that can act as format-
          specific read options. Some examples are
          `LoadPullReadOptions`, and `LoadPullDerivedVars`.
        * **varnames** (*Mapping[RoleLike, str]*) – A mapping of variable roles to their names. Defaults to
          `options.reading.varnames`.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](../../../loadpull/loadpullblock/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Examples

    Using all the default options:

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> datafile = pwdt.DataFile("/data/loadpull.cst")
    >>> lpblock = datafile.read_as_loadpullblock()
    ```

    Modifying global options to control how variables are named and
    which variables are derived from a and b waves:

    ```
    >>> pwdt.options.reading.varnames["power.delivered.load"] = "Pout"
    >>> pwdt.options.reading.format_specific.loadpull.derived_vars.ampm = False
    >>> lpblock = datafile.read_as_loadpullblock() # uses global options
    ```

    Passing in a dict of roles to varnames and an instance of
    `LoadPullDerivedVars` to override the global settings:

    ```
    >>> lpblock = datafile.read_as_loadpullblock(
    ...     "/data/loadpull.cst",
    ...     varnames={'gamma.load': 'GL'},
    ...     format_specific=pwdt.LoadPullDerivedVars(ampm=False),
    ... )
    ```

    Notes

    The indexing in the pandas DataFrame always uses `'ivars-idxs'`
    as the rows index and `'flat'` column index. The global
    indexing options are ignored.

    See also

    [`LoadPullBlock`](../../../loadpull/loadpullblock/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock"), [`LoadPullBlock.from_file()`](../../../loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_file.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_file "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_file")

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/fileio/datafile/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.translate.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.translate[](#keysight-pwdatatools-datafile-translate "Link to this heading")

DataFile.translate(*dst: str | PathLike | [DataFile](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools._api.datafile.DataFile")*, *dst\_mode: Literal['x', 'w', 'a'] = 'x'*, *\**, *dst\_format: str | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.translate "Link to this definition")
:   Translate a DataFile to another file format.

    The destination format can be set explicitly using an optional
    keyword argument. However, if the `dst` has a recognized
    file extension, its format can be automatically inferred and
    the keyword argument isn’t needed.

    Parameters:
    :   * **dst** (*str, os.PathLike, DataFile*) – The destination file path. If `dst_format=None`,
          attempts are made to ascertain the format by examining the
          destination file’s extension. Known file extensions are
          stored in `options.writing.extentions`.
        * **dst\_mode** (*{‘x’, ‘w’, ‘a’}, default ‘x’*) – The mode to use when opening `dst`. If `dst` does not yet
          exist, all three modes yield the same behavior. However, the 3
          modes have differing behaviors if attempting to write to a
          pre-existing file. `'w'` overwrites the existing file.
          `'a'` appends to the existing file. `'x'` results in a
          FileExistsError.
        * **dst\_format** (*str, optional*) – Sets the destination file’s format. Supported types are stored
          in `options.writing.formats`. Any value other than
          `None` overrides the format inferred from or set by `dst`.
        * **iffs** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing.

    Notes

    This method always uses the global format-specific read and
    write options. The only way to override the global options is to
    perform translation as a two-step process (read then write).

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Examples

    The below examples assume the following imports:

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import DataFile
    >>> from pathlib import Path
    ```

    If the datafile’s [`get_format()`](keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.get_format.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.get_format "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.get_format") is able to be determined and
    the destination file `dst` has a known file extension, simply
    input `dst` and nothing else.

    ```
    >>> src = DataFile('/home/s_param.s4p')
    >>> src.translate('/home/s_param.ds')  # known extensions (.s4p and .ds)
    >>> src.translate(Path('/home/noise.mdf')) # known extensions (.s4p and .mdf)
    ```

    If the the destination file `dst` has a missing or unknown file
    extension, use the `dst_format` parameter or a DataFile with
    `format_override` set.

    ```
    >>> src = DataFile('/home/s_param.s4p')
    >>> # using dst_format:
    >>> src.translate('/home/s_param', dst_format='mdif')
    >>> using a DataFile as dst:
    >>> dst = DataFile('/home/noise', format_override='mdif')
    >>> src.translate(dst)
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` already exists, a FileExistsError is
    raised if `dst_mode='x'`, which is the default. A `dst_mode`
    parameter value of `'w'` will overwrite the entire existing file,
    while a value of `'a'` will append to the existing file.

    Note

    Only certain types of datafiles work for `dst_mode='a'`. A
    ValueError is raised if append mode is not supported for the format
    of `dst`.

    ```
    >>> src = DataFile('/home/s_param.s4p')
    >>> src.translate('/s_param.ds', dst_mode='w')  # overwrite dst, if it exists
    >>> src.translate('/s_param.mdf', dst_mode='a') # append to dst, if it exists
    ```

    See also

    [`translate_file()`](../../translate_file.md#keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.translate_file "keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.translate_file"), [`read_file()`](../../read_file.md#keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.read_file "keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.read_file"), [`write_file()`](../../write_file.md#keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.write_file "keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.write_file")

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/fileio/datafile/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.tree.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.tree[](#keysight-pwdatatools-datafile-tree "Link to this heading")

DataFile.tree() → str[](#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.tree "Link to this definition")
:   Return a tree view of this DataFile, similar to a directory tree.

    In the tree, each Group is represented as a “folder” and each
    Block is represented as a “file”. The tree is returned as a string
    with newlines separating each level of the tree. The tree is
    indented to show the hierarchy. The string is meant to be printed
    to the console or shown in a GUI, not for programmatic use.

    Returns:
    :   Representation of the DataFile as a tree.

    Return type:
    :   *str*

    Notes

    This method scans the datafile to build the tree. Whenever possible,
    it does this without reading the data. The speed of the scan depends
    on the file format and the size of the file. For binary file formats,
    the scan can be very fast, even for large files. For text-based file
    formats, the scan can be slower.

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> datafile = pwdt.DataFile('/home/s_param_and_HB.ds')
    >>> print(datafile.tree())
    <[/] Group 's_param_and_HB'>
    ├── <[0] Block 'Sweep2.Sweep1.SP1.SP' with 5 Vars>
    ├── <[1] Block 'HB1.HB' with 4 Vars>
    └── <[2] Block 'aele_0.HB1.HB' with 2 Vars>
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/fileio/datafile/index.md === -->

# DataFile[](#datafile "Link to this heading")

This page gives an overview of public attributes and methods available for the DataFile class.

*class* DataFile(*path\_or\_obj: str | PathLike | BaseDataFile*, *format\_override: str | None = None*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "Link to this definition")
:   An object that represents a datafile on a filesystem.

    The datafile may or may not exist.

    Parameters:
    :   * **path\_or\_obj** – A str, path object, or file object that points to a file
          location. The file may or may not exist.
        * **format\_override** – Optionally override the format that would be inferred by the
          file extension. Also allows to assign a format to files with
          missing file extensions. If `None`, the format is
          determined by the file extension on `path_or_obj`. Defaults
          to `None`.

    Examples

    The below examples assume the following imports:

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import DataFile
    >>> from pathlib import Path
    ```

    You can create a DataFile by passing in a str path, a
    `pathlib.Path`, or another DataFile object.

    ```
    >>> # using a str path
    >>> datafile = DataFile('/home/s_param.s4p')
    >>> # using a pathlib.Path
    >>> datafile = DataFile(Path('/home/s_param.s4p'))
    >>> # using another DataFile
    >>> datafile2 = DataFile(datafile)
    ```

    If the file has a known file extension, the format is inferred
    automatically.

    ```
    >>> datafile = DataFile('/home/s_param.s4p')
    >>> datafile.get_format()
    'touchstone'
    ```

    If the file has a missing or unknown file extension, you can
    specify the `format_override` parameter.

    ```
    >>> datafile = DataFile('/home/noise.txt', format_override='mdif')
    >>> datafile.get_format()
    'mdif'
    ```

    Raises:
    :   * **ValueError** – If `path_or_obj` is a directory.
          If invalid `format_override` value.
        * **TypeError** – If `path_or_obj` is wrong type.

**Attributes**

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| [`DataFile.folder`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.folder.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.folder "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.folder") | Full path to folder containing DataFile. |
| [`DataFile.format_override`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.format_override.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.format_override "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.format_override") | Optional str format which takes precedence over the file extension. |
| [`DataFile.ext`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.ext.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.ext "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.ext") | The file extension (an empty str if missing extension). |
| [`DataFile.name`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.name.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.name "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.name") | The file's stem plus extension, if present. |
| [`DataFile.path`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.path.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.path "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.path") | The full path to the file. |
| [`DataFile.stem`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.stem.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.stem "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.stem") | Base filename (filename without extension). |
| [`DataFile.suffix`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.suffix.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.suffix "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.suffix") | File extension (alias for ext property). |

| [`DataFile.__repr__`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.__repr__.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.__repr__ "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.__repr__")() | Return evaluateable str representation of instance. |

| [`DataFile.is_s2pmdif`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_s2pmdif.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_s2pmdif "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_s2pmdif")() | Return bool indicating whether file is an S2PMDIF file. |
| [`DataFile.is_same`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_same.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_same "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_same")(other\_datafile) | Return True if other datafile has same contents as instance. |
| [`DataFile.is_smatrixio`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_smatrixio.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_smatrixio "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_smatrixio")() | Return bool indicating whether file is a SMatrixIO file. |
| [`DataFile.is_touchstone`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_touchstone.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_touchstone "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile.is_touchstone")() | Return bool indicating whether is a Touchstone S-parameter file. |

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/fileio/defaults.md === -->

# File IO Options Defaults[](#file-io-options-defaults "Link to this heading")

ADS\_READ\_OPTIONS\_DEFAULT[](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_ads.ADS_READ_OPTIONS_DEFAULT "Link to this definition")
:   Default options for reading ADS datasets.

    It is an instance of [`ADSReadOptions`](options.md#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_ads.ADSReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_ads.ADSReadOptions") with the following property
    values:

    * `engine_pref`: `None`

CITI\_READ\_OPTIONS\_DEFAULT[](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_citi.CITI_READ_OPTIONS_DEFAULT "Link to this definition")
:   Default options for reading CITI files.

    It is an instance of [`CITIReadOptions`](options.md#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_citi.CITIReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_citi.CITIReadOptions") with the following property
    values:

    * `engine_pref`: `None`

CSV\_READ\_OPTIONS\_DEFAULT[](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_csv.CSV_READ_OPTIONS_DEFAULT "Link to this definition")
:   Default options for reading CSV.

    It is an instance of [`CSVReadOptions`](options.md#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_csv.CSVReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_csv.CSVReadOptions") with the following property
    values:

    * `engine_pref`: `None`
    * `pandas_kwargs`: `None`

LOADPULL\_READ\_OPTIONS\_DEFAULT[](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_loadpull.LOADPULL_READ_OPTIONS_DEFAULT "Link to this definition")
:   Default options for reading load pull files.

    It is an instance of [`LoadPullReadOptions`](options.md#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_loadpull.LoadPullReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_loadpull.LoadPullReadOptions") with the following property
    values:

    * `derived_vars`: [`LOADPULL_DERIVED_VARS_DEFAULT`](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_loadpull.LOADPULL_DERIVED_VARS_DEFAULT "keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_loadpull.LOADPULL_DERIVED_VARS_DEFAULT")
    * `always_freq_suffixed`: `ALWAYS_FREQ_SUFFIXED_DEFAULT`
    * `power_ivar_pref`: `None`
    * `uniform_ivars`: `False`

LOADPULL\_DERIVED\_VARS\_DEFAULT[](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_loadpull.LOADPULL_DERIVED_VARS_DEFAULT "Link to this definition")
:   Default loadpull derived variables.

    It is an instance of [`LoadPullDerivedVars`](options.md#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_loadpull.LoadPullDerivedVars "keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_loadpull.LoadPullDerivedVars") with the following
    properties:

    * `main`: `ALL_DERIVED_VARS_ROLES`
    * `freq_enums`: `DERIVED_VARS_FREQ_ENUMS_DEFAULT`
    * `power_units`: `'dBm'`

MDM\_READ\_OPTIONS\_DEFAULT[](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_mdm.MDM_READ_OPTIONS_DEFAULT "Link to this definition")
:   Default options for reading MDM.

    It is an instance of [`MDMReadOptions`](options.md#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_mdm.MDMReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_mdm.MDMReadOptions") with the following property
    values:

    * `iccap_values_as_vars`: False

TOUCHSTONE\_READ\_OPTIONS\_DEFAULT[](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_touchstone.TOUCHSTONE_READ_OPTIONS_DEFAULT "Link to this definition")
:   Default options for reading Touchstone.

    It is an instance of `TouchStoneReadOptions` with the following property
    values:

    * `engine_pref`: `None`
    * `network_blockname`: `"Network"`
    * `noise_blockname`: `"Noise"`

ADS\_WRITE\_INVALID\_DEFAULT[](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_ads.ADS_WRITE_INVALID_DEFAULT "Link to this definition")
:   Default options for writing invalid data to ADS datasets.

    It is an instance of [`ADSWriteInvalid`](options.md#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_ads.ADSWriteInvalid "keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_ads.ADSWriteInvalid") with the following property
    values:

    * boolean = `"float_w_nan"`
    * complexfloating = `"complex_w_nan"`
    * floating = `"float_w_nan"`
    * integer = `"float_w_nan"`
    * string = `"str_w_null"`
    * nan\_rep = `numpy.nan`
    * integer\_null\_rep = `99999`
    * string\_null\_rep = `"null"`

MDIF\_WRITE\_INVALID\_DEFAULT[](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_mdif.MDIF_WRITE_INVALID_DEFAULT "Link to this definition")
:   Default options for writing invalid data to MDIFs.

    It is an instance of [`MDIFWriteInvalid`](options.md#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_mdif.MDIFWriteInvalid "keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_mdif.MDIFWriteInvalid") with the following property
    values:

    * boolean = `"float_w_nan"`
    * complexfloating = `"complex_w_nan"`
    * floating = `"float_w_nan"`
    * integer = `"float_w_nan"`
    * string = `"str_w_null"`
    * nan\_rep = `"NaN"`
    * integer\_null\_rep = `99999`
    * string\_null\_rep = `"null"`

## Format-Specific Write Options[](#format-specific-write-options "Link to this heading")

*class* ADSWriteInvalid(*boolean: Literal['float\_w\_nan', 'int\_w\_null'] = 'float\_w\_nan'*, *complexfloating: Literal['complex\_w\_nan'] = 'complex\_w\_nan'*, *floating: Literal['float\_w\_nan'] = 'float\_w\_nan'*, *integer: Literal['float\_w\_nan', 'int\_w\_null'] = 'float\_w\_nan'*, *string: Literal['str\_w\_null'] = 'str\_w\_null'*, *nan\_rep: float = nan*, *integer\_null\_rep: int = 99999*, *string\_null\_rep: str = 'null'*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_ads.ADSWriteInvalid "Link to this definition")
:   Immutable options for writing invalid datapoint(s) to ADS datasets.

    Each of the 5 generic datatypes has one or more available options for
    writing invalid data to ADS datasets. Additionally, there are options
    for NaN and null representations. NaN values only apply to floating
    and complexfloating datatypes. Note that the current implementation
    does not allow for separate definition of NaN for floating and
    complexfloating.

    get\_formats() → Tuple[str, ...][](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_ads.ADSWriteInvalid.get_formats "Link to this definition")
    :   Return all supported formats.

    mapping() → Dict[str, Any][](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_ads.ADSWriteInvalid.mapping "Link to this definition")
    :   Return dictionary mapping of the options.

    replace(*\*\*kwargs*) → [ADSWriteInvalid](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_ads.ADSWriteInvalid "keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_ads.ADSWriteInvalid")[](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_ads.ADSWriteInvalid.replace "Link to this definition")
    :   Replace property value(s).

        Since all instances of ADSWriteInvalid are immutable, this returns a
        new instance with all property values copied from self, except any
        new value(s) specified as kwarg(s).

        Parameters:
        :   * **boolean** (*{“float\_w\_nan”, “int\_w\_null”}*) – Defaults to `self.boolean`.
            * **complexfloating** (*{“complex\_w\_nan”}*) – Defaults to `self.complexfloating`.
            * **floating** (*{“float\_w\_nan”}*) – Defaults to `self.floating`.
            * **integer** (*{“float\_w\_nan”, “int\_w\_null”}*) – Defaults to `self.integer`.
            * **string** (*{“str\_w\_null”}*) – Defaults to `self.string`.
            * **nan\_rep** (*float*) – Defaults to `self.nan_rep`.
            * **integer\_null\_rep** (*int*) – Defaults to `self.integer_null_rep`.
            * **string\_null\_rep** (*str*) – Defaults to `self.string_null_rep`.

*class* CSVWriteOptions(*\**, *engine\_pref: Literal['pandas'] | None = None*, *pandas\_kwargs: dict[str, Any] | None = None*, *multidim\_pref: Literal['dims', 'default\_ints'] = 'dims'*, *default\_ints: Literal[1, 0] = 1*, *cols\_delim: Tuple[str, str, str] = ('[', ',', ']')*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_csv.CSVWriteOptions "Link to this definition")
:   A class to store immutable options for writing to CSV files.

    *property* cols\_delim*: Tuple[str, str, str]*[](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_csv.CSVWriteOptions.cols_delim "Link to this definition")
    :   Return the cols\_delim attribute.

    *property* default\_ints*: Literal[1, 0]*[](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_csv.CSVWriteOptions.default_ints "Link to this definition")
    :   Return the default\_ints attribute.

    *property* engine\_pref*: Literal['pandas'] | None*[](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_csv.CSVWriteOptions.engine_pref "Link to this definition")
    :   Preference for engine used to write CSV datasets.

    get\_formats() → Tuple[str, ...][](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_csv.CSVWriteOptions.get_formats "Link to this definition")
    :   Return format name.

    mapping() → Dict[str, Any][](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_csv.CSVWriteOptions.mapping "Link to this definition")
    :   Return a dictionary mapping of the options.

    *property* multidim\_pref*: Literal['dims', 'default\_ints']*[](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_csv.CSVWriteOptions.multidim_pref "Link to this definition")
    :   Return the multidim\_pref attribute.

    *property* pandas\_kwargs*: Dict[str, Any]*[](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_csv.CSVWriteOptions.pandas_kwargs "Link to this definition")
    :   Keyword arguments to pass to the pandas writer.

    replace(*\*\*kwargs*) → [CSVWriteOptions](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_csv.CSVWriteOptions "keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_csv.CSVWriteOptions")[](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_csv.CSVWriteOptions.replace "Link to this definition")
    :   Return a new instance with the specified properties replaced.

*class* MDIFWriteInvalid(*boolean: Literal['float\_w\_nan', 'int\_w\_null'] = 'float\_w\_nan'*, *complexfloating: Literal['complex\_w\_nan'] = 'complex\_w\_nan'*, *floating: Literal['float\_w\_nan'] = 'float\_w\_nan'*, *integer: Literal['float\_w\_nan', 'int\_w\_null'] = 'float\_w\_nan'*, *string: Literal['str\_w\_null'] = 'str\_w\_null'*, *nan\_rep: float | str = 'NaN'*, *integer\_null\_rep: int = 99999*, *string\_null\_rep: str = 'null'*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_mdif.MDIFWriteInvalid "Link to this definition")
:   Immutable options for writing variables with invalid datapoint(s) to MDIF.

    Each of the 5 generic datatypes has one or more available options for
    writing invalid data to MDIFs. Additionally, there are options for
    NaN and null representation in MDIF files. NaN values only apply to
    floating and complexfloating datatypes.

    get\_formats() → Tuple[str, ...][](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_mdif.MDIFWriteInvalid.get_formats "Link to this definition")
    :   Return supported writeable formats.

    mapping() → Dict[str, Any][](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_mdif.MDIFWriteInvalid.mapping "Link to this definition")
    :   Return a dictionary mapping of the options.

    replace(*\*\*kwargs*) → [MDIFWriteInvalid](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_mdif.MDIFWriteInvalid "keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_mdif.MDIFWriteInvalid")[](#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.writing.options_mdif.MDIFWriteInvalid.replace "Link to this definition")
    :   Replace property value(s).

        Since all instances of MDIFWriteInvalid are immutable, this returns a
        new instance with all property values copied from self, except any
        new value(s) specified as kwarg(s).

        Parameters:
        :   * **boolean** (*{“float\_w\_nan”, “int\_w\_null”}*) – Defaults to `self.boolean`.
            * **complexfloating** (*{“complex\_w\_nan”}*) – Defaults to `self.complexfloating`.
            * **floating** (*{“float\_w\_nan”}*) – Defaults to `self.floating`.
            * **integer** (*{“float\_w\_nan”, “int\_w\_null”}*) – Defaults to `self.integer`.
            * **string** (*{“str\_w\_null”}*) – Defaults to `self.string`.
            * **nan\_rep** (*float or str*) – Defaults to `self.nan_rep`.
            * **integer\_null\_rep** (*int*) – Defaults to `self.integer_null_rep`.
            * **string\_null\_rep** (*str*) – Defaults to `self.string_null_rep`.

# read\_file[](#read-file "Link to this heading")

read\_file(*src: str | PathLike | [DataFile](datafile/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools._api.datafile.DataFile")*, *loc: Literal['/'] = '/'*, *\**, *data: bool = True*, *format\_specific: FormatSpecificReadOptions | None = None*, *idxs: IdxsOption | None = None*, *idxs\_tol: float | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*, *src\_format: str | None = None*, *varnames: Mapping[str, str] | None = None*) → [Group](../main/group/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools._api.group.Group")[](#keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.read_file "Link to this definition")

read\_file(*src: str | PathLike | [DataFile](datafile/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools._api.datafile.DataFile")*, *loc: str*, *\**, *data: bool = True*, *format\_specific: FormatSpecificReadOptions | None = None*, *idxs: IdxsOption | None = None*, *idxs\_tol: float | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*, *src\_format: str | None = None*, *varnames: Mapping[str, str] | None = None*) → [Group](../main/group/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools._api.group.Group") | [Block](../main/block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block")
:   Read a datafile and return a Group or Block.

    Known readable file extensions are stored in `options.reading.extentions`.
    Known readable file formats are stored in `options.reading.formats`.

    Parameters:
    :   * **src** (*str, os.PathLike, or DataFile*) – The source file path.
        * **loc** (*str, default ‘/’*) – The location within the source file to return as a Group or Block.
          If `'/'` (the “root”), the entire source file is read and
          returned as a Group. Otherwise, it represents some portion of the
          source file. This may be the name of a single Block (for example,
          in an MDIF or ADS dataset), or the full path to a Group or Block
          (e.g. `'/G1/X'`, where ‘G1’ is the name of a Group and ‘X’ is
          the name of the desired Group or Block).
        * **data** (*bool, default True*) – A bool that determines whether to read the data from `src`.
          If `False`, only the metadata is read. Reading only metadata
          can be useful for quickly examining information about the
          variables.
        * **format\_specific** (*FormatSpecificReadOptions, optional*) – Defines the format-specific options for reading `src`. By
          default, the global option
          `options.reading.format_specific` sets format-specific
          reading options, but this parameter can override the global
          options. If provided, it must be an instance of
          `FormatSpecificReadOptions`. See the docs for a list of
          all classes that can act as format-specific read options. Some
          examples are `ADSReadOptions`,
          `LoadPullReadOptions`, and `LoadPullDerivedVars`.
        * **idxs** (*{‘auto’, True, False}, optional*) – If `True`, create index variables (idxs) when reading the
          source file. If `False`, do not create them. If `'auto'`,
          use the format-specific defaults. If `None`, use the global
          option `options.reading.idxs`.
        * **idxs\_tol** (*float, optional*) – The tolerance to use when generating index variables from float
          or complex data. If NaN, no tolerance is used and indexes are
          created by using exact equality. If provided, it takes precedence
          over the global option `options.reading.idxs_tol`.
        * **iffs** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing.
        * **src\_format** (*str, optional*) – Sets the source file’s format. Supported types are stored in
          `options.reading.formats`. Any value other
          than `None` overrides the format inferred from or set by
          `src`.
        * **varnames** (*Mapping[str, str], optional*) – A mapping of variable roles to their names. Defaults to
          `options.reading.varnames`.

    Returns:
    :   A `Group` or `Block` containing the contents of
        `src`. If `loc` is `'/'`, a `Group` is always
        returned. Otherwise, a `Block` is returned if the contents
        of `loc` can be represented by a single `Block`. If the
        contents of `loc` cannot be represented by a single
        `Block`, a `Group` is returned.

    Return type:
    :   *Union*[[*Group*](../main/group/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group"), [*Block*](../main/block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")]

    Examples

    The below examples assume the following imports:

    ```
    >>> from pathlib import Path
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    ```

    If the source file `src` has a known file extension, and you want
    to read the entire file, you can simply input `src` as a str,
    `pathlib.Path`, or `DataFile`.
    Known file extensions are stored in
    `pwdatatools.options.reading.extentions`.

    ```
    >>> # Using a str path with known ext (.ds)
    >>> g1 = pwdt.read_file('/home/s_param.ds')
    >>> # Using a pathlib.Path with known ext (.mdf)
    >>> g2 = pwdt.read_file(Path('/home/noise_param.mdf'))
    >>> # Using a DataFile with known ext (.mdm)
    >>> b3 = pwdt.read_file(pwdt.DataFile('/home/FNoise.mdm'))
    ```

    If `src` has a missing or unknown file extension, you can use the
    `src_format` parameter. Alternatively, you can input an instance of
    `DataFile` as the `src`
    argument and use DataFile’s `format_override` parameter to define
    the source’s format.

    ```
    >>> # Using src_format parameter since src has missing ext
    >>> g1 = pwdt.read_file('/data/noise', src_format='mdif')
    >>> # Using src_format parameter since src has non-standard ext
    >>> g2 = pwdt.read_file('/data/noise.txt', src_format='mdif')
    >>> # Using DataFile with format_override
    >>> b1 = pwdt.read_file(pwdt.DataFile('/data/noise.txt', format_override='mdif'))
    ```

    If you want to perform a partial read, use the `loc` parameter. You
    have to know the name of the part that you want to read from `src`.
    A Group or Block may be returned, depending on the contents of the
    part.

    ```
    >>> # partial read of ADS datasets always returns a Block
    >>> block1 = pwdt.read_file('/home/s_param.ds', loc='/SP1.SP')
    >>> # partial read of a .pwdt file may return Group or Block
    >>> group1 = pwdt.read_file('/home/ac_dc_meas.pwdt', loc='/Network')
    ```

    See also

    [`Group`](../main/group/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools._api.group.Group"), [`Block`](../main/block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block")

# read\_file\_as\_block[](#read-file-as-block "Link to this heading")

read\_file\_as\_block(*src: str | os.PathLike | [DataFile](datafile/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile")*, *loc: str = '/'*, *\**, *data: bool = True*, *format\_specific: FormatSpecificReadOptions | None = None*, *idxs: IdxsOption | None = None*, *idxs\_tol: float | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*, *src\_format: str | None = None*, *varnames: Mapping[str, str] | None = None*) → [Block](../main/block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")[](#keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.read_file_as_block "Link to this definition")
:   Read a datafile and return a new instance of Block.

    Known readable file extensions are stored in `options.reading.extentions`.
    Known readable file formats are stored in `options.reading.formats`.

    Parameters:
    :   * **src** (*str, os.PathLike, or DataFile*) – The source file path.
        * **loc,** (*str, default ‘/’*) – The location within the source file to return as a Block. If
          `'/'` (the “root”), the entire source file is read.
          Otherwise, it represents some portion of the source file. This
          may be the name of a single Block (for example, in an MDIF or
          ADS dataset), or the full path to a Group or Block (e.g.
          `'/G1/X'`, where ‘G1’ is the name of a Group and ‘X’ is the
          name of the desired Block).
        * **data** (*bool, default True*) – A bool that determines whether to read the data from `src`.
          If `False`, only the metadata is read. Reading only metadata
          can be useful for quickly examining information about the
          variables.
        * **format\_specific** (*FormatSpecificReadOptions, optional*) – Defines the format-specific options for reading `src`. By
          default, the global option
          `options.reading.format_specific` sets format-specific
          reading options, but this parameter can override the global
          options. If provided, it must be an instance of
          `FormatSpecificReadOptions`. See the docs for a list of
          all classes that can act as format-specific read options. Some
          examples are `ADSReadOptions`,
          `LoadPullReadOptions`, and `LoadPullDerivedVars`.
        * **idxs** (*{‘auto’, True, False}, optional*) – If `True`, create index variables (idxs) when reading the
          source file. If `False`, do not create them. If `'auto'`,
          use the format-specific defaults. If `None`, use the global
          option `options.reading.idxs`.
        * **idxs\_tol** (*float, optional*) – The tolerance to use when generating index variables from float
          or complex data. If NaN, no tolerance is used and indexes are
          created by using exact equality. If provided, it takes precedence
          over the global option `options.reading.idxs_tol`.
        * **iffs** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing.
        * **src\_format** (*str, optional*) – Sets the source file’s format. Supported types are stored in
          `options.reading.formats`. Any value other
          than `None` overrides the format inferred from or set by
          `src`.
        * **varnames** (*Mapping[str, str], optional*) – A mapping of variable roles to their names. Defaults to
          `options.reading.varnames`.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](../main/block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If data cannot be represented as a single Block.

    Examples

    The below examples assume the following imports:

    ```
    >>> from pathlib import Path
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    ```

    If the source file `src` has a known file extension, and you want
    to read the entire file, you can simply input `src` as a str,
    `pathlib.Path`, or `DataFile`.
    Known file extensions are stored in `options.reading.extentions`.

    ```
    >>> # Using a str path with known ext (.lpd)
    >>> b1 = pwdt.read_file_as_block('/home/device.lpd')
    >>> # Using a pathlib.Path with known ext (.spl)
    >>> b2 = pwdt.read_file_as_block(Path('/home/device.spl'))
    >>> # Using a DataFile with a known ext (.mdm)
    >>> b3 = pwdt.read_file_as_block(pwdt.DataFile('/home/FNoise.mdm'))
    ```

    If `src` has a missing or unknown file extension, you can use
    the `src_format` parameter. Alternatively, you can input an
    instance of `DataFile` as
    the `src` argument and use DataFile’s `format_override`
    parameter to define the source’s format.

    ```
    >>> # Using the ``src_format`` parameter since the file extension is missing
    >>> b1 = pwdt.read_file_as_block('/data/noise', src_format='mdif')
    >>> # Using the ``src_format`` parameter since the file extension is non-standard
    >>> b2 = pwdt.read_file_as_block('/data/noise.txt', src_format='mdif')
    >>> # Using a DataFile with ``format_override`` is another option
    >>> b3 = pwdt.read_file_as_block(pwdt.DataFile('/noise', format_override='mdif'))
    ```

    If you want to perform a partial read, use the `loc` parameter.
    You have to know the name of the part that you want to read from
    `src`. A Block is always returned. If the contents of the part
    cannot be represented by a single Block, a ValueError is raised.

    ```
    >>> b1 = pwdt.read_file_as_block('/home/s_param.ds', loc='/SP1.SP')
    >>> b2 = pwdt.read_file_as_block('/home/ac_dc_meas.pwdt', loc='/Network')
    ```

    See also

    `Block.from_file()`


---

# read\_file\_as\_group[](#read-file-as-group "Link to this heading")

read\_file\_as\_group(*src: str | os.PathLike | [DataFile](datafile/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile")*, *loc: str = '/'*, *\**, *data: bool = True*, *format\_specific: FormatSpecificReadOptions | None = None*, *idxs: IdxsOption | None = None*, *idxs\_tol: float | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*, *src\_format: str | None = None*, *varnames: Mapping[str, str] | None = None*) → [Group](../main/group/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group")[](#keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.read_file_as_group "Link to this definition")
:   Read a datafile and return a new instance of Group.

    Known readable file extensions are stored in `options.reading.extentions`.
    Known readable file formats are stored in `options.reading.formats`.

    Parameters:
    :   * **src** (*str, os.PathLike, or DataFile*) – The source file path.
        * **loc** (*str, default ‘/’*) – The location within the source file to return as a Group. If
          `'/'` (the “root”), the entire source file is read.
          Otherwise, it represents some portion of the source file. This
          may be the full path to a Group or Block (e.g. `'/parent/X'`,
          where ‘parent’ is the name of the parent Group and ‘X’ is the
          name of the desired Group or Block). If the contents of `loc`
          can be represented by a single Block, the Block is placed into a
          Group and the Group is returned.
        * **data** (*bool, default True*) – A bool that determines whether to read the data from `src`.
          If `False`, only the metadata is read. Reading only metadata
          can be useful for quickly examining information about the
          variables.
        * **format\_specific** (*FormatSpecificReadOptions, optional*) – Defines the format-specific options for reading `src`. By
          default, the global option
          `options.reading.format_specific` sets format-specific
          reading options, but this parameter can override the global
          options. If provided, it must be an instance of
          `FormatSpecificReadOptions`. See the docs for a list of
          all classes that can act as format-specific read options. Some
          examples are `ADSReadOptions`,
          `LoadPullReadOptions`, and `LoadPullDerivedVars`.
        * **idxs** (*{‘auto’, True, False}, optional*) – If `True`, create index variables (idxs) when reading the
          source file. If `False`, do not create them. If `'auto'`,
          use the format-specific defaults. If `None`, use the global
          option `options.reading.idxs`.
        * **idxs\_tol** (*float, optional*) – The tolerance to use when generating index variables from float
          or complex data. If NaN, no tolerance is used and indexes are
          created by using exact equality. If provided, it takes precedence
          over the global option `options.reading.idxs_tol`.
        * **iffs** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing.
        * **src\_format** (*str, optional*) – Sets the source file’s format. Supported types are stored in
          `options.reading.formats`. Any value other
          than `None` overrides the format inferred from or set by
          `src`.
        * **varnames** (*Mapping[str, str], optional*) – A mapping of variable roles to their names. Defaults to
          `options.reading.varnames`.

    Return type:
    :   [*Group*](../main/group/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group")

    Examples

    The below examples assume the following imports:

    ```
    >>> from pathlib import Path
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    ```

    If the source file `src` has a known file extension, and you want
    to read the entire file, you can simply input `src` as a str,
    `pathlib.Path`, or `DataFile`.
    Known file extensions are stored in
    `pwdatatools.options.reading.extentions`.

    ```
    >>> # Using a str path with known ext (.ds)
    >>> g1 = pwdt.read_file_as_group('/home/s_param.ds')
    >>> # Using pathlib.Path with known ext (.mdf)
    >>> g2 = pwdt.read_file_as_group(Path('/home/noise_param.mdf'))
    >>> # Using DataFile with a known ext (.mdm)
    >>> g3 = pwdt.read_file_as_group(pwdt.DataFile('/home/FNoise.mdm'))
    ```

    If `src` has a missing or unknown file extension, you can use the
    `src_format` parameter. Alternatively, you can input an instance of
    `DataFile` as the `src`
    argument and use DataFile’s `format_override` parameter to define
    the source’s format.

    ```
    >>> # Using the `src_format` parameter since the file extension is missing
    >>> g1 = pwdt.read_file_as_group('/data/noise', src_format='mdif')
    >>> # Using the `src_format` parameter since the file extension is non-standard
    >>> g2 = pwdt.read_file_as_group('/data/noise.txt', src_format='mdif')
    >>> # Using DataFile's `format_override` parameter is another option
    >>> g3 = pwdt.read_file_as_group(
    ...     pwdt.DataFile('/noise.txt', format_override='mdif')
    ... )
    ```

    If you want to perform a partial read, use the `loc` parameter. You
    have to know the name of the part that you want to read from `src`.
    A Group will always be returned, even if the contents could be
    contained within a single Block (the Block is placed into a Group and
    the Group is returned).

    ```
    >>> # partial read of ADS dataset returns Group containing one Block
    >>> group1 = pwdt.read_file_as_group('/home/s_param.ds', loc='/SP1.SP')
    >>> # partial read of a .pwdt file
    >>> group2 = pwdt.read_file_as_group('/home/ac_dc_meas.pwdt', loc='/Network')
    ```

    See also

    `Group.from_file()`


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/fileio/read_file_as_loadpullblock.md === -->

# read\_file\_as\_loadpullblock[](#read-file-as-loadpullblock "Link to this heading")

read\_file\_as\_loadpullblock(*src: str | PathLike | [DataFile](datafile/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools._api.datafile.DataFile")*, *\**, *format\_specific: [LoadPullReadOptions](options.md#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_loadpull.LoadPullReadOptions "keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_loadpull.LoadPullReadOptions") | [LoadPullDerivedVars](options.md#keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_loadpull.LoadPullDerivedVars "keysight.pwdatatools._file_io.reading.options_loadpull.LoadPullDerivedVars") | None = None*, *varnames: Mapping[str, str] | None = None*) → [LoadPullBlock](../loadpull/loadpullblock/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.read_file_as_loadpullblock "Link to this definition")
:   Read a load pull datafile and return a LoadPullBlock.

    The source file must have a known file extension that is associated
    with Keysight, Focus, or Maury load pull files. Known readable load
    pull file extensions are stored in
    `options.reading.format_to_extension_map['loadpull']`.

    Parameters:
    :   * **src** (*str, os.PathLike, or DataFile*) – The source file path.
        * **format\_specific** (*FormatSpecificReadOptions, optional*) – Defines the format-specific options for reading `src`. By
          default, the global option
          `options.reading.format_specific` sets format-specific
          reading options, but this parameter can override the global
          options. If provided, it must be an instance of
          `FormatSpecificReadOptions`. See the docs for a list of
          all classes that can act as format-specific read options. Some
          examples are `LoadPullReadOptions` and
          `LoadPullDerivedVars`.
        * **varnames** (*mapping of str to str, optional*) – A mapping of variable roles to their names. Defaults to
          `options.reading.varnames`.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](../loadpull/loadpullblock/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Examples

    Using all the default options:
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.read\_file\_as\_loadpullblock(“/data/loadpull.cst”)

    Modifying global options to control how variables are named and
    which variables are derived from a and b waves:

    ```
    >>> pwdt.options.reading.varnames["power.delivered.load"] = "Pout"
    >>> pwdt.options.reading.format_specific.loadpull.derived_vars.ampm = False
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.read_file_as_loadpullblock("/data/loadpull.cst")
    ```

    Passing in a dict of roles to varnames and an instance of
    `LoadPullDerivedVars` to override the global settings:

    ```
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.read_file_as_loadpullblock(
    ...     "/data/loadpull.cst",
    ...     varnames={'gamma.load': 'GL'},
    ...     format_specific=pwdt.LoadPullDerivedVars(ampm=False),
    ... )
    ```

    See also

    `LoadPullBlock.from_file()`


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/fileio/translate_file.md === -->

# translate\_file[](#translate-file "Link to this heading")

translate\_file(*src: str | PathLike | [DataFile](datafile/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools._api.datafile.DataFile")*, *dst: str | PathLike | [DataFile](datafile/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools._api.datafile.DataFile")*, *dst\_mode: Literal['x', 'w', 'a'] = 'x'*, *\*\*kwargs*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.translate_file "Link to this definition")
:   Translate a datafile from one format to another.

    There are several supported source and destination formats. The
    formats can be set explicitly using optional keyword parameters
    `src_format` and `dst_format`. However, if `src` and `dst` have
    recognized file extensions, the formats can be automatically
    inferred and the keyword arguments do not need to be used.

    Parameters:
    :   * **src** (*str, os.PathLike, or DataFile*) – The source file path. If `src_format=None`, attempts are
          made to ascertain the format by examining the source file’s
          extension. Known file extensions are stored in
          `options.reading.extentions`.
        * **dst** (*str, os.PathLike, or DataFile*) – The destination file path. If `dst_format=None`, attempts are
          made to ascertain the format by examining the destination file’s
          extension. Known file extensions are stored in
          `options.writing.extentions`.
        * **dst\_mode** (*{‘x’, ‘w’, ‘a’}, default ‘x’*) – The mode to use when opening `dst`. If `dst` does not yet
          exist, all three modes yield the same behavior. However, the 3
          modes have differing behaviors if attempting to write to a pre-
          existing file. `'w'` overwrites the existing file. `'a'`
          appends to the existing file. `'x'` results in a
          FileExistsError.

    Other Parameters:
    :   * **src\_format** (*str, optional*) – Sets the source file’s format. Supported types are stored in
          `options.reading.formats`. Any value other than `None`
          overrides the format inferred from or set by `src`.
        * **dst\_format** (*str, optional*) – Sets the destination file’s format. Supported types are stored in
          `options.writing.formats`. Any value other than
          `None` overrides the format inferred from or set by `dst`.
        * **iffs** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Notes

    This function always uses the global format-specific read and write
    options. The only way to override the global options is to perform
    translation as a two-step process (read then write).

    Examples

    The below examples assume the following imports:

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import translate_file, DataFile
    >>> from pathlib import Path
    ```

    If the source file `src` and destination file `dst` both have
    known file extensions, simply input `src` and `dst` and nothing
    else.

    ```
    >>> # str src & dst with known exts (.s4p and .ds)
    >>> translate_file('/home/s_param.s4p', '/home/s_param.ds')
    >>> # pathlib.Path src & dst with known exts (.ds and .mdf)
    >>> translate_file(Path('/home/noise.ds'), Path('/home/noise.mdf'))
    >>> # str src & DataFile dst with known exts (.ds and .pwdt)
    >>> translate_file('/home/noise.ds', DataFile('/home/noise.pwdt'))
    ```

    If the the source file and/or destination file have missing or unknown
    file extensions, use the `src_format` and/or `dst_format` parameters.
    Alternatively, input an instance of
    `DataFile` as the `src` and/or
    `dst` arguments and use DataFile’s `format_override` parameter
    to define the format(s).

    ```
    >>> translate_file('/home/s_param', '/home/s_param.ds', src_format='touchstone')
    >>> translate_file('/noise', '/noise_new', src_format='ads', dst_format='mdif')
    >>> # Or, use DataFiles:
    >>> src = DataFile('/home/noise', format_override='mdif')
    >>> dst = DataFile('/home/noise_translated', format_override='pwdt')
    >>> translate_file(src, dst)
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` already exists, a FileExistsError is
    raised if `dst_mode='x'`, which is the default. A `dst_mode`
    parameter value of `'w'` will overwrite the entire existing file,
    while a value of `'a'` will append to the existing file.

    Note

    Only certain types of datafiles work for `dst_mode='a'`. A
    ValueError is raised if append mode is not supported for the format
    of `dst`.

    ```
    >>> # overwrite dst, if it exists
    >>> translate_file('/home/s_param.mdf', '/home/s_param.ds', dst_mode='w')
    >>> # append to dst, if it exists
    >>> translate_file('/home/s_param.ds', '/home/s_param.mdf', dst_mode='a')
    >>> # append to dst, if it exists
    >>> translate_file('/home/s_param.ds', '/home/s_param.pwdt', dst_mode='a')
    ```

    See also

    `DataFile.translate()`
    [`read_file()`](read_file.md#keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.read_file "keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.read_file")
    [`write_file()`](write_file.md#keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.write_file "keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.write_file")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/fileio/write_file.md === -->

# write\_file[](#write-file "Link to this heading")

write\_file(*group\_or\_block: [Group](../main/group/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools._api.group.Group") | [Block](../main/block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block")*, *dst: str | PathLike | [DataFile](datafile/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools._api.datafile.DataFile")*, *dst\_mode: Literal['x', 'w', 'a'] = 'x'*, *\**, *dst\_format: str | None = None*, *\*\*kwargs*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.write_file "Link to this definition")
:   Write a Group or Block to a file.

    Known writeable file extensions are stored in `options.writing.extentions`.
    Known writeable file formats are stored in `options.writing.formats`.

    Whether metadata is written depends on the format of output file.
    If the output file’s format has places to store metadata (such as
    in the native pwdatatools .pwdt format or HDF5 format), then metadata
    is automatically included in the output file.

    Parameters:
    :   * **group\_or\_block** (*Group or Block*) – An instance of `Group` or `Block` to write to a
          file.
        * **dst** (*str, os.PathLike, or DataFile*) – The destination file path. If it has a missing or unknown file
          extension, you must either 1) input a `DataFile` with a
          `format_override`, or 2) use the `dst_format` parameter.
        * **dst\_mode** (*{‘x’, ‘w’, ‘a’}, default ‘x’*) – The mode to use when opening `dst`. If `dst` does not yet
          exist, all three modes yield the same behavior. However, the 3
          modes have differing behaviors if attempting to write to a pre-
          existing file. `'w'` overwrites the existing file. `'a'`
          appends to the existing file. `'x'` results in a
          FileExistsError.
        * **dst\_format** (*str, optional*) – Sets the destination file’s format. Supported types are stored in
          `options.writing.formats`. Any value other than
          `None` overrides the format inferred from or set by `dst`.

    Other Parameters:
    :   * **format\_specific** (*FormatSpecificWriteOptions, optional*) – Defines the format-specific options for writing `dst`. By
          default, the global option
          `options.writing.format_specific` sets format-specific
          writing options, but this parameter overrides the global
          options. If provided, it must be an instance of
          `FormatSpecificWriteOptions`. See the docs for a list
          of all classes that are format-specific write options. Some
          examples are `ADSWriteOptions`,
          `MDIFWriteOptions`, `ADSWriteInvalid`, and
          `MDIFWriteInvalid`.
        * **loc** (*str, default ‘/’*) – The write location within `dst`. Only applies to file
          formats that support multiple levels of hierarchy (for
          example, native and HDF5 formats). This parameter sets the
          write location within the file’s directory-like (or tree-like)
          structure. The “root” location is ‘/’.
        * **iffs** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Examples

    The below examples assume that `group` is an instance of
    [`Group`](../main/group/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools._api.group.Group"), `block` is an
    instance of [`Block`](../main/block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block"), and
    the following imports:

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import write_file, DataFile
    >>> from pathlib import Path
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` has a known file extension, you can
    simply input `dst` as a str, `pathlib.Path`, or
    `DataFile`. Known file
    extensions are stored in `options.writing.extentions`.

    ```
    >>> # str path with a known ext (.ds)
    >>> write_file(group, '/home/s_param.ds')
    >>> # pathlib.Path with a known ext (.mdf)
    >>> write_file(block, Path('/home/noise_param.mdf'))
    >>> # DataFile with a known ext (.pwdt)
    >>> write_file(group, DataFile('/home/noise_param.pwdt'))
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` has a missing or non-standard file
    extension, you can use the `dst_format` parameter. Alternatively,
    you can input an instance of
    `DataFile` as the `dst`
    argument and use the DataFile’s `format_override` parameter to
    define the destination’s format.

    ```
    >>> # no ext solved by dst_format
    >>> write_file(group, '/data/s_param', dst_format='mdif')
    >>> # non-standard ext solved by dst_format
    >>> write_file(block, '/data/noise_param.txt', dst_format='mdif')
    >>> # no ext solved by DataFile
    >>> write_file(group, DataFile('/data/noise_param', format_override='mdif'))
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` already exists, a FileExistsError is
    raised if `dst_mode='x'`, which is the default. A `dst_mode`
    parameter value of `'w'` will overwrite the entire existing file,
    while a value of `'a'` will append to the existing file.

    Note

    Only certain types of datafiles work for `dst_mode='a'`. A
    ValueError is raised if append mode is not supported for the format
    of `dst`.

    ```
    >>> # overwrite dst, if it exists
    >>> write_file(group, '/home/data/s_param.ds', dst_mode='w')
    >>> # append to dst, if it exists
    >>> write_file(block, '/home/data/noise_param.mdf', dst_mode='a')
    >>> # append to dst, if it exists
    >>> write_file(group, '/home/data/run1.pwdt', dst_mode='a')
    ```

    See also

    [`Group`](../main/group/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools._api.group.Group"), [`Block`](../main/block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block")
