<!-- === 来源: api_reference/global_options.md === -->

# Global Options[](#global-options "Link to this heading")

The `pwdatatools` library provides a set of global options that can
be used to control certain behaviors. These options are divided into
four categories: licensing, logging, reading, and writing. The options
can be accessed and modified through the
`keysight.pwdatatools.options` object.

Notes

In cases where you do not want to rely on the global options when
reading or writing files, you can always override the global options
by parameters in the specific functions and methods. Some of these
parameters can be provided as instances of immutable options classes.

Examples

```
>>> import keysight.pwdatatools as pwdt
```

Let’s check the current logging level. By default, the logging level is
set to ‘error’, which prints only errors:

```
>>> pwdt.options.logging.level
'error'
```

Let’s change logging level to ‘info’, which prints info, warnings, and
errors, but suppresses debug messages:

```
>>> pwdt.options.logging.level = 'info'
```

Let’s access the readable and writeable file extensions and formats:

```
>>> pwdt.options.reading.extensions
<returns list of readable extensions>
>>> pwdt.options.writing.formats
<returns list of writeable types>
>>> pwdt.options.reading.format_to_extension_map
<returns mapping of readable file extensions to formats>
```

Let’s set the variable name associated with a particular role:

```
>>> pwdt.options.reading.varnames['impedance.reference'] = 'Zref'
```

Let’s get the current settings for writing invalid datapoints to ADS datasets:

```
>>> ads_invalid = pwdt.options.writing.format_specific.ads.invalid
>>> ads_invalid
ADSWriteInvalidGlobal(
    boolean='float_w_nan',
    complexfloating='complex_w_nan',
    floating='float_w_nan',
    integer='float_w_nan',
    string='str_w_null',
    nan_rep=nan,
    integer_null_rep=99999,
    string_null_rep='null',
)
```

Let’s change settings for writing invalid datapoints to ADS datasets:

```
>>> # First, check for other boolean options
>>> ads_invalid.boolean_options
('float_w_nan', 'int_w_null')
```

There’s only one other boolean option, so let’s pick that one:

```
>>> ads_invalid.boolean = 'int_w_null'
```

Now, ADS datasets with bool variables with bad datapoints will be
converted to ints with a sentinel value. Let’s see what that sentinel
value will be:

```
>>> ads_invalid.integer_null_rep
99999
```

Let’s also change the default string used for bad datapoints:

```
>>> ads_invalid.string_null_rep = 'invalid'
```

Now, ADS datasets with string variables with bad datapoints will use
the string ‘invalid’ to mark bad points.

Let’s look at all of our changes:

```
>>> ads_invalid
ADSWriteInvalidGlobal(
    boolean='int_w_null',
    complexfloating='complex_w_nan',
    floating='float_w_nan',
    integer='float_w_nan',
    string='str_w_null',
    nan_rep=nan,
    integer_null_rep=99999,
    string_null_rep='invalid',
)
```

# keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.apply[](#keysight-pwdatatools-grid-apply "Link to this heading")

Grid.apply(*dataframe: DataFrame*, *grid\_varname: str*) → DataFrame[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.apply "Link to this definition")
:   Apply grid to DataFrame, returning new DataFrame.

    The input DataFrame must have an integer index (and not a
    MultiIndex). The output DataFrame will have an integer index with
    the same index name as the input (but not the same index values,
    unless the input and output DataFrames happen to have the same
    number of rows).

    Parameters:
    :   * **dataframe** (*DataFrame*) – DataFrame to be gridded.
        * **grid\_varname** (*str*) – Name of complex grid variable.

# keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.drop\_edges[](#keysight-pwdatatools-grid-drop-edges "Link to this heading")

Grid.drop\_edges(*circular: bool | Literal['auto']*) → [Grid](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Grid "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.gridding.Grid")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.drop_edges "Link to this definition")
:   Drop the outside edges of the Grid, returning a new Grid.

    Parameters:
    :   **circular** – Only applies to polar coord system. Either a bool indicating
        whether to treat the grid as a circle, or the literal ‘auto’,

        circle when dropping.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/grid/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.from_gridded_series.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.from\_gridded\_series[](#keysight-pwdatatools-grid-from-gridded-series "Link to this heading")

*classmethod* Grid.from\_gridded\_series(*gridded\_series: Series*) → [Grid](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Grid "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.gridding.Grid")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.from_gridded_series "Link to this definition")
:   Create a Grid instance from an already-gridded Series.

    The grid will be calculated from the extents of the gamma Series.
    The number of points in the grid will be determined by the number
    of unique values in the Series.

    Since the coord\_system is unknown, first ‘rect’ is attempted, then
    ‘polar’.

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If the gridded series does not meet the requirements for
        being considered gridded.


---

    If coord\_system is “rect’, always return False.

# Grid[](#grid "Link to this heading")

This page gives an overview of public attributes and methods available for the Grid class. The Grid class is used extensively in load pull applications. For more information and examples, see [Load Pull Examples](../../../examples/loadpull/index.md#load-pull-examples).

*class* Grid(*\**, *coord\_system: CoordSystem*, *extents: Extents2D*, *npointsx: int*, *npointsy: int*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Grid "Link to this definition")
:   Object to store 2D grid attributes and apply to data.

    Parameters:
    :   * **coord\_system** (*Literal[‘rect’, ‘polar’]*) – Coordinate system.
        * **extents** (*Extents2D*) – Object that holds the max and min values of x and y.
        * **npointsx** (*int*) – Number of x points.
        * **npointsy** (*int*) – Number of y points.

**Attributes**

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| [`Grid.coord_system`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.coord_system.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.coord_system "keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.coord_system") | Coordinate system, either 'rect' or 'polar'. |
| [`Grid.extents`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.extents.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.extents "keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.extents") | Extents of grid. |
| [`Grid.npointsx`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.npointsx.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.npointsx "keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.npointsx") | Number of x points. |
| [`Grid.npointsy`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.npointsy.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.npointsy "keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.npointsy") | Number of y points. |
| [`Grid.x_unique`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.x_unique.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.x_unique "keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.x_unique") | Unique x values in grid. |
| [`Grid.y_unique`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.y_unique.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.y_unique "keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.y_unique") | Unique y values in grid. |

**Instance Methods**

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| [`Grid.apply`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.apply.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.apply "keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.apply")(dataframe, grid\_varname) | Apply grid to DataFrame, returning new DataFrame. |
| [`Grid.drop_edges`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.drop_edges.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.drop_edges "keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.drop_edges")(circular) | Drop the outside edges of the Grid, returning a new Grid. |
| [`Grid.includes_pole`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.includes_pole.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.includes_pole "keysight.pwdatatools.Grid.includes_pole")() | Return bool indicating whether pole is included. |

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.__init__.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.\_\_init\_\_[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-init "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.\_\_init\_\_(*variables: Variables = None*, *\**, *name: str | None = None*, *gamma\_ivarname: str | None = None*, *gamma\_idxname: str | None = None*, *z\_ivarname: str | None = None*, *z\_idxname: str | None = None*, *power\_ivarname: str | None = None*, *power\_idxname: str | None = None*, *outer\_ivarnames: Iterable[str] | None = None*, *outer\_idxnames: Iterable[str] | None = None*, *exprs: ExprsDictLike | None = None*, *attrs: AttrsDictLike | None = None*, *copy: bool = False*, *\*\*kwargs*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.__init__ "Link to this definition")
:   Initialize an instance of LoadPullBlock.

    Parameters:
    :   * **variables** (*DataFrame, sequence of Var, str to VarLike mapping, or None*) – Several types of inputs are supported: a pandas DataFrame, a
          mapping of str variable name to ArrayLikes or Vars, or a sequence
          of Vars. Examples of ArrayLike objects are numpy ndarrays and
          MaskedArrays, Python lists, and pandas Series. If a mapping or
          sequence is provided, all data objects must have the same length
          for axis 0.
        * **name** (*str, optional*) – A str name for the LoadPullBlock. If not specified, the name is
          set to an empty string.
        * **gamma\_ivarname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the gamma independent variable. A LoadPullBlock
          must have either a gamma ivar or a z ivar, but not both.
        * **gamma\_idxname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the gamma indepedent variable’s index.
        * **z\_ivarname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the impedance independent variable. A LoadPullBlock
          must have either a gamma ivar or a z ivar, but not both.
        * **z\_idxname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the impedance indepedent variable’s index.
        * **power\_ivarname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the power independent variable.
        * **power\_idxname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the power indepedent variable’s index.
        * **outer\_ivarnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the outer independent variables. Outer ivars can be
          any arbitrary swept variables, such as frequency, bias, etc.
        * **outer\_idxnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the outer indepedent variable’s indexes.
        * **exprs** (*ExprsDictLike, optional*) – A mapping of str expression name to Expr objects. The Expr objects
          represent expressions that can be applied to variables or other
          expressions.
        * **attrs** (*dict-like, optional*) – A dict or dict-like object of arbitrary Block-level metadata. The
          keys must be strings and the values must be one of the following:
          str, int, float, complex, bool, None, dict, list, or
          numpy.ndarray. Nested dicts and lists are allowed.
        * **copy** (*bool, default False*) – If True, make a copy of the input variables. If False, use the
          input variables as-is, if possible.

    Other Parameters:
    :   * **data** (*DataFrame, str to ArrayLike mapping, or None*) – A keyword-only argument that exists mostly for backwards
          compatibility. It is recommended to use variables instead of this
          keyword-only argument. You cannot specify values for both data
          and variables.
        * **idxnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the indexes. This is another way to define the
          names of the gamma, z, power, and outer indexes. To use this
          argument, you must also specify ivarnames and variables
          input must contain Vars with the proper gamma/z/power roles.
          Defaults to `None`.
        * **ivarnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the independent variables. This is another way
          to define the names of the gamma, z, power, and outer
          independent variables. To use this argument, variables
          input must contain Vars with the proper gamma/z/power roles.
          Defaults to `None`.
        * **validate** (*bool, default True*) – An expert-level keyword-only argument that determines whether to
          validate the input data. Only set to `False` if you are certain
          that the input data is valid.

    Notes

    If an ivarname is provided and its associated idxname is not
    provided, then its idxname is assumed to be “i” + “ivarname”.
    For example, if gamma\_ivarname is “GammaLoad” and gamma\_idxname
    is not provided, then gamma\_idxname is assumed to be “iGammaLoad”.

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    ```

    Instantiate a LoadPullBlock with a gamma ivar, using the default
    naming pattern for the gamma idxname:

    ```
    >>> data = {
    ...     'GammaLoad': [0.5 + 0.4j, 0.4 + 0.1j, 0.3 + 0.2j],
    ...     'iGammaLoad': [0, 1, 2],
    ...     'Gain': [10, 11, 12]
    ... }
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(data, gamma_ivarname='GammaLoad')
    >>> lpblock.gamma_ivarname
    'GammaLoad'
    >>> lpblock.gamma_idxname
    'iGammaLoad'
    ```

    Since the default naming pattern was used for gamma\_idxname, we
    did not have to specify it during initialization.

    Instantiate a LoadPullBlock with a gamma ivar and a power ivar. Note
    that when gamma and power are swept, the power sweep is always the
    inner sweep.

    ```
    >>> data = {
    ...     'GammaLoad': [0.5 + 0.4j, 0.5 + 0.4j, 0.4 + 0.1j, 0.4 + 0.1j],
    ...     'iGammaLoad': [0, 0, 1, 1],
    ...     'PSource': [-10.0, -8.0, -10.0, -8.0],
    ...     'iPSource': [0, 1, 0, 1],
    ...     'Gain': [10, 11, 12, 10.5, 11.5]
    ... }
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(
    ...     data,
    ...     gamma_ivarname='GammaLoad',
    ...     power_ivarname='PSource',
    ... )
    >>> lpblock.gamma_ivarname
    'GammaLoad'
    >>> lpblock.gamma_idxname
    'iGammaLoad'
    >>> lpblock.power_ivarname
    'PSource'
    >>> lpblock.power_idxname
    'iPSource'
    ```

    Since we did not specify the power\_idxname, it was assumed to be
    “iPSource”, which is the default naming pattern (“i” + “PSource”).

    See also

    The [`read_file_as_loadpullblock()`](../../../fileio/read_file_as_loadpullblock.md#keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.read_file_as_loadpullblock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.read_file_as_loadpullblock")
    function is the easiest way to instantiate a LoadPullBlock from a
    file. For more detailed information and examples, see
    [Work with Load Pull Data](../../../../howto/work_with_loadpull_data.md#work-with-load-pull-data).

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.__setitem__.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.\_\_setitem\_\_[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-setitem "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.\_\_setitem\_\_(*key: str*, *value: VarLike | Scalar*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.__setitem__ "Link to this definition")
:   Set a variable in the Block.

    Parameters:
    :   * **key** (*str*) – The name of the variable to add.
        * **value** (*Var, ArrayLike, or Scalar*) – If a [`Var`](../../../main/var/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var"), its data is finalized (if needed) and its
          metadata is added as-is. If an ArrayLike or Scalar, it is
          assumed to be the data for a new Var. A Scalar is
          automatically converted to a 1D ndarray of length equal to the
          number of observations in the Block.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Notes

    If value is a Var and the key does not match the Var’s name, a
    copy of the Var is created with a name that matches the key.

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** –

        * If value is not a Var and the varname already exists. In
          that scenario, use the [`set_data_in_place()`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.set_data_in_place.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.set_data_in_place "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.set_data_in_place") method
          instead.
          - If a scalar value is set in a Block without any variables.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_gcomp.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at\_gcomp[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-at-gcomp "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.at\_gcomp(*gcomp\_values: float | int | ArrayLike*, *gain\_varname: str*, *\**, *gcomp\_varname: str = 'auto'*, *gcomp\_idxname: str | None = 'auto'*, *extrap: bool = False*) → [LoadPullBlock](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_gcomp "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new LoadPullBlock at specified gain compression value(s).

    Interpolation and/or extrapolation is performed if needed.

    Parameters:
    :   * **gcomp\_values** – Gain compression values at which to calculate all responses.
          Can be a single float value, a list of values, or a numpy
          array.
        * **gain\_varname** – The name of the gain variable to use to calculate compression.
        * **gcomp\_varname, default ‘auto’** – The name of the new calculated gain compression variable.
          Defaults to `'auto'`, which results in a varname of
          `'gain_varname' + '_comp'`.
        * **gcomp\_idxname, default ‘auto’** – The name of the level of the DataFrame’s row MultiIndex that
          will be created to correspond to the gain compression. If
          `None`, no new level is created. Defaults to `'auto'`,
          which means that the index name will be `'i' + 'gcomp_col'`.
        * **extrap, default False** – If `True`, extrapolate the data (if needed). If `False`,
          points requested that are outside of the available the data
          regions will be filled with NaN values.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    >>> # Calculate the data at 0.5dB Gain compression point
    >>> lpblock_at_gcomp = lpblock.at_gcomp(0.5, 'Gain')
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_power.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at\_power[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-at-power "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.at\_power(*power\_values: float | int | ArrayLike*, *power\_varname: str*, *\**, *power\_idxname: str | None = 'auto'*, *interp: bool = True*, *extrap: bool = False*, *interp\_method: Literal['cubic', 'linear'] = 'cubic'*, *extrap\_method: Literal['cubic', 'linear', 'constant'] = 'cubic'*) → [LoadPullBlock](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_power "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new LoadPullBlock at specified power value(s).

    Parameters:
    :   * **power\_values** – Power value(s) at which to calculate all responses.
        * **power\_varname** – The name of the power variable to use for calculations.
        * **power\_idxname, default ‘auto’** – The name of the idx variable that will be created to
          correspond to the power. If `None`, no new idx variable is
          created. This also means that there is no power ivar.
          Defaults to `'auto'`, which creates an idx variable with
          the name `'i' + 'power_varname'`.
        * **interp, default True** – If `True`, interpolate the data (if needed). If `False`,
          points requested that fall between existing data points are
          filled with NaNs.
        * **extrap, default False** – If `True`, extrapolate the data (if needed). If `False`,
          points requested that are outside of the available the data
          regions are filled with NaN values.
        * **interp\_method, default ‘cubic’** – The interpolation method to use.
        * **extrap\_method, default ‘cubic’** – The extrapolation method to use.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Notes

    If `interp=False` and `extrap_method='cubic'`, interpolation
    is performed as a side effect of extrapolation.

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    >>> # Calculate the data at 42 dBm PLoad
    >>> lpblock_at_power = lpblock.at_power(42.0, 'PLoad')
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.attrs.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.attrs[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-attrs "Link to this heading")

*property* LoadPullBlock.attrs*: [AttrsDict](../../../metadata/attrsdict/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict "keysight.pwdatatools._core.properties.AttrsDict")*[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.attrs "Link to this definition")
:   A dict of arbitrary attributes (metadata).

    The attributes are stored in an [`AttrsDict`](../../../metadata/attrsdict/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict "keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict"), which is a
    type-restricted dict. The keys must be str, and the values must
    be one of the following types: str, int, float, complex, bool,
    None, `numpy.ndarray`, dict, or list. Nested dicts and
    lists are also supported.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.contourplot.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.contourplot[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-contourplot "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.contourplot(*z: str*, *\**, *ax=None*, *sca: bool = False*, *levels: int | ArrayLike | None = None*, *add\_xlabel: bool = True*, *add\_ylabel: bool = True*, *xlabel: str | None = None*, *ylabel: str | None = None*, *\*\*kwargs*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.contourplot "Link to this definition")
:   Plot contour lines on a matplotlib Axes.

    A matplotlib QuadContourSet object is returned, which can be used
    to further customize the plot. If you need access to the
    underlying Axes object, use the QuadContourSet’s `axes`
    attribute.

    LoadPullBlocks with a power ivar or outer ivars are not supported.
    If you want to plot contours for a LoadPullBlock with a power
    ivar, you must first get rid of the power dependnecy by selecting
    a subset of the data. There are several ways of doing this,
    including `LoadPullBlock.loc` and the following
    LoadPullBlock methods: [`at_power()`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_power.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_power "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_power"), [`at_gcomp()`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_gcomp.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_gcomp "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_gcomp"), and
    `xs()`.

    The same limitation is true of LoadPullBlocks with outer ivar(s),
    such as frequency, bias, etc. You must select a subset of the
    data before plotting.

    Parameters:
    :   * **z** – Name of the z variable to plot. The x and y variables are
          always the real and imaginary parts of the gamma ivar.
        * **ax** – An optional matplotlib Axes object. If `None`, use the
          current matplotlib Axes. Defaults to `None`.
        * **sca** – A bool that determines whether to set the Axes used for
          plotting as the current Axes. Invokes
          `matplotlib.pyplot.sca()`. Defaults to `False`.
        * **levels** (*int or array-like, optional*) – Determines the number and positions of the contour lines or
          regions. If an int, use matplotlib’s MaxNLocator, which tries
          to automatically choose no more than n+1 “nice” contour levels
          between minimum and maximum numeric values of Z. If array-
          like, draw contour lines at the specified levels. The values
          must be in increasing order.
        * **add\_xlabel** – If `True`, add an x label to the Axes. Defaults to `True`.
        * **add\_ylabel** – If `True`, add a y label to the Axes. Defaults to `True`.
        * **xlabel** – If `add_xlabel` is `True`, use this as the x label. If
          `None`, auto-create the label. Auto-creation tries to use
          the name of the x variable. If name of the variable is
          unknown, use the default of label, which is `'x'`. Defaults
          to `None`.
        * **ylabel** – If `add_ylabel` is `True`, use this as the y label. If
          `None`, auto-create the label. Auto-creation tries to use
          the name of the y variable. If name of the variable is
          unknown, use the default of label, which is `'y'`. Defaults
          to `None`.
        * **\*\*kwargs** – Additional keyword arguments are passed on to
          `matplotlib.pyplot.contour`.

    Returns:
    :   A matplotlib QuadContourSet instance.

    Return type:
    :   QuadContourSet

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If there is no gamma or impedance ivar.
        If there is a power ivar or any outer ivars.

    Examples

    Start with a LoadPullBlock:
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(…)

    Plot Gain contours on a rectangular chart:
    >>> lpblock.contourplot(‘Gain’)

    Plot Gain contours on a Smith chart:
    >>> contourset = lpblock.contourplot(‘Gain’)
    >>> from kesight.pwdatatools import viz
    >>> viz.draw\_smith\_chart(contourset.axes)

    Plot red DrainEff contours on the same Axes as Gain contours:
    >>> lpblock.contourplot(‘DrainEff’, ax=contourset.axes, colors=’red’)

    Return the QuadContourSet object, and use it to further customize:
    >>> cs = lpblock.contourplot(‘Gain’)
    >>> plt.clabel(cs, inline=True, fontsize=9, fmt=’%.1f’)


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.coord_system.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.coord\_system[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-coord-system "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.coord\_system() → Literal['rect', 'polar'] | None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.coord_system "Link to this definition")
:   If data is gridded, return the grid’s coordinate system (‘rect’ or ‘polar’).

    If data is not gridded, return `None`.

    To be considered “gridded”, the following conditions must be met:
    1) The data must have either a gamma or impedance dependency.
    2) The gamma or impedance values must be regularly-spaced.
    3) There must be the same number of y points for each x point.
    4) If the data also has a power-depedency (in addition to gamma or
    impedance), the 2D grid must be identical for each power value.

    If the data does not meet the specified conditions, it is not
    considered “gridded”, and `None` is returned.

    If the data has a frequency dependency, the 2D grid can have
    different spacing, number of points, and extents at each frequency
    point, but the coordinate system (‘rect’ or ‘polar’) must be
    consistent (a ValueError is raised if not). If, at any frequency,
    the data does not meet the conditions to be considered “gridded”,
    `None` is returned.

    Return type:
    :   {‘rect’, ’polar’, None}

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    >>> # Determine the coordinate system of the data
    >>> coord_system = lpblock.coord_system()
    >>> if coord_system is None:
    ...     print('Data is not gridded.')
    >>> elif coord_system == 'rect':
    ...     print('Data is gridded in rectangular coordinates.')
    >>> elif coord_system == 'polar':
    ...     print('Data is gridded in polar coordinates.')
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.copy.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.copy[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-copy "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.copy() → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.copy "Link to this definition")
:   Return a copy of self (always a deep copy).

    Returns:
    :   A deep copy of self.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](../../../main/block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Notes

    Because the underlying data in each Var is immutable, only metadata
    needs to be copied, so this operation is relatively inexpensive.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.drop_grid_edges.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.drop\_grid\_edges[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-drop-grid-edges "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.drop\_grid\_edges(*depth: int = 1*, *circular: bool | Literal['auto'] = 'auto'*) → [LoadPullBlock](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.drop_grid_edges "Link to this definition")
:   Return new LoadPullBlock with grid pts along outer edges removed.

    Parameters:
    :   * **depth** – The number of “rows” of outer grid points to remove from each
          edge of the grid. For example, if depth=1, the outermost grid
          points are removed. If depth=2, the outermost 2 rows of grid
          points are removed along each edge.
        * **circular** – Either a bool or `'auto'`. Only applies for grids with
          a polar coordinate system and if drop\_edges is `True`. If
          `'auto'`, automatically determine if the grid is circular or
          donut shaped and if so, only drop grid points around the
          circumference. Otherwise, if a bool, respect the parameter
          setting. Defaults to `'auto'`.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    >>> # The LoadPullBlock must be gridded
    >>> lpblock_gridded = lpblock.grid_data('rect', drop_edges=False)
    >>> # Remove the outermost 2 rows of grid points along each edge
    >>> lpblock_no_edges = lpblock_gridded.drop_grid_edges(depth=2)
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.drop_invalid_regular.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.drop\_invalid\_regular[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-drop-invalid-regular "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.drop\_invalid\_regular() → [LoadPullBlock](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.drop_invalid_regular "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new LoadPullBlock with all NaN and null values removed.

    The observations are dropped in such a way as to maintain
    regularity of the power sweeps at each Gamma or Impedance value.
    If there are outer ivars, power sweeps are NOT regularized across
    outer ivar values.

    If there is no power sweep, the NaN and null values are simply dropped.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.drop_observations.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.drop\_observations[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-drop-observations "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.drop\_observations(*condition: ArrayLike*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.drop_observations "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new filtered Block without the specified observations.

    Parameters:
    :   **condition** (*1D bool array-like*) – A 1D boolean array-like with the same length as the Block’s
        axis 0, with True values at the indexes of the observations
        to drop.

    Returns:
    :   A new Block with the observations dropped where condition
        is True.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](../../../main/block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> block = pwdt.Block(data={'Vout': [1, 2, 3], 'Vin': [4, 5, 6]})
    >>> vout = block['Vout']
    >>> vin = block['Vin']
    >>> block.drop_observations(vout > 2)
    <Block with 2 variables and 1 observation>
    >>> block.drop_observations((vout == 1) & (vin >= 4))
    <Block with 2 variables and 1 observation>
    >>> block.drop_observations(vout == 1 | vin >= 4)
    <Block with 2 variables and 1 observation>
    ```


---

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.fill\_null[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-fill-null "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.fill\_null(*values: [FillValues](../../../public_submodules/datatypes/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.datatypes.FillValues.md#keysight.pwdatatools.datatypes.FillValues "keysight.pwdatatools.datatypes.FillValues") | None = None*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.fill_null "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new Block with null values filled for all variables.

    Parameters:
    :   **values** (*FillValues, optional*) – An optional instance of `FillValues`. If not provided,
        the defult fill values are used.

    Notes

    Here are the default fill values for each dtype:

    * float: NaN
    * complex: NaN + 1j \* NaN
    * integer: 0
    * boolean: False
    * string: ‘’

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_block.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from\_block[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-from-block "Link to this heading")

*classmethod* LoadPullBlock.from\_block(*block: [Block](../../../main/block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block")*) → [LoadPullBlock](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_block "Link to this definition")
:   Make an instance of LoadPullBlock from a Block.

    Parameters:
    :   **block** (*Block*) – The Block to convert to a LoadPullBlock.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> block = pwdt.Block.from_file("/data/loadpull.cst")
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock.from_block(block)
    ```

    Notes

    This method always copies the Block’s variables, making
    completely separate instances of all variables and copying the
    metadata.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_file.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from\_file[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-from-file "Link to this heading")

*classmethod* LoadPullBlock.from\_file(*src: str | os.PathLike | BaseDataFile*, *loc: str = '/'*, *\**, *data: bool = True*, *format\_specific: FormatSpecificReadOptions | None = None*, *idxs: IdxsOption | None = None*, *idxs\_tol: float | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*, *src\_format: str | None = None*, *varnames: Mapping[str, str] | None = None*) → [LoadPullBlock](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_file "Link to this definition")
:   Make an instance of LoadPullBlock by reading a loadpull datafile.

    The following file formats are supported: “loadpull” (which
    includes Maury, Focus, and Keysight NVNA MDIF-based files),
    and “native” (.pwdt file extension).

    Parameters:
    :   * **src** (*str, os.PathLike, or DataFile*) – The datafile to read.
        * **loc** (*str, default ‘/’*) – The location within the source file to return as a LoadPullBlock. If
          `'/'` (the “root”), the entire source file is read.
          Otherwise, it represents the name or position of a Block in
          the source file (for example, `'/0'` or `'/LPData`).
        * **data** (*bool, default True*) – A bool that determines whether to read the data from `src`.
          If `False`, only the metadata is read. Reading only metadata
          can be useful for quickly examining information about the
          variables.
        * **format\_specific** (*FormatSpecificReadOptions, optional*) – Defines the format-specific options for reading `src`. By
          default, the global option
          `options.reading.format_specific` sets format-specific
          reading options, but this parameter can override the global
          options. If provided, it must be an instance of
          `FormatSpecificReadOptions`. See the docs for a list of
          all classes that can act as format-specific read options.
          Examples are `LoadPullReadOptions` and
          `LoadPullDerivedVars`.
        * **idxs** (*{‘auto’, True, False}, optional*) – If `True`, create index variables (idxs) when reading the
          source file. If `False`, do not create them. If `'auto'`,
          use the format-specific defaults. If `None`, use the global
          option `options.reading.idxs`.
        * **idxs\_tol** (*float, optional*) – The tolerance to use when generating index variables from float
          or complex data. If NaN, no tolerance is used and indexes are
          created by using exact equality. If provided, it takes precedence
          over the global option `options.reading.idxs_tol`.
        * **iffs** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing.
        * **src\_format** (*str, optional*) – Sets the source file’s format. Supported types are stored in
          `options.reading.formats`. Any value other
          than `None` overrides the format inferred from or set by
          `src`.
        * **varnames** (*Mapping[RoleLike, str]*) – A mapping of variable roles to their names. Defaults to
          `options.reading.varnames`.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Examples

    Using all the default options:

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock.from_file("/data/loadpull.cst")
    ```

    Modifying global options to control how variables are named and
    which variables are derived from a and b waves:

    ```
    >>> pwdt.options.reading.varnames["power.delivered.load"] = "Pout"
    >>> pwdt.options.reading.format_specific.loadpull.derived_vars.ampm = False
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock.from_file("/data/loadpull.cst")
    ```

    Passing in a dict of roles to varnames and an instance of
    `LoadPullDerivedVars` to override the global settings:

    ```
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock.from_file(
    ...     "/data/loadpull.cst",
    ...     varnames={'gamma.load': 'GL'},
    ...     format_specific=pwdt.LoadPullDerivedVars(ampm=False),
    ... )
    ```

    See also

    :func:read\_file\_as\_loadpullblock


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_pandas_dataframe.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from\_pandas\_dataframe[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-from-pandas-dataframe "Link to this heading")

*classmethod* LoadPullBlock.from\_pandas\_dataframe(*dataframe: DataFrame*, *\**, *name: str = ''*, *gamma\_ivarname: str | None = None*, *gamma\_idxname: str | None = None*, *z\_ivarname: str | None = None*, *z\_idxname: str | None = None*, *power\_ivarname: str | None = None*, *power\_idxname: str | None = None*, *outer\_ivarnames: Iterable[str] | None = None*, *outer\_idxnames: Iterable[str] | None = None*, *combine\_multidim: bool = True*, *dimscales\_delim: Tuple[str, str, str] = ('[', ',', ']')*, *\*\*kwargs*) → [LoadPullBlock](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_pandas_dataframe "Link to this definition")
:   Create a new LoadPullBlock from a pandas DataFrame.

    Parameters:
    :   * **dataframe** (*pandas.DataFrame*) – A DataFrame containing all variable data. The DataFrame’s row
          index can be either a “default” index (a RangeIndex or
          Int64Index with start=0 & step=1), or non-default index(es)
          with name(s).
        * **name** (*str, default “”*) – A str name for the LoadpullBlock.
        * **gamma\_ivarname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the gamma independent variable. A LoadPullBlock
          must have either a gamma ivar or a z ivar, but not both.
        * **gamma\_idxname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the gamma indepedent variable’s index.
        * **z\_ivarname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the impedance independent variable. A LoadPullBlock
          must have either a gamma ivar or a z ivar, but not both.
        * **z\_idxname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the impedance indepedent variable’s index.
        * **power\_ivarname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the power independent variable.
        * **power\_idxname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the power indepedent variable’s index.
        * **outer\_ivarnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the outer independent variables. Outer ivars can be
          any arbitrary swept variables, such as frequency, bias, etc.
        * **outer\_idxnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the outer indepedent variable’s indexes.
        * **combine\_multidim** (*bool, default True*) – If `True`, combine multidimensional variables into a single
          variable. If `False`, keep multidimensional variables as
          separate variables.
        * **dimscales\_delim** (*tuple of str, default (‘[’, ‘,’, ‘]’)*) – The characters used to separate the dimension scales in the
          column names of the DataFrame. The first character is the
          opening delimiter, the second is the delimiter between
          dimension scales, and the third is the closing delimiter.
          For example, the default delimiters would interpret columns
          like `'S[2,1]'` and `'PortZ[1]'` as variables `'S'` and
          `'PortZ'` with dimension scales of (2,1) and (1),
          respectively.

    Other Parameters:
    :   * **idxnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the indexes. This is another way to define the
          names of the gamma, z, power, and outer indexes. To use this
          argument, you must also specify ivarnames and variables
          input must contain Vars with the proper gamma/z/power roles.
          Defaults to `None`.
        * **ivarnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the independent variables. This is another way
          to define the names of the gamma, z, power, and outer
          independent variables. To use this argument, variables
          input must contain Vars with the proper gamma/z/power roles.
          Defaults to `None`.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.gamma_idx.md === -->

LoadPullBlock.gamma\_idx() → [Var](../../../main/var/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.gamma_idx "Link to this definition")

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.gamma_ivar.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.gamma\_ivar[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-gamma-ivar "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.gamma\_ivar(*form: Literal['complex', 'real', 'imag', 'mag', 'angle', 'polar\_str', 'rect\_str'] = 'complex'*, *\*\*kwargs*) → [Var](../../../main/var/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.gamma_ivar "Link to this definition")
:   Return the gamma independent variable (ivar) in the form specified.

    If a gamma is not present in the ivars, a ValueError is raised.
    A variable is considered to be a gamma if its main role is
    “gamma” in the Var’s `role` metadata.

    Parameters:
    :   **form** – The form of the gamma ivar to return. Must be one of
        `"complex"`, `"real"`, `"imag"`, `"mag"`, `"angle"`,
        `"polar_str"`, or `"rect_str"`. Defaults to `"complex"`.

    Other Parameters:
    :   * **enable\_prefix** (*bool*) – If `True`, the returned Var will have a name that is
          prefixed with the real\_prefix, imag\_prefix, mag\_prefix,
          or angle\_prefix, depending on the value of `form`. If
          `False`, the returned Var will have a name that is
          not prefixed. The default is `True`.
        * **enable\_suffix** (*bool*) – If `True`, the returned Var will have a name that is
          suffixed with the real\_suffix, imag\_suffix, mag\_suffix,
          or angle\_suffix, depending on the value of `form`. If
          `False`, the returned Var will have a name that is
          not suffixed. The default is `True`.
        * **real\_prefix** (*str*) – A prefix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="real"`. The default is `'real_of_'`.
        * **real\_suffix** (*str*) – A suffix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="real"`. The default is `""`.
        * **imag\_prefix** (*str*) – A prefix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="imag"`. The default is `'imag_of_'`.
        * **imag\_suffix** (*str*) – A suffix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="imag"`. The default is `""`.
        * **mag\_prefix** (*str*) – A prefix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="mag"`. The default is `'mag_of_'`.
        * **mag\_suffix** (*str*) – A suffix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="mag"`. The default is `""`.
        * **angle\_prefix** (*str*) – A prefix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="angle"`. The default is `'angle_of'`.
        * **angle\_suffix** (*str*) – A suffix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="angle"`. The default is `""`.
        * **angle\_deg** (*bool*) – If `True`, the angle values are returned in degrees.
          If `False`, the angle are returned in radians. The default
          is `True`.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](../../../main/var/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    >>> # Get the gamma ivar in complex form
    >>> gamma_complex = lpblock.gamma_ivar()
    >>> # Get the gamma ivar in real form
    >>> gamma_real = lpblock.gamma_ivar("real")
    >>> # Get the gamma ivar in polar string form (e.g., "0.520 | 170.0")
    >>> # This is ideal for plotting labels
    >>> gamma_polar_str = lpblock.gamma_ivar("polar_str")
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.gamma_ivar_scatterplot.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.gamma\_ivar\_scatterplot[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-gamma-ivar-scatterplot "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.gamma\_ivar\_scatterplot(*\*\*kwargs*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.gamma_ivar_scatterplot "Link to this definition")
:   Plot the gamma ivar as a scatterplot on a matplotlib Axes.

    All keyword arguments are passed to seaborn’s scatterplot
    function, with the exception of the `data` kwarg. The `data`
    kwarg passed to seaborn’s scatterplot is always this LoadPullBlock
    instance. If no Axes object is supplied with the `ax` keyword
    parameter, then the current Axes is used.

    Return type:
    :   matplotlib.axes.Axes

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If there is no gamma ivar.

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    ```

    Plot the gamma ivar on the current Axes:
    >>> lpblock.gamma\_ivar\_scatterplot()

    Plot the gamma ivar on a given Axes:
    >>> fig, ax = plt.subplots()
    >>> lpblock.gamma\_ivar\_scatterplot(ax=ax)

    Plot the gamma ivar on a Smith chart:
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import viz
    >>> fig, ax = plt.subplots()
    >>> viz.draw\_smith\_chart(ax)
    >>> lpblock.gamma\_ivar\_scatterplot(ax=ax)

    Plot the gamma ivar with swept freq shown with different hues
    (note that you only need to pass the str varname, not a Series):
    >>> fig, ax = plt.subplots()
    >>> lpblock.gamma\_ivar\_scatterplot(ax=ax, hue=’freq’)

    Customize the color and markers:
    >>> lpblock.gamma\_ivar\_scatterplot(color=’red’, marker=”x”)

    Return the Axes object to a variable for further customization:
    >>> ax = lpblock.gamma\_ivar\_scatterplot()
    >>> ax.set\_title(“My custom title”)


---

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.gamma\_to\_z[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-gamma-to-z "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.gamma\_to\_z(*z\_name: str*) → [LoadPullBlock](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.gamma_to_z "Link to this definition")

    Parameters:

        the gamma ivar.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock

    >>> lpblock_z = lpblock.gamma_to_z("ZLoad")
    >>> lpblock_z.z_ivarname
    'ZLoad'
    >>> lpblock_z.gamma_ivarname is None
    ```

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.get.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.get[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-get "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.get(*varname: str*, *default=None*) → [Var](../../../main/var/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var") | None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.get "Link to this definition")
:   Return a variable by name, or None if the variable does not exist.

    Similar to [`__getitem__()`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.__getitem__.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.__getitem__ "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.__getitem__") and [`get_var()`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.get_var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.get_var "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.get_var") methods, except
    that this method returns None if the varname is not found, while
    the other two methods raise a KeyError.

    Parameters:
    :   * **varname** – The name of the variable to retrieve.
        * **default** – The value to return if the variable is not found. Only
          None is supported.

    Return type:
    :   *Optional*[[*Var*](../../../main/var/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")]


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.get_grid.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.get\_grid[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-get-grid "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.get\_grid() → [Grid](../../grid/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Grid "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.gridding.Grid") | None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.get_grid "Link to this definition")
:   Return a Grid object from self.

    If the sweep includes outer swept variables, a ValueError is
    raised. If you want get a Grid from a LoadPullBlock with
    swept outer variables, you must first take a cross-section
    (for example, use the `xs` method or `loc`) to reduce the
    dimensionality.

    To be considered “gridded”, the following conditions must be met:
    1) The data must have either a gamma or impedance dependency.
    2) The gamma or impedance values must be regularly-spaced.
    3) There must be the same number of y points for each x point.
    4) If the data also has a power-depedency (in addition to gamma or
    impedance), the 2D grid must be identical for each power value.

    If the data does not meet the specified conditions, it is not
    considered “gridded”, and `None` is returned.

    If the data has a frequency dependency, the 2D grid can have
    different spacing, number of points, and extents at each frequency
    point, but the coordinate system (‘rect’ or ‘polar’) must be
    consistent (a ValueError is raised if not). If, at any frequency,
    the data does not meet the conditions to be considered “gridded”,
    `None` is returned.

    Return type:
    :   [*Grid*](../../grid/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Grid "keysight.pwdatatools.Grid")

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If the sweep includes outer swept variables.

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    >>> # Get the grid from the LoadPullBlock
    >>> grid = lpblock.get_grid()
    >>> if grid is not None:
    >>>     # The data is gridded
    >>>     # Do something with the grid
    >>> else:
    >>>     # The data is not gridded
    >>>     # Do something else
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.get_sweep.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.get\_sweep[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-get-sweep "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.get\_sweep() → [LoadPullSweep](../../loadpullsweep/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.sweeping.LoadPullSweep")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.get_sweep "Link to this definition")

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.grid_data.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.grid\_data[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-grid-data "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.grid\_data(*coord\_system: Literal['rect', 'polar']*, *\**, *npointsx: int = 10*, *npointsy: int = 0*, *xmax: float = nan*, *xmin: float = nan*, *ymax: float = nan*, *ymin: float = nan*, *drop\_edges: bool = True*, *circular: bool | Literal['auto'] = 'auto'*) → [LoadPullBlock](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.grid_data "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new LoadPullBlock with data interpolated on a 2D grid.

    By default, the 2D grid’s extents are set by the extents of the
    data. But the grid’s extents may be set explicitly by the keyword
    args xmax, xmin, ymax, and ymin.

    Gridding will fail as you approach the edges of the original
    data’s extents. These bad points will contain NaN values. Also, it
    is possible that gridding does not fail, but instead produces
    unreliable data near the edges. For these reasons, the default
    behavior is to drop the outermost grid points around the edges of
    the gridded data.

    Parameters:
    :   * **coord\_system** – Either `'rect'` or `'polar'`.
        * **npointsx** – Number of points in grid’s x dimension. If 0, use same
          value as npointsy. Defaults to 10.
        * **npointsy** – Number of points in grid’s y dimension. If 0, use same
          value as npointsx. Defaults to 0.
        * **xmax** – Maximum value of x. If NaN, the max value of the original
          data is used. Default is NaN.
        * **xmin** – Minimum value of x. If NaN, the min value of the original
          data is used. Default is NaN.
        * **ymax** – Maximum value of y. If NaN, the max value of the original
          data is used. Default is NaN.
        * **ymin** – Minimum value of y. If NaN, the min value of the original
          data is used. Default is NaN.
        * **drop\_edges** – A bool that determines whether the outermost “rows” of gridded
          data around the grid’s edges are removed. Defaults to `True`.
        * **circular** – Either a bool or `'auto'`. Only applies to polar grids and
          when drop\_edges is `True`. If `'auto'`, tries to
          automatically determine if the grid is circular or donut-
          shaped and if so, drops only grid points around the
          circumference. Otherwise, if a bool, respects the parameter
          setting. Defaults to `'auto'`.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    >>> # Apply a rectangular grid with 8 points in x and y
    >>> lpblock_gridded = lpblock.grid_data('rect', npointsx=8)
    >>> # Apply a polar grid with the default 10 points in x and y
    >>> lpblock_gridded = lpblock.grid_data('polar')
    >>> # Apply a polar grid, explicitly setting the extents of the grid
    >>> lpblock_gridded = lpblock.grid_data(
    ...     'polar', xmax=0.5, xmin=0.1, ymax=0.5, ymin=0.1
    ... )
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.has_regular_power_ivar.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.has\_regular\_power\_ivar[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-has-regular-power-ivar "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.has\_regular\_power\_ivar() → bool[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.has_regular_power_ivar "Link to this definition")
:   Return True if the data has identical power values at each gamma or z.

    If the data has outer ivars (for example, frequency), this
    function returns True even if the data has different power values
    at each outer ivar value.

    Return type:
    :   *bool*

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    >>> if lpblock.has_regular_power_ivar():
    >>>     # The data has identical power values at each Gamma or Z
    >>>     # Do something
    >>> else:
    >>>     # The data does not have identical power values at each Gamma or Z
    >>>     # Do something else
    ```

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If the data does not have a power ivar.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.info.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.info[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-info "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.info(*varnames: str | Iterable[str] | None = None*) → DataFrame[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.info "Link to this definition")
:   Return a DataFrame containing information about the specified variable(s).

    The information on each variables includes the kind (ivar, dvar, or
    idx), role, dtype, min, max, NaN count, and masked value count.

    For complex variables, the min and max values are the minimum and
    maximum magnitudes. For string, boolean and other non-numeric types,
    the min and max values are not defined.

    Parameters:
    :   **varname** (*str or iterable of str, optional*) – Name(s) of a variable(s) on which to gather info. If `None`,
        return info for all variables. If `varnames` is a str,
        return a DataFrame with that variable’s info. If `varnames`
        is an iterable of str, return a DataFrame containing all of
        those variables’ info. Defaults to `None`.

    Return type:
    :   pandas.DataFrame


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.is_gridded.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.is\_gridded[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-is-gridded "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.is\_gridded() → bool[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.is_gridded "Link to this definition")
:   Return bool indicating whether the data is gridded.

    To be considered “gridded”, the following conditions must be met:
    1) The data must have either a gamma or impedance dependency.
    2) The gamma or impedance values must be regularly-spaced.
    3) There must be the same number of y points for each x point.
    4) If the data also has a power-depedency (in addition to gamma or
    impedance), the 2D grid must be identical for each power value.

    If the data does not meet the specified conditions, it is not
    considered “gridded”, and `False` is returned.

    If the data has a frequency dependency, the 2D grid can have
    different spacing, number of points, and extents at each frequency
    point, but the coordinate system (‘rect’ or ‘polar’) must be
    consistent (a ValueError is raised if not). If, at any frequency,
    the data does not meet the conditions to be considered “gridded”,
    `False` is returned.

    Return type:
    :   *bool*

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If the gridding is not consistent across power values.
        If the coordinate system is not consistent across frequencies.

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    >>> # Check if the data is gridded
    >>> is_gridded = lpblock.is_gridded()
    >>> if is_gridded:
    >>>     # The data is gridded
    >>>     # Do something
    >>> else:
    >>>     # The data is not gridded
    >>>     # Do something else
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.is_group.md === -->

LoadPullBlock.is\_group() → bool[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.is_group "Link to this definition")
:   Always returns False since this object is a Block, not a Group.

LoadPullBlock.items() → ItemsView[str, [Var](../../../main/var/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")][](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.items "Link to this definition")
:   Return an items view iterator over (varname, Var) pairs.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.iter_sections.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.iter\_sections[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-iter-sections "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.iter\_sections(*varnames: str | Iterable[str] | None = None*, *\**, *auto\_pref: Literal['idxs', 'ivars'] | None = None*, *tol: float | Mapping[str, float] = nan*) → Iterator[Tuple[Tuple[Any, ...], Self]][](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.iter_sections "Link to this definition")
:   Iterate over sections of the Block.

    The Block is split into sections based upon the values of the
    implicitly or explicitly specified variable(s).

    Parameters:
    :   * **varnames** (*str or iterable of str, optional*) – Name(s) of the variable(s) on which to split the Block into
          sections. If a single str, the Block is split into sections
          based on the values of that variable. If an iterable of str,
          the Block is split into sections based on every combination
          of the values of all of those variables. If `None`, the
          Block is split into sections based on the the setting of
          `auto_pref`.
        * **auto\_pref** (*{‘idxs’, ‘ivars’}, optional*) – Only applies if `varnames` is `None`. If `'idxs'`,
          the Block is split into sections based on the values of the
          outer idx variables (as defined by [`Block.idxnames`](../../../main/block/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.idxnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.idxnames "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.idxnames")). If
          `'ivars'`, the Block is split into sections based on the
          values of the outer independent variables (as defined by
          [`Block.ivarnames`](../../../main/block/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.ivarnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.ivarnames "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.ivarnames")). If `None`, prefer the Block’s idx
          variables. If no idxs, the ivars are checked next. If the
          Block has neither idxs nor ivars, a ValueError is raised.
        * **tol** (*float or dict-like, default NaN*) – The absolute tolerance to use when splitting the Block into
          sections based upon variables with inexact dtypes. Inexact
          dtypes include all float and complex dtypes. If tol is a
          float, it is the tolerance used for all variables. If tol
          is a dict-like, the keys are variable names and the values are
          float tolerances for each variable. If any tolerance is NaN,
          the Block is split into sections based on exact value matches
          for that variable.

    Yields:
    :   * *Any* – The value(s) of the variable(s) on which the Block is split.
        * *Block* – A Block containing the observations for the current section.

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.keep\_vars[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-keep-vars "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.keep\_vars(*varnames: Iterable[str]*, *\**, *keep\_crucial: bool = True*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.keep_vars "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new Block with only the specified variables.

    All kept Vars are deepcopied.

    Parameters:
    :   * **varnames** – The names of the variables to keep.
        * **keep\_crucial** – If `True`, keep all vars returned by the
          [`crucial_varnames()`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.crucial_varnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.crucial_varnames "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.crucial_varnames") method. Otherwise, only keep them
          if they are explicitly included in varnames. Defaults to
          `True`.

    Returns:
    :   A new Block with only the specified variables.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](../../../main/block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Raises:
    :   **KeyError** – If any of the varnames are not in the Block.


---

:   Keep only the specified variables (modifies Block in place).

    All kept variables are unmodified.

    Parameters:
    :   * **varnames** – The names of the variables to keep.
        * **keep\_crucial** – If `True`, keep all vars returned by the
          [`crucial_varnames()`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.crucial_varnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.crucial_varnames "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.crucial_varnames") method. Otherwise, only keep them
          if they are explicitly included in varnames. Defaults to
          `True`.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Raises:
    :   **KeyError** – If any of the variable names in varnames are not in data.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.regularize_power_ivar.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.regularize\_power\_ivar[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-regularize-power-ivar "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.regularize\_power\_ivar(*\**, *points: ndarray | List[float] | None = None*, *npoints: int = 0*, *interp\_method: Literal['cubic', 'linear'] = 'cubic'*, *extrap: bool = False*, *extrap\_method: Literal['cubic', 'linear', 'constant'] = 'cubic'*, *decimals: int = 1*, *drop\_invalid: bool = False*) → [LoadPullBlock](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.regularize_power_ivar "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new LoadPullBlock with regularized power ivar values.

    A regularized power sweep has the exact same values at each gamma
    or z value. If the data has outer ivar(s) (for example,
    frequency), the power is regularized at each outer sweep value,
    but is NOT regularized across outer sweep values.

    If both `points` and `npoints` are supplied, the `points`
    are treated as extra points to be explicitly added to the evenly-
    spaced power sweep calculated by `npoints`. Therefore, the total
    number of power points will be `npoints` plus the number of
    points in `points`.

    Parameters:
    :   * **points** (*array\_like or None, optional*) – The power values to use for each gamma or z point in the new
          regularized LoadPullBlock. If `None`, the power values are
          determined solely by the `npoints` argument. Otherwise,
          if a list or array is provided, the points are explicitly
          included.
        * **npoints** (*int, default 0*) – The number of power points to use for each gamma or z point in
          the new regularized power LoadPullBlock. If 0, the number of
          points is the average number of power points at each gamma or
          z value. However, if `points` is supplied, then those points
          are used instead.
        * **interp\_method** (*{‘cubic’, ‘linear’}, default ‘cubic’*) – The interpolation method to use when interpolating the data
          to fill in missing values. Must be one of `"linear"` or
          `"cubic"`.
        * **extrap** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, extrapolate the data to fill in missing values.
        * **extrap\_method** (*{‘cubic’, ‘linear’, ‘constant’}, default ‘cubic’*) – The extrapolation method to use when extrapolating the data
          to fill in missing values. Must be one of `"linear"`,
          `"cubic"`, or `"constant"`.
        * **decimals** (*int, default 1*) – The number of decimal places to round the power values to.
        * **drop\_invalid** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, observations with NaN or NA values are dropped.
          They are dropped in such a way as to maintain a regular
          power ivar.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    ```

    Regularize the power, providing explicit power points:

    ```
    >>> lpblock = lpblock.regularize_power_ivar(points=[-10, -5, 0, 5, 10])
    ```

    Regularize the power, providing the number of power points:
    >>> lpblock = lpblock.regularize\_power\_ivar(npoints=5)

    Regularize the power, providing both explicit power points and
    the number of power points resulting in a total of 5 + 10 = 15
    power points, evenly spaced between pmax and pmin and including
    the explicit power points provided:

    ```
    >>> lpblock = lpblock.regularize_power_ivar(
    ...     points=[-10, -5, 0, 5, 10], npoints=10
    ... )
    ```

    Enable extrapolation (uses default cubic method):

    ```
    >>> lpblock = lpblock.regularize_power_ivar(extrap=True)
    ```

    Use linear interpolation and extrapolation, instead of the
    default cubic interpolation and extrapolation. Note you must also
    set `extrap=True` to enable extrapolation:

    ```
    >>> lpblock = lpblock.regularize_power_ivar(
    ...     extrap=True,
    ...     interp_method="linear",
    ...     extrap_method="linear",
    ... )
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.set_data.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.set\_data[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-set-data "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.set\_data(*data: Mapping[str, ArrayLike] | pd.DataFrame*, *\**, *combine\_multidim\_vars: bool = True*, *dimscales\_delim: Tuple[str, str, str] = ('[', ',', ']')*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.set_data "Link to this definition")
:   Replace the data for variable(s) in the Block, returning a new Block.

    If a variable does not exist, it is created. If it does exist,
    its data is replaced and all other attributes are preserved.

    Parameters:
    :   * **data** (*mapping of str to array-like or pandas.DataFrame*) – The new data to set in the Block.
        * **combine\_multidim\_vars** (*bool, default True*) – If `True`, combine multidimensional variables in data into
          a single variable. This also has the effect of combining the
          existing multidimensional variables in the Block. If
          `False`, do not combine multidimensional variables.
        * **dimscales\_delim** (*length 3 tuple of str, default (‘[’, ‘,’, ‘]’)*) – A tuple of str characters for embedding dimension scales into
          the varnames of flattened multidim variables. Only applies if
          `combine_multidim_vars=True`. The first character is the
          opening delimiter, the second is the delimiter between
          dimension scales, and the third is the closing delimiter. For
          example, the default delimiters would work for flattened
          multidim varnames like `'S[2,1]'` and `'PortZ[1]'`.

    Returns:
    :   A new Block with the specified data.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](../../../main/block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** –

        * If the length along axis 0 of any of the array-likes in
          data does not match the Block’s observation count.
          - If data is a pandas DataFrame and combine\_multidim\_vars
          is `True`, and the dimscales of a multidimensional variable
          in the DataFrame do not match the dimscales of the
          corresponding multidimensional variable in the Block.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.set_data_in_place.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.set\_data\_in\_place[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-set-data-in-place "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.set\_data\_in\_place(*data: Mapping[str, ArrayLike | Scalar] | pd.DataFrame*, *\**, *combine\_multidim\_vars: bool = True*, *dimscales\_delim: Tuple[str, str, str] = ('[', ',', ']')*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.set_data_in_place "Link to this definition")
:   Set the data for variable(s) in the Block (in place).

    If a variable does not exist, it is created. If it does exist,
    its data is replaced and all other attributes are preserved.

    Parameters:
    :   * **data** (*mapping of str to array-like/scalar values or pandas.DataFrame*) – The new data to set in the Block.
        * **combine\_multidim\_vars** (*bool, default True*) – If `True`, combine multidimensional variables in data into
          a single variable. This also has the effect of combining the
          existing multidimensional variables in the Block. If
          `False`, do not combine multidimensional variables.
        * **dimscales\_delim** (*length 3 tuple of str, default (‘[’, ‘,’, ‘]’)*) – A tuple of str characters for embedding dimension scales into
          the varnames of flattened multidim variables. Only applies if
          `combine_multidim_vars=True`. The first character is the
          opening delimiter, the second is the delimiter between
          dimension scales, and the third is the closing delimiter. For
          example, the default delimiters would work for flattened
          multidim varnames like `'S[2,1]'` and `'PortZ[1]'`.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** –

        * If the length along axis 0 of any of the array-likes in
          data does not match the Block’s observation count.
          - If data is a pandas DataFrame and combine\_multidim\_vars
          is `True`, and the dimscales of a multidimensional variable
          in the DataFrame do not match the dimscales of the
          corresponding multidimensional variable in the Block.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.set_vars_in_place.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.set\_vars\_in\_place[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-set-vars-in-place "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.set\_vars\_in\_place(*variables: [Var](../../../main/var/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var") | Iterable[[Var](../../../main/var/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")]*, *\**, *copy: bool = False*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.set_vars_in_place "Link to this definition")
:   Set variables in the Block (in place).

    This method adds new variable(s) and/or replaces existing
    variables. If a variable by the same name already exists
    in the Block, it is replaced.

    Parameters:
    :   * **variables** (*Var or iterable of Var*) – Var instance(s) to set in the Block.
        * **copy** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, always make a copy of the Var(s) before setting
          them in the Block. If `False`, only make copies when necessary.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Notes

    Copies are made when the Var’s block attribute is not None and
    copy is False. This is to ensure that the Var is not shared
    between multiple Blocks.


---

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.to\_adscontourblock[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-to-adscontourblock "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.to\_adscontourblock(*name: str | None = None*) → ADSContourBlock[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.to_adscontourblock "Link to this definition")
:   Return an ADSContourBlock from self.

    The ADSCountourBlock data is formatted in a special way that
    facilitates contour plotting in PathWave Advanced Design System
    (ADS).

    Parameters:
    :   **name** – The str name of the ADSContourBlock. If `None`, the name of
        the LoadPullBlock is used. Defaults to `None`.

    Return type:
    :   ADSContourBlock

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(...)  # start with a LoadPullBlock
    >>> adsblock = lpblock.to_adscontourblock()  # convert to ADSContourBlock
    >>> adsblock.to_file("lpdata.ds")  # save the ADSContourBlock to an ADS dataset
    >>> # create a Group of consisting of any groupable members, including
    >>> # Blocks, ADSContourBlocks, LoadPullBlocks, and other Groups
    >>> group = pwdt.Group(members=[adsblock, block])
    >>> group.to_file("/home/group.ds")  # save the Group to an ADS dataset
    ```
