<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.to_file.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.to\_file[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-to-file "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.to\_file(*dst: str | PathLike | BaseDataFile*, *dst\_mode: Literal['x', 'w', 'a'] = 'x'*, *\**, *dst\_format: str | None = None*, *format\_specific: FormatSpecificWriteOptions | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*, *loc: str = '/'*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.to_file "Link to this definition")
:   Write data (and possibly metadata) to a file.

    For all destination file formats except native pwdatatools files
    (.pwdt extension), the idxs are used as the ivars in the file, and
    the ivars become dvars in the file. This avoids limitations in
    most file formats with respect to complex ivars.

    If the destination file dst has a known file extension that maps
    to a supported format, then the file is written in that format.
    If the destination file has a missing or unknown file extension,
    you must either 1) input a [`DataFile`](../../../fileio/datafile/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile") with a
    `format_override` as `dst`, or 2) use the `dst_format`
    parameter. Known writeable file extensions are stored in
    `options.writing.extentions`. Known writeable file formats
    are stored in `options.writing.formats`.

    Whether metadata is written depends on the format of output file.
    If the output file’s format has places to store metadata (such as
    in the native pwdatatools .pwdt format), then metadata is
    automatically included in the output file.

    Parameters:
    :   * **dst** – The destination file as a str path, a `pathlib.Path`,
          or a `pwdatatools.DataFile`.
        * **dst\_mode** – The mode to use when opening `dst`. If `dst` does not yet
          exist, all three modes yield the same behavior. However, the 3
          modes have differing behaviors if attempting to write to a pre-
          existing file. `'w'` overwrites the existing file. `'a'`
          appends to the existing file. `'x'` results in a
          FileExistsError. Defaults to `'x'`.
        * **dst\_format** – Sets the destination file’s format. Supported types are stored
          in `options.writing.formats`. Any value other than
          `None` overrides the format inferred from or set by `dst`.
          Defaults to `None`.
        * **format\_specific** (*FormatSpecificWriteOptions*) – Defines the format-specific options for writing `dst`. By
          default, the global option
          `options.writing.format_specific` sets format-specific
          writing options, but this parameter overrides the global
          options. If provided, it must be an instance of
          `FormatSpecificWriteOptions`. See the docs for a list
          of all classes that are format-specific write options. Some
          examples are `ADSWriteOptions`,
          `MDIFWriteOptions`, `ADSWriteInvalid`, and
          `MDIFWriteInvalid`.
        * **loc** (*str*) – The write location within `dst`. Only applies to file
          formats that support multiple levels of hierarchy (for
          example, native and HDF5 formats). This parameter sets the
          write location within the file’s directory-like (or tree-like)
          structure. The “root” location is ‘/’. Defaults to `'/'`.
        * **iffs** (*bool*) – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing. Defaults to `False`.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Examples

    The below examples assume that `block` is an instance of
    [`LoadPullBlock`](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock") and
    the folllowing imports:

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import DataFile
    >>> from pathlib import Path
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` has a known file extension, you can
    simply input `dst` as a str, `pathlib.Path`, or
    `DataFile`. Known file
    extensions are stored in `options.writing.extentions`.

    ```
    >>> # str path with known ext (.ds)
    >>> block.to_file('/home/s_param.ds')
    >>> # pathlib.Path with known ext (.mdf)
    >>> block.to_file(Path('/home/noise_param.mdf'))
    >>> # DataFile with known ext (.pwdt)
    >>> block.to_file(DataFile('/home/noise_param.pwdt'))
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` has a missing or non-standard file
    extension, you can use the `dst_format` parameter. Alternatively,
    you can input an instance of
    `DataFile` as the `dst`
    argument and use the DataFile’s `format_override` parameter to
    define the destination’s format.

    ```
    >>> # no ext solved by dst_format
    >>> block.to_file('/data/s_param', dst_format='mdif')
    >>> # non-standard ext solved by dst_format
    >>> block.to_file('/data/noise_param.txt', dst_format='mdif')
    >>> # no ext solved by DataFile
    >>> block.to_file(DataFile('/data/noise_param', format_override='mdif'))
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` already exists, a FileExistsError is
    raised if `dst_mode='x'`, which is the default. A `dst_mode`
    parameter value of `'w'` will overwrite the entire existing file,
    while a value of `'a'` will append to the existing file.

    Note

    Only certain types of datafiles work for `dst_mode='a'`. A
    ValueError is raised if append mode is not supported for the format
    of `dst`.

    ```
    >>> block.to_file('/s_param.ds', dst_mode='w')  # overwrite dst if it exists
    >>> block.to_file('/noise_param.mdf', dst_mode='a') # append to dst if it exists
    >>> block.to_file('/run1.pwdt', dst_mode='a') # append to dst if it exists
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.to_pandas_dataframe.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.to\_pandas\_dataframe[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-to-pandas-dataframe "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.to\_pandas\_dataframe(*\**, *index: Literal['default', 'ivars', 'idxs'] = 'default'*, *cols\_nlevels: int = 0*, *cols\_level\_names: bool = True*, *cols\_delim: Tuple[str, str, str] = ('[', ',', ']')*, *multidim\_pref: Literal['dims', 'default\_ints'] = 'dims'*, *dims\_pref: Literal[None, 'nums', 'names'] = None*, *default\_ints: Literal[1, 0] = 1*) → DataFrame[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.to_pandas_dataframe "Link to this definition")
:   Create a pandas DataFrame from this Block.

    Parameters:
    :   * **index** (*{‘default’, ‘ivars’, ‘idxs’}, default ‘default’*) – The index to use for the rows in the returned DataFrame. If
          `'default'`, use a default pandas integer RangeIndex. If
          `'ivars'`, use the Block’s independent variables (ivars)
          to create a pandas Index or MultiIndex. If `'idxs'`, use
          the index variables to create a pandas Index or MultiIndex.
          If `'ivars'` or `'idxs'` is the chosen option but no
          ivars or idxs exist, a default integer RangeIndex is used.
        * **cols\_nlevels** (*int, default 0*) – The number of levels to use for MultiIndex cols. If `-1`,
          let the max number of dims in the Vars determine the number of
          levels. If `0`, use a flat Index for the cols (instead of
          MultiIndex). If `1`, use a MultiIndex with 1 level. If the
          value is greater than max ndim + 1, fill the extra levels with
          empty strings.
        * **cols\_level\_names** (*bool, default True*) – If `True` set the names of the levels in the returned
          MultiIndex to `'varname'`, `'i'`, `'j'`, etc. Ignored if
          the return cols Index is flat.
        * **cols\_delim** (*3-tuple of str, default (‘[’, ‘,’, ‘]’)*) – Only applies to multi-dimensional variables. A 3-tuple of str
          characters used for embedding dimscales into the varnames in a
          flat string columns index. The first character is the opening
          delimiter, the second is the delimiter between dimscales, and
          the third is the closing delimiter. For example, the default
          delimiters would create varnames like `'S[2,1]'` and
          `'PortZ[1]'`.
        * **multidim\_pref** (*{‘dims’, ‘default\_ints’}, default ‘dims’*) – Only applies to multi-dimensional variables. If `'dims'`,
          use dimscales set by dims\_pref. If `'default_ints'`, use
          the integers set by default\_ints.
        * **dims\_pref** (*{None, ‘nums’, ‘names’}, optional*) – Only applies to multi-dimensional variables. The type of
          dimension scales preferred for the DataFrame columns if the
          Vars’ dims are used. If `'nums'`, use the numeric
          dimscales. If `'names'`, use the string dimscales.
          If `None` (the default), prefer numeric dimscales if they
          exist, otherwise use string dimscales. In all cases, if the
          dimscales are not present, use the integers set by
          default\_ints.
        * **default\_ints** (*{1, 0}, default 1*) – Only applies to multi-dimensional variables. The integer-based
          indexing to use for the columns in the event that any Var’s
          dimscales are not used. If `1`, use 1-based ints for the
          index labels. If `0`, use 0-based ints.

    Returns:
    :   A pandas DataFrame with the Block’s data.

    Return type:
    :   pandas.DataFrame

    Notes

    The dtypes in the DataFrame are automatically set based on the
    dtypes of the data in the Block, taking into account whether Vars
    have nulls or not.

    Examples

    Let’s create a sample Block and convert it to a pandas DataFrame
    using the default settings. Here, we create a Block with one idx
    variable, two ivars, and three dvars. Two of the dvars are
    multi-dimensional: the `PortR` variable has two dimensions, and
    the `magS` variable has three dimensions.

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> import pandas as pd
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Block
    >>> pd.options.display.width = 200  # all DataFrame printouts use this width
    >>> data = {
    ...     "i": [100, 101, 102, 103],
    ...     "sample": [1, 2, 1, 2],
    ...     "bias": [4.1, 4.1, 5.2, 5.2],
    ...     "Pout": [30.5, 31.2, 29.4, 30.6],
    ...     "PortR": np.random.randint(30, 60, (4, 2)),
    ...     "magS": np.random.random((4, 2, 2)),
    ... }
    >>> block = Block(data, ivarnames=["sample", "bias"], idxnames=["i"])
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe()
    >>> print(df)
         i  sample  bias  Pout  PortR[1]  PortR[2]  magS[1,1]  magS[1,2]  magS[2,1]  magS[2,2]
    0  100       1   4.1  30.5        58        50   0.260715   0.646049   0.339258   0.248542
    1  101       2   4.1  31.2        53        48   0.898790   0.525265   0.895914   0.743558
    2  102       1   5.2  29.4        54        45   0.630773   0.832599   0.929879   0.238047
    3  103       2   5.2  30.6        46        54   0.226296   0.456732   0.961668   0.068220
    ```

    Each of the 1D variables are able to fit in one column, but the
    multi-dimensional variables are split into multiple columns. The
    columns for the multi-dimensional variables are created by
    embedding default integers into the varnames. The DataFrame’s
    index is a default RangeIndex.

    Let’s create a new DataFrame with a non-default index. First, let’s use
    the Block’s ivars. This creates a MultiIndex from the Block’s independent
    variables since there are two ivars.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(index="ivars")
    >>> print(df)
                   i  Pout  PortR[1]  PortR[2]  magS[1,1]  magS[1,2]  magS[2,1]  magS[2,2]
    sample bias
    1      4.1   100  30.5        58        50   0.260715   0.646049   0.339258   0.248542
    2      4.1   101  31.2        53        48   0.898790   0.525265   0.895914   0.743558
    1      5.2   102  29.4        54        45   0.630773   0.832599   0.929879   0.238047
    2      5.2   103  30.6        46        54   0.226296   0.456732   0.961668   0.068220
    ```

    The other index option is to use the Block’s idxs. This creates a
    regular Index (not a MultiIndex) from the Block’s index variables,
    since there is only one idx variable.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(index="idxs")
    >>> print(df)
         sample  bias  Pout  PortR[1]  PortR[2]  magS[1,1]  magS[1,2]  magS[2,1]  magS[2,2]
    i
    100       1   4.1  30.5        58        50   0.260715   0.646049   0.339258   0.248542
    101       2   4.1  31.2        53        48   0.898790   0.525265   0.895914   0.743558
    102       1   5.2  29.4        54        45   0.630773   0.832599   0.929879   0.238047
    103       2   5.2  30.6        46        54   0.226296   0.456732   0.961668   0.068220
    ```

    Now, let’s explore the options for the columns. In the absence
    of dimensional metadata for the multi-dimensional variables,
    the default integers are used for the column labels. There
    are two options for the default integers: 0-based and 1-based.
    Let’s use 0-based integers.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(default_ints=0)
    >>> print(df)
         i  sample  bias  Pout  PortR[0]  PortR[1]  magS[0,0]  magS[0,1]  magS[1,0]  magS[1,1]
    0  100       1   4.1  30.5        58        50   0.260715   0.646049   0.339258   0.248542
    1  101       2   4.1  31.2        53        48   0.898790   0.525265   0.895914   0.743558
    2  102       1   5.2  29.4        54        45   0.630773   0.832599   0.929879   0.238047
    3  103       2   5.2  30.6        46        54   0.226296   0.456732   0.961668   0.068220
    ```

    By default `pwdatatools` uses square brackets and commas
    as the delimiters for embedding the dimension scales into the
    column labels for multi-dimensional variables. Let’s change
    the delimiters to parentheses and semicolons.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_delim=('(', ';', ')'))
    >>> print(df)
         i  sample  bias  Pout  PortR(1)  PortR(2)  magS(1;1)  magS(1;2)  magS(2;1)  magS(2;2)
    0  100       1   4.1  30.5        58        50   0.260715   0.646049   0.339258   0.248542
    1  101       2   4.1  31.2        53        48   0.898790   0.525265   0.895914   0.743558
    2  102       1   5.2  29.4        54        45   0.630773   0.832599   0.929879   0.238047
    3  103       2   5.2  30.6        46        54   0.226296   0.456732   0.961668   0.068220
    ```

    Now, let’s add dimensional metadata to our multi-dimensional
    variables. This is done assigning an instance of [`Dims`](../../../metadata/dims/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims")
    to the [`Var.dims`](../../../main/var/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dims.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dims") attribute. Each instance of [`Dims`](../../../metadata/dims/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims")
    may define numeric (“nums”) and/or string (“names”) dimension
    scales.

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Dims
    >>> block["PortR"].dims = Dims(ndim=2, i_nums=[5, 6], i_names=["Port5", "Port6"])
    >>> block["magS"].dims = Dims(
    ...     ndim=3,
    ...     i_nums=[3, 4],
    ...     i_names=["P3", "P4"],
    ...     j_nums=[1, 2],
    ...     j_names=["P1", "P2"],
    ... )
    ```

    Now, let’s create a pandas DataFarme using the default settings.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe()
    >>> print(df)
         i  sample  bias  Pout  PortR[5]  PortR[6]  magS[3,1]  magS[3,2]  magS[4,1]  magS[4,2]
    0  100       1   4.1  30.5        58        50   0.260715   0.646049   0.339258   0.248542
    1  101       2   4.1  31.2        53        48   0.898790   0.525265   0.895914   0.743558
    2  102       1   5.2  29.4        54        45   0.630773   0.832599   0.929879   0.238047
    3  103       2   5.2  30.6        46        54   0.226296   0.456732   0.961668   0.068220
    ```

    As you can see, the column labels now use the numeric dimension
    scales. We can optionally use the string dimension scales instead.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(dims_pref="names")
    >>> print(df)
         i  sample  bias  Pout  PortR['Port5']  PortR['Port6']  magS['P3','P1']  magS['P3','P2']  magS['P4','P1']  magS['P4','P2']
    0  100       1   4.1  30.5              58              50         0.260715         0.646049         0.339258         0.248542
    1  101       2   4.1  31.2              53              48         0.898790         0.525265         0.895914         0.743558
    2  102       1   5.2  29.4              54              45         0.630773         0.832599         0.929879         0.238047
    3  103       2   5.2  30.6              46              54         0.226296         0.456732         0.961668         0.068220
    ```

    We can choose to use the default integers for the column labels
    instead of the dimension scales.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(multidim_pref="default_ints")
    >>> print(df)
         i  sample  bias  Pout  PortR[1]  PortR[2]  magS[1,1]  magS[1,2]  magS[2,1]  magS[2,2]
    0  100       1   4.1  30.5        58        50   0.260715   0.646049   0.339258   0.248542
    1  101       2   4.1  31.2        53        48   0.898790   0.525265   0.895914   0.743558
    2  102       1   5.2  29.4        54        45   0.630773   0.832599   0.929879   0.238047
    3  103       2   5.2  30.6        46        54   0.226296   0.456732   0.961668   0.068220
    ```

    Instead of creating flat columns, we can create a MultiIndex.
    if we use `cols_nlevels=-1`, the columns will have as many
    levels as the maximum number of dimensions in the Vars.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_nlevels=-1)
    >>> print(df)
    varname    i sample bias  Pout PortR          magS
    i                                  5   6         3                   4
    j                                                1         2         1         2
    0        100      1  4.1  30.5    58  50  0.260715  0.646049  0.339258  0.248542
    1        101      2  4.1  31.2    53  48  0.898790  0.525265  0.895914  0.743558
    2        102      1  5.2  29.4    54  45  0.630773  0.832599  0.929879  0.238047
    3        103      2  5.2  30.6    46  54  0.226296  0.456732  0.961668  0.068220
    ```

    We can optionally turn off the level names in the columns if we
    are using a MultiIndex.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_nlevels=-1, cols_level_names=False)
    >>> print(df)
         i sample bias  Pout PortR          magS
                                 5   6         3                   4
                                               1         2         1         2
    0  100      1  4.1  30.5    58  50  0.260715  0.646049  0.339258  0.248542
    1  101      2  4.1  31.2    53  48  0.898790  0.525265  0.895914  0.743558
    2  102      1  5.2  29.4    54  45  0.630773  0.832599  0.929879  0.238047
    3  103      2  5.2  30.6    46  54  0.226296  0.456732  0.961668  0.068220
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.tricontourplot.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.tricontourplot[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-tricontourplot "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.tricontourplot(*z: str*, *\**, *ax=None*, *sca: bool = False*, *levels: int | ArrayLike | None = None*, *add\_xlabel: bool = True*, *add\_ylabel: bool = True*, *xlabel: str | None = None*, *ylabel: str | None = None*, *\*\*kwargs*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.tricontourplot "Link to this definition")
:   Plot contour lines on a matplotlib Axes using triangulation.

    This method is similar to `contourplot` except that this method
    uses triangulation instead of interpolating onto a regular grid.
    Therefore, it can directly support irregular scattered data.

    A matplotlib TriContourSet object is returned, which can be used
    to further customize the plot. If you need access to the
    underlying Axes object, use the TriContourSet’s `axes`
    attribute.

    LoadPullBlocks with a power ivar or outer ivars are not supported.
    If you want to plot contours for a LoadPullBlock with a power
    ivar, you must first get rid of the power dependnecy by selecting
    a subset of the data. There are several ways of doing this,
    including `LoadPullBlock.loc` and the following
    LoadPullBlock methods: [`at_power()`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_power.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_power "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_power"), [`at_gcomp()`](keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_gcomp.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_gcomp "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.at_gcomp"), and
    `xs()`.

    The same limitation is true of LoadPullBlocks with outer ivar(s),
    such as frequency, bias, etc. You must select a subset of the
    data before plotting.

    Parameters:
    :   * **z** – Name of the z variable to plot. The x and y variables are
          always the real and imaginary parts of the gamma ivar.
        * **ax** – The matplotlib Axes to plot on. If `None`, use the
          current axes. Defaults to `None`.
        * **sca** – A bool that determines whether to set the Axes used for plotting
          as the current Axes. Invokes `matplotlib.pyplot.sca()`.
          Defaults to `False`.
        * **levels** (*int or array-like, optional*) – Determines the number and positions of the contour lines or
          regions. If an int, use matplotlib’s MaxNLocator, which tries to
          automatically choose no more than n+1 “nice” contour levels
          between minimum and maximum numeric values of Z. If array-like,
          draw contour lines at the specified levels. The values must be in
          increasing order.
        * **add\_xlabel** – If `True`, add an x label to the Axes. Defaults to `True`.
        * **add\_ylabel** – If `True`, add a y label to the Axes. Defaults to `True`.
        * **xlabel** – If `add_xlabel` is `True`, use this as the x label. If
          `None`, auto-create the label. Auto-creation tries to use the
          name of the x variable. If name of the variable is unknown, use
          the default of label, which is `'x'`. Defaults to `None`.
        * **ylabel** – If `add_ylabel` is `True`, use this as the y label. If
          `None`, auto-create the label. Auto-creation tries to use the
          name of the y variable. If name of the variable is unknown, use
          the default of label, which is `'y'`. Defaults to `None`.
        * **\*\*kwargs** – Additional keyword arguments are passed on to
          `matplotlib.pyplot.tricontour`. See the matplotlib documentation
          for more info.

    Return type:
    :   matplotlib.contour.TriContourSet

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.update[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-update "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.update(*other=None*, *\*\*kwargs*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.update "Link to this definition")

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.with_idxs.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.with\_idxs[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-with-idxs "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.with\_idxs(*data\_varnames: str | Iterable[str] | None = None*, *idxnames: str | Iterable[str] | None = None*, *\**, *tol: float | Mapping[str, float] = nan*, *existing: Literal['raise', 'replace'] = 'raise'*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.with_idxs "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new Block with added integer index variables

    The index variables are created by iterating through the combinations
    of values of the data variable(s).

    Parameters:
    :   * **data\_varnames** (*str or iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the data variables from which to create index
          variables. If None, use the ivars.
        * **idxnames** (*str or iterable of str, optional*) – The names to use for the new index variables. If None, the
          names are created from the data\_varnames by prepending ‘i’.
        * **tol** (*float or dict-like, default NaN*) – The absolute tolerance(s) for determining unique values in the
          data variables. If a float, the same tolerance is used for all
          data variables. If a dict-like, the keys are the data variable
          names and the values are the tolerances for those variables.
          If NaN, do not use a tolerance and instead use exact
          value matches.
        * **existing** (*{‘raise’, ‘replace’}, default ‘raise’*) – What to do if the index variable names already exist in the
          Block. If ‘raise’, raise a ValueError. If ‘replace’, replace the
          existing index variables with new ones.

    Returns:
    :   A new Block with the new index variables.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](../../../main/block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> import keysight.pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> block = pwdt.Block(
    ...     {
    ...         "bias": [1.2, 1.2, 1.3, 1.3],
    ...         "freq": [1e9, 2e9, 1e9, 2e9],
    ...         "z": [1, 2, 3, 4],
    ...     }
    ... )
    >>> block_idxs = block.with_idxs(["bias", "freq"])
    >>> block_idxs.idxnames
    ('ibias', 'ifreq')
    ```

    Let’s view the the new Block’s data as a pandas DataFrame to help
    visualize the new idx variables. We use the idx variables as the
    DataFrame’s rows index.

    ```
    >>> df = block_idxs.to_pandas_dataframe(index='idxs')
    >>> print(df)
                 bias          freq  z
    ibias ifreq
    0     0       1.2  1.000000e+09  1
          1       1.2  2.000000e+09  2
    1     0       1.3  1.000000e+09  3
          1       1.3  2.000000e+09  4
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.z_ivar.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.z\_ivar[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-z-ivar "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.z\_ivar(*form: Literal['complex', 'real', 'imag', 'mag', 'angle', 'polar\_str', 'rect\_str'] = 'complex'*, *\*\*kwargs*) → [Var](../../../main/var/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.z_ivar "Link to this definition")
:   Return the impedance independent variable (ivar) in the form specified.

    If an impedance is not present in the ivars, a ValueError is raised.
    A variable is considered to be a Z if its main role is “impedance”
    in the LoadPullBlock’s metadata.

    Parameters:
    :   **form** – The form of the Z ivar to return. Must be one of
        `"complex"`, `"real"`, `"imag"`, `"mag"`, `"angle"`,
        `"polar_str"`, or `"rect_str"`. Defaults to `"complex"`.

    Other Parameters:
    :   * **enable\_prefix** (*bool*) – If `True`, the returned Var will have a name that is
          prefixed with the real\_prefix, imag\_prefix, mag\_prefix,
          or angle\_prefix, depending on the value of `form`. If
          `False`, the returned Var will have a name that is
          not prefixed. The default is `True`.
        * **enable\_suffix** (*bool*) – If `True`, the returned Var will have a name that is
          suffixed with the real\_suffix, imag\_suffix, mag\_suffix,
          or angle\_suffix, depending on the value of `form`. If
          `False`, the returned Var will have a name that is
          not suffixed. The default is `True`.
        * **real\_prefix** (*str*) – A prefix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="real"`. The default is `calc.REAL_PREFIX_DEFAULT`.
        * **real\_suffix** (*str*) – A suffix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="real"`. The default is `""`.
        * **imag\_prefix** (*str*) – A prefix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="imag"`. The default is `calc.IMAG_PREFIX_DEFAULT`.
        * **imag\_suffix** (*str*) – A suffix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="imag"`. The default is `""`.
        * **mag\_prefix** (*str*) – A prefix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="mag"`. The default is `calc.MAG_PREFIX_DEFAULT`.
        * **mag\_suffix** (*str*) – A suffix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="mag"`. The default is `""`.
        * **angle\_prefix** (*str*) – A prefix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="angle"`. The default is `calc.ANGLE_PREFIX_DEFAULT`.
        * **angle\_suffix** (*str*) – A suffix to add to the name of the returned Var if
          `form="angle"`. The default is `""`.
        * **angle\_deg** (*bool*) – If `True`, the angle values are returned in degrees.
          If `False`, the angle are returned in radians.The default
          is `True`.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](../../../main/var/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> lpblock = LoadPullBlock(...)
    >>> real_z_ivar = lpblock.z_ivar("real")
    >>> imag_z_ivar = lpblock.z_ivar("imag")
    >>> # Get rectangular string representation of Z ivar,
    >>> # which is ideal for plotting labels:
    >>> rect_str_z_ivar = lpblock.z_ivar("rect_str")
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.z_to_gamma.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.z\_to\_gamma[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-z-to-gamma "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.z\_to\_gamma(*gamma\_name: str*) → [LoadPullBlock](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.z_to_gamma "Link to this definition")
:   Return LoadPullBlock with gamma sweep, converted from impedance sweep.

        column in the DataFrame.

    Examples

    ```
    >>> lpblock = LoadPullBlock(...)
    >>> lpblock_g = lpblock.z_to_gamma("GammaLoad")
    ```

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.zrefload.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.zrefload[](#keysight-pwdatatools-loadpullblock-zrefload "Link to this heading")

LoadPullBlock.zrefload(*suffix: str | None = None*) → [Var](../../../main/var/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.zrefload "Link to this definition")
:   Return a variable with the role “impedance.load.reference”.

    If there is only one zrefload variable, the `suffix` parameter
    is not needed. If there are multiple zrefload variables, the
    `suffix` parameter is required to select the desired one.
    This assumes that zrefload variables can be distinguished by
    suffixes on their names.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](../../../main/var/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** –

        * If no zrefload variable is found.
          - If multiple zrefload variables are found and suffix is not provided.
          - If suffix is provided but doesn’t match the suffix of any zrefload
          variable’s name.

    Examples

    Many times, a LoadPullBlock will contain only one zrefload variable.
    In this case, the suffix parameter is not needed:

    ```
    >>> lpblock = LoadPullBlock(...)
    >>> zrefload_var = lpblock.zrefload()
    ```

    If there are multiple zrefload variables, the suffix parameter is
    required to select one. A common reason for having more than one zrefload
    variable is that zrefload varies vs. frequency. It is typical to use
    enumerated frequency suffixes, such as “\_F1”, “\_F2”, “\_F3”, etc. So the
    zrefload variable names might be “ZrefLoad\_F1”, “ZrefLoad\_F2”,
    “ZrefLoad\_F3”, etc.

    ```
    >>> lpblock = LoadPullBlock(...)
    >>> zrefload_var = lpblock.zrefload(suffix="F1") # this works
    >>> zrefload_var = lpblock.zrefload(suffix="_F1") # this works too
    >>> zrefload_var = lpblock.zrefload(suffix="1") # this also works
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/loadpull/loadpullblock/index.md === -->

# LoadPullBlock[](#loadpullblock "Link to this heading")

This page documents public attributes and methods available in the LoadPullBlock class. For more information and examples, see [Load Pull Examples](../../../examples/loadpull/index.md#load-pull-examples).

*class* LoadPullBlock(*variables: Variables = None*, *\**, *name: str | None = None*, *gamma\_ivarname: str | None = None*, *gamma\_idxname: str | None = None*, *z\_ivarname: str | None = None*, *z\_idxname: str | None = None*, *power\_ivarname: str | None = None*, *power\_idxname: str | None = None*, *outer\_ivarnames: Iterable[str] | None = None*, *outer\_idxnames: Iterable[str] | None = None*, *exprs: ExprsDictLike | None = None*, *attrs: AttrsDictLike | None = None*, *copy: bool = False*, *\*\*kwargs*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "Link to this definition")
:   An object to store and manipulate load pull data.

    Parameters:
    :   * **variables** (*DataFrame, sequence of Var, str to VarLike mapping, or None*) – Several types of inputs are supported: a pandas DataFrame, a
          mapping of str variable name to ArrayLikes or Vars, or a sequence
          of Vars. Examples of ArrayLike objects are numpy ndarrays and
          MaskedArrays, Python lists, and pandas Series. If a mapping or
          sequence is provided, all data objects must have the same length
          for axis 0.
        * **name** (*str, optional*) – A str name for the LoadPullBlock. If not specified, the name is set
          to an empty string.
        * **gamma\_ivarname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the gamma independent variable. A LoadPullBlock
          must have either a gamma ivar or a z ivar, but not both.
        * **gamma\_idxname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the gamma indepedent variable’s index.
        * **z\_ivarname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the impedance independent variable. A LoadPullBlock
          must have either a gamma ivar or a z ivar, but not both.
        * **z\_idxname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the impedance indepedent variable’s index.
        * **power\_ivarname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the power independent variable.
        * **power\_idxname** (*str, optional*) – The name of the power indepedent variable’s index.
        * **outer\_ivarnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the outer independent variables. Outer ivars can be
          any arbitrary swept variables, such as frequency, bias, etc.
        * **outer\_idxnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the outer indepedent variable’s indexes.
        * **exprs** (*ExprsDictLike, optional*) – A mapping of str expression name to Expr objects. The Expr objects
          represent expressions that can be applied to variables or other
          expressions.
        * **attrs** (*dict-like, optional*) – A dict or dict-like object of arbitrary Block-level metadata. The
          keys must be strings and the values must be one of the following:
          str, int, float, complex, bool, None, dict, list, or
          numpy.ndarray. Nested dicts and lists are allowed.

    Other Parameters:
    :   * **data** (*DataFrame, str to ArrayLike mapping, or None*) – A keyword-only argument that exists mostly for backwards
          compatibility. It is recommended to use variables instead of this
          keyword-only argument. You cannot specify values for both data
          and variables.
        * **idxnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the indexes. This is another way to define the
          names of the gamma, z, power, and outer indexes. To use this
          argument, you must also specify ivarnames and variables
          input must contain Vars with the proper gamma/z/power roles.
          Defaults to `None`.
        * **ivarnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the independent variables. This is another way
          to define the names of the gamma, z, power, and outer
          independent variables. To use this argument, variables
          input must contain Vars with the proper gamma/z/power roles.
          Defaults to `None`.

    Notes

    If an ivarname is provided and its associated idxname is not
    provided, then its idxname is assumed to be “i” + “ivarname”.
    For example, if gamma\_ivarname is “GammaLoad” and gamma\_idxname
    is not provided, then gamma\_idxname is assumed to be “iGammaLoad”.

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    ```

    Instantiate a LoadPullBlock with a gamma ivar, using the default
    naming pattern for the gamma idxname:

    ```
    >>> data = {
    ...     'GammaLoad': [0.5 + 0.4j, 0.4 + 0.1j, 0.3 + 0.2j],
    ...     'iGammaLoad': [0, 1, 2],
    ...     'Gain': [10, 11, 12]
    ... }
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(data, gamma_ivarname='GammaLoad')
    >>> lpblock.gamma_ivarname
    'GammaLoad'
    >>> lpblock.gamma_idxname
    'iGammaLoad'
    ```

    Since the default naming pattern was used for gamma\_idxname, we
    did not have to specify it during initialization.

    Instantiate a LoadPullBlock with a gamma ivar and a power ivar. Note
    that when gamma and power are swept, the power sweep is always the
    inner sweep.

    ```
    >>> data = {
    ...     'GammaLoad': [0.5 + 0.4j, 0.5 + 0.4j, 0.4 + 0.1j, 0.4 + 0.1j],
    ...     'iGammaLoad': [0, 0, 1, 1],
    ...     'PSource': [-10.0, -8.0, -10.0, -8.0],
    ...     'iPSource': [0, 1, 0, 1],
    ...     'Gain': [10, 11, 12, 10.5, 11.5]
    ... }
    >>> lpblock = pwdt.LoadPullBlock(
    ...     data,
    ...     gamma_ivarname='GammaLoad',
    ...     power_ivarname='PSource',
    ... )
    >>> lpblock.gamma_ivarname
    'GammaLoad'
    >>> lpblock.gamma_idxname
    'iGammaLoad'
    >>> lpblock.power_ivarname
    'PSource'
    >>> lpblock.power_idxname
    'iPSource'
    ```

    Since we did not specify the power\_idxname, it was assumed to be
    “iPSource”, which is the default naming pattern (“i” + “PSource”).

    See also

    The [`read_file_as_loadpullblock()`](../../fileio/read_file_as_loadpullblock.md#keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.read_file_as_loadpullblock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.read_file_as_loadpullblock")
    function is the easiest way to instantiate a LoadPullBlock from a
    file. For more detailed information and examples, see
    [Work with Load Pull Data](../../../howto/work_with_loadpull_data.md#work-with-load-pull-data).

| [`LoadPullBlock.exprs`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.exprs.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.exprs "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.exprs") |  |

| [`LoadPullBlock.outer_ivarnames`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.outer_ivarnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.outer_ivarnames "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.outer_ivarnames") | Return the names of the outer independent variables (ivars). |
| [`LoadPullBlock.power_idxname`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.power_idxname.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.power_idxname "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.power_idxname") | Return the name of the power index variable (idx var). |
| [`LoadPullBlock.power_ivarname`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.power_ivarname.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.power_ivarname "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.power_ivarname") | Return the name of the power independent variable (ivar). |
| [`LoadPullBlock.varnames`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.varnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.varnames "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.varnames") | Names of all variables (ivars, dvars, and idxs) as a tuple of str. |
| [`LoadPullBlock.z_idxname`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.z_idxname.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.z_idxname "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.z_idxname") | Return the name of the impedance index variable (idx var). |

# LoadPullSweep[](#loadpullsweep "Link to this heading")

This page gives an overview of public attributes and methods available in the LoadPullSweep class. The LoadPullSweep class is used extensively by the [`LoadPullBlock`](../loadpullblock/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock") class. For more information and examples, see [Load Pull Examples](../../../examples/loadpull/index.md#load-pull-examples).

*class* LoadPullSweep(*\**, *outer\_ivarnames: Sequence[str] | None = None*, *outer\_idxnames: Sequence[str] | None = None*, *gamma\_ivarname: str | None = None*, *gamma\_idxname: str | None = None*, *z\_ivarname: str | None = None*, *z\_idxname: str | None = None*, *power\_ivarname: str | None = None*, *power\_idxname: str | None = None*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep "Link to this definition")
:   An immutable object to store ivarnames and idxnames for a LoadPullBlock.

    Parameters:
    :   * **outer\_ivarnames** (*sequence of str, optional*) – Arbitrary names of the outer independent variables (ivars).
          Examples of outer swept variables are frequency, bias, and
          temperature. The outermost ivar should be first, and the
          innermost last. For example, if frequency is swept at each
          bias level, then `outer_ivarnames=["Bias", "Frequency"]`.
        * **outer\_idxnames** (*sequence of str, optional*) – Names of indexes for outer ivars. If `None`, then
          `outer_ivarnames` must also be `None`.
        * **gamma\_ivarname** (*str, optional*) – Name of swept gamma variable (ivar). If `None`, then gamma
          is not swept.
        * **gamma\_idxname** (*str, optional*) – Name of gamma index. If `None`, then `gamma` must also be
          `None`.
        * **z\_ivarname** (*str, optional*) – Name of swept impedance variable (ivar). If `None`, then
          impedance is not swept.
        * **z\_idxname** (*str, optional*) – Name of impedance index variable. If `None`, then z must
          also be `None`.
        * **power\_ivarname** (*str, optional*) – Name of swept power variable (ivar). If `None`, then power
          is not swept.
        * **power\_idxname** (*str, optional*) – Name of power index variable. If `None`, then power must
          also be `None`.

**Attributes**

| --- | --- |
| [`LoadPullSweep.idxnames`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.idxnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.idxnames "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.idxnames") | Names of the index variables (idxs) as a tuple of str. |
| [`LoadPullSweep.idxnames_map`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.idxnames_map.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.idxnames_map "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.idxnames_map") | A frozen mapping of varnames to idxnames. |
| [`LoadPullSweep.ivarnames`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.ivarnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.ivarnames "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.ivarnames") | Names of the independent variables (ivars) as a tuple of str. |
| [`LoadPullSweep.gamma_idxname`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.gamma_idxname.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.gamma_idxname "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.gamma_idxname") | Name of gamma index variable (str or None). |
| [`LoadPullSweep.gamma_ivarname`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.gamma_ivarname.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.gamma_ivarname "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.gamma_ivarname") | Name of gamma variable (str or None). |
| [`LoadPullSweep.gamma_or_z_idxname`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.gamma_or_z_idxname.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.gamma_or_z_idxname "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.gamma_or_z_idxname") | Return name of gamma or z index variable (str or None). |
| [`LoadPullSweep.gamma_or_z_ivarname`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.gamma_or_z_ivarname.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.gamma_or_z_ivarname "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.gamma_or_z_ivarname") | Return name of gamma or z indepedent variable (str or None). |
| [`LoadPullSweep.outer_idxnames`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.outer_idxnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.outer_idxnames "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.outer_idxnames") | Names of the outer index variables (outer idxs) as a tuple of str. |
| [`LoadPullSweep.outer_ivarnames`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.outer_ivarnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.outer_ivarnames "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.outer_ivarnames") | Names of the outer independent variables (outer ivars) as a tuple of str. |
| [`LoadPullSweep.power_idxname`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.power_idxname.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.power_idxname "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.power_idxname") | Name of power index variable (str or None). |
| [`LoadPullSweep.power_ivarname`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.power_ivarname.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.power_ivarname "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.power_ivarname") | Name of power variable (str or None). |
| [`LoadPullSweep.z_idxname`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.z_idxname.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.z_idxname "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.z_idxname") | Name of impedance index variable (str or None). |
| [`LoadPullSweep.z_ivarname`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.z_ivarname.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.z_ivarname "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullSweep.z_ivarname") | Name of impedance variable (str or None). |

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/block/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.__init__.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Block.\_\_init\_\_[](#keysight-pwdatatools-block-init "Link to this heading")

Block.\_\_init\_\_(*variables: Variables = None*, *\**, *name: str | None = None*, *ivarnames: Iterable[str] | None = None*, *idxnames: Iterable[str] | None = None*, *attrs: AttrsDictLike | None = None*, *exprs: ExprsDictLike | None = None*, *copy: bool = False*, *\*\*kwargs*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.__init__ "Link to this definition")
:   Initialize an instance of Block.

    Parameters:
    :   * **variables** (*DataFrame, sequence of Var, str to VarLike dict, optional*) – Several types of inputs are supported: a pandas DataFrame, a
          mapping of str variable name to ArrayLikes or Vars, or a sequence
          of Vars. Examples of ArrayLike objects are numpy ndarrays and
          MaskedArrays, Python lists, and pandas Series. If a mapping or
          sequence is provided, all data objects must have the same length
          for axis 0.
        * **name** (*str, optional*) – A str name for the Block. If not specified, the name is set to
          an empty string.
        * **ivarnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the independent variables (ivars).
        * **idxnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of variables that act as indexes along axis 0. Axis 0 is
          also known as the “shared” axis or dimension.
        * **attrs** (*dict-like, optional*) – A dict or dict-like object of arbitrary Block-level metadata. The
          keys must be strings and the values must be one of the following:
          str, int, float, complex, bool, None, dict, list, or
          numpy.ndarray. Nested dicts and lists are allowed.
        * **exprs** (*ExprsDictLike, optional*) – A mapping of str expression name to Expr objects. The Expr objects
          represent expressions that can be applied to variables or other
          expressions.
        * **copy** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, always copy the input variables. If `False`, do
          not copy the variables unless necessary.

    Other Parameters:
    :   * **data** (*DataFrame, str to ArrayLike mapping, or None*) – A keyword-only argument that exists mostly for backwards
          compatibility. It is recommended to use variables instead of this
          keyword-only argument. You cannot specify values for both data
          and variables.
        * **validate** (*bool, default True*) – An expert-level keyword-only argument that determines whether to
          validate the input data. Only set to `False` if you are certain
          that the input data is valid.

    Examples

    Let’s initialize a Block with a dict containing some data. The keys
    must be strings (these become variable names) and the values can be
    any array-like objects (these become variable data).

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> data = {'freq': np.array([1, 2, 3]), 'Vout': [1, 2, 3]}
    >>> block = pwdt.Block(data)
    >>> block
    Block(
        <'freq', 'Vout', ... with 3 observations>,
        name='',
        ivarnames=(),
        attrs={},
    )
    ```

    Initializing a Block with a pandas DataFrame is also supported. The
    below code results in a Block that is identical to the Block we
    created with a dict.

    ```
    >>> import pandas as pd
    >>> df = pd.DataFrame(data)
    >>> block = pwdt.Block(df)
    ```

    For more control when creating a Block from a pandas DataFrame, use
    [`Block.from_pandas_dataframe()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe"). It has additional parameters
    related to how the DataFrame is converted to a Block.

    So far, we’ve only intialized Blocks with data. However, a Block can
    be initialized with various metadata. Here we initialize the Block
    with a name, the names of the independent variables (ivarnames), the
    names of index variables (idxnames), variable-level metadata (using
    instances of [`Var`](../../var/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")), and some arbitrary Block-level metadata
    (as attrs).

    ```
    >>> freq = pwdt.Var(data=[1e9, 2e9, 3e9], name='freq', unit='Hz')
    >>> vout = pwdt.Var(data=[5, 6, 7], name='Vout', role='voltage', unit='V')
    >>> variables = {'ifreq': np.array([1, 2, 3]), 'freq': freq, 'Vout': vout}
    >>> attrs = {'temperature': 150, 'comments': 'This is measured data'}
    >>> block = pwdt.Block(
    ...     variables,
    ...     name='foo',
    ...     ivarnames=['freq'],
    ...     idxnames=['ifreq'],
    ...     attrs=attrs,
    ... )
    >>> print(block)
    Block(
        <'Vout', ... with 3 observations>,
        name='foo',
        ivarnames=('freq',),
        idxnames=('ifreq',),
        attrs={'temperature': ..., 'comments': ...},
    )
    ```

    See also

    [`Block.from_file()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_file.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_file "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_file"), [`Block.from_pandas_dataframe()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe")

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/block/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.__setitem__.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Block.\_\_setitem\_\_[](#keysight-pwdatatools-block-setitem "Link to this heading")

Block.\_\_setitem\_\_(*key: str*, *value: VarLike | Scalar*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.__setitem__ "Link to this definition")
:   Set a variable in the Block.

    Parameters:
    :   * **key** (*str*) – The name of the variable to add.
        * **value** (*Var, ArrayLike, or Scalar*) – If a [`Var`](../../var/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var"), its data is finalized (if needed) and its
          metadata is added as-is. If an ArrayLike or Scalar, it is
          assumed to be the data for a new Var. A Scalar is
          automatically converted to a 1D ndarray of length equal to the
          number of observations in the Block.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Notes

    If value is a Var and the key does not match the Var’s name, a
    copy of the Var is created with a name that matches the key.

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** –

        * If value is not a Var and the varname already exists. In
          that scenario, use the [`set_data_in_place()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data_in_place.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data_in_place "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data_in_place") method
          instead.
          - If a scalar value is set in a Block without any variables.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/block/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.attrs.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Block.attrs[](#keysight-pwdatatools-block-attrs "Link to this heading")

*property* Block.attrs*: [AttrsDict](../../../metadata/attrsdict/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict "keysight.pwdatatools._core.properties.AttrsDict")*[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.attrs "Link to this definition")
:   A dict of arbitrary attributes (metadata).

    The attributes are stored in an [`AttrsDict`](../../../metadata/attrsdict/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict "keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict"), which is a
    type-restricted dict. The keys must be str, and the values must
    be one of the following types: str, int, float, complex, bool,
    None, `numpy.ndarray`, dict, or list. Nested dicts and
    lists are also supported.

# keysight.pwdatatools.Block.copy[](#keysight-pwdatatools-block-copy "Link to this heading")

Block.copy() → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.copy "Link to this definition")
:   Return a copy of self (always a deep copy).

    Returns:
    :   A deep copy of self.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Notes

    Because the underlying data in each Var is immutable, only metadata
    needs to be copied, so this operation is relatively inexpensive.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/block/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.drop_observations.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Block.drop\_observations[](#keysight-pwdatatools-block-drop-observations "Link to this heading")

Block.drop\_observations(*condition: ArrayLike*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.drop_observations "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new filtered Block without the specified observations.

    Parameters:
    :   **condition** (*1D bool array-like*) – A 1D boolean array-like with the same length as the Block’s
        axis 0, with True values at the indexes of the observations
        to drop.

    Returns:
    :   A new Block with the observations dropped where condition
        is True.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> block = pwdt.Block(data={'Vout': [1, 2, 3], 'Vin': [4, 5, 6]})
    >>> vout = block['Vout']
    >>> vin = block['Vin']
    >>> block.drop_observations(vout > 2)
    <Block with 2 variables and 1 observation>
    >>> block.drop_observations((vout == 1) & (vin >= 4))
    <Block with 2 variables and 1 observation>
    >>> block.drop_observations(vout == 1 | vin >= 4)
    <Block with 2 variables and 1 observation>
    ```


---

# keysight.pwdatatools.Block.drop\_vars[](#keysight-pwdatatools-block-drop-vars "Link to this heading")

Block.drop\_vars(*varnames: Iterable[str]*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.drop_vars "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new Block with the specified variables dropped.

    All kept Vars are deepcopied.

    Parameters:
    :   **varnames** – The names of the variables to drop.

    Returns:
    :   A new Block with the specified variables dropped.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Raises:
    :   **KeyError** – If any of the variable names in varnames are not in the
        Block.

# keysight.pwdatatools.Block.fill\_null[](#keysight-pwdatatools-block-fill-null "Link to this heading")

Block.fill\_null(*values: [FillValues](../../../public_submodules/datatypes/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.datatypes.FillValues.md#keysight.pwdatatools.datatypes.FillValues "keysight.pwdatatools.datatypes.FillValues") | None = None*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.fill_null "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new Block with null values filled for all variables.

    Parameters:
    :   **values** (*FillValues, optional*) – An optional instance of `FillValues`. If not provided,
        the defult fill values are used.

    Notes

    Here are the default fill values for each dtype:

    * float: NaN
    * complex: NaN + 1j \* NaN
    * integer: 0
    * boolean: False
    * string: ‘’

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/block/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_file.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from\_file[](#keysight-pwdatatools-block-from-file "Link to this heading")

*classmethod* Block.from\_file(*src: str | os.PathLike | BaseDataFile*, *loc: str = '/'*, *\**, *data: bool = True*, *format\_specific: FormatSpecificReadOptions | None = None*, *idxs: IdxsOption | None = None*, *idxs\_tol: float | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*, *src\_format: str | None = None*, *varnames: Mapping[str, str] | None = None*) → [Block](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_file "Link to this definition")
:   Read a datafile and return a new instance of Block.

    If the source file src has a known file extension that maps
    to a supported format, then the format can be automatically
    inferred.

    If the source file has a missing or unknown file extension, you
    must either 1) input a [`DataFile`](../../../fileio/datafile/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile") with a `format_override`
    as `src`, or 2) use the `src_format` parameter. Known readable
    file extensions are stored in `options.reading.extentions`.
    Known readable file formats are stored in
    `options.reading.formats`.

    Parameters:
    :   * **src** (*str, os.PathLike, or DataFile*) – The source file path.
        * **loc** (*str, default ‘/’*) – The location within the source file to return as a Block. If
          `'/'` (the “root”), the entire source file is read.
          Otherwise, it represents the name or position of a Block in
          the source file (for example, `'/0'` or `'/SP`).
        * **data** (*bool, default True*) – A bool that determines whether to read the data from `src`.
          If `False`, only the metadata is read. Reading only metadata
          can be useful for quickly examining information about the
          variables.
        * **format\_specific** (*FormatSpecificReadOptions, optional*) – Defines the format-specific options for reading `src`. By
          default, the global option
          `options.reading.format_specific` sets format-specific
          reading options, but this parameter can override the global
          options. If provided, it must be an instance of
          `FormatSpecificReadOptions`. See the docs for a list of
          all classes that can act as format-specific read options. Some
          examples are `ADSReadOptions`,
          `LoadPullReadOptions`, and `LoadPullDerivedVars`.
        * **idxs** (*{‘auto’, True, False}, optional*) – If `True`, create index variables (idxs) when reading the
          source file. If `False`, do not create them. If `'auto'`,
          use the format-specific defaults. If `None`, use the global
          option `options.reading.idxs`.
        * **idxs\_tol** (*float, optional*) – The tolerance to use when generating index variables from float
          or complex data. If NaN, no tolerance is used and indexes are
          created by using exact equality. If provided, it takes precedence
          over the global option `options.reading.idxs_tol`.
        * **iffs** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing.
        * **src\_format** (*str, optional*) – Sets the source file’s format. Supported types are stored in
          `options.reading.formats`. Any value other
          than `None` overrides the format inferred from or set by
          `src`.
        * **varnames** (*Mapping[str, str], optional*) – A mapping of variable roles to their names. Defaults to
          `options.reading.varnames`.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If data cannot be represented as a single Block.

    Examples

    The below examples assume the following imports:

    ```
    >>> from pathlib import Path
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    ```

    If the source file `src` has a known file extension, and you want
    to read the entire file, you can simply input `src` as a str,
    `pathlib.Path`, or `DataFile`.
    Known file extensions are stored in `options.reading.extentions`.

    ```
    >>> # Using a str path with known ext (.lpd)
    >>> b1 = pwdt.Block.from_file('/home/device.lpd')
    >>> # Using a pathlib.Path with known ext (.spl)
    >>> b2 = pwdt.Block.from_file(Path('/home/device.spl'))
    >>> # Using a DataFile with a known ext (.mdm)
    >>> b3 = pwdt.Block.from_file(pwdt.DataFile('/home/FNoise.mdm'))
    ```

    If `src` has a missing or unknown file extension, you can use
    the `src_format` parameter. Alternatively, you can input an
    instance of `DataFile` as
    the `src` argument and use DataFile’s `format_override`
    parameter to define the source’s format.

    ```
    >>> # Using the ``src_format`` parameter since the file extension is missing
    >>> b1 = pwdt.Block.from_file('/data/noise', src_format='mdif')
    >>> # Using ``src_format`` since the file extension is non-standard
    >>> b2 = pwdt.Block.from_file('/data/noise.txt', src_format='mdif')
    >>> # Using a DataFile with ``format_override`` is another option
    >>> datafile = pwdt.DataFile('/data/noise', format_override='mdif')
    >>> b3 = pwdt.Block.from_file(datafile)
    ```

    If you want to perform a partial read, use the `loc` parameter.
    You have to know either the name or the position of the part that
    you want to read from `src`. A Block is always returned. If the
    contents of the part cannot be represented by a single Block, a
    ValueError is raised.

    ```
    >>> # Read a Block by name
    >>> b1 = pwdt.Block.from_file('/home/s_param.ds', loc='/SP')
    >>> # Read a Block by position
    >>> b2 = pwdt.Block.from_file('/home/ac_dc_meas.pwdt', loc='/0')
    ```

    See also

    [`read_file_as_block()`](../../../fileio/read_file_as_block.md#keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.read_file_as_block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.read_file_as_block")
    [`read_file()`](../../../fileio/read_file.md#keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.read_file "keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.read_file")
    `Group.from_file()`


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/block/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from\_pandas\_dataframe[](#keysight-pwdatatools-block-from-pandas-dataframe "Link to this heading")

*classmethod* Block.from\_pandas\_dataframe(*dataframe: pd.DataFrame*, *\**, *name: str = ''*, *idxnames: Iterable[str] | None = None*, *ivarnames: Iterable[str] | None = None*, *combine\_multidim: bool = True*, *dimscales\_delim: Tuple[str, str, str] = ('[', ',', ']')*, *attrs: AttrsDictLike | None = None*) → [Block](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe "Link to this definition")
:   Create a Block from a pandas DataFrame and optional metadata.

    The Block’s dims are derived from the DataFrame’s columns.

    Parameters:
    :   * **dataframe** (*pandas.DataFrame*) – The Block’s variables are constructed from the DataFrame’s columns
          and named row indexes (if present).
        * **name** (*str, default ‘’*) – A str name for the Block.
        * **idxnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the index variables (idxs) in the Block.
        * **ivarnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the independent variables (ivars) in the Block.
        * **combine\_multidim** (*bool, default True*) – If `True`, combine multidimensional variables into a single
          variable. If `False`, keep multidimensional variables as
          separate variables. Defaults to `True`.
        * **dimscales\_delim** (*3-tuple of str, default (‘[’, ‘,’, ‘]’)*) – The characters used to separate the dimension scales in the
          column names of the DataFrame. The first character is the
          opening delimiter, the second is the delimiter between
          dimension scales, and the third is the closing delimiter.
          For example, the default delimiters would interpret columns
          like `'S[2,1]'` and `'PortZ[1]'` as variables `'S'` and
          `'PortZ'` with dimension scales of (2,1) and (1),
          respectively. Defaults to `('[', ',', ']')`.
        * **attrs** (*dict-like, optional*) – Arbitrary Block-level metadata.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/block/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.get.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Block.get[](#keysight-pwdatatools-block-get "Link to this heading")

Block.get(*varname: str*, *default=None*) → [Var](../../var/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var") | None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.get "Link to this definition")
:   Return a variable by name, or None if the variable does not exist.

    Similar to [`__getitem__()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.__getitem__.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.__getitem__ "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.__getitem__") and [`get_var()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.get_var.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.get_var "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.get_var") methods, except
    that this method returns None if the varname is not found, while
    the other two methods raise a KeyError.

    Parameters:
    :   * **varname** – The name of the variable to retrieve.
        * **default** – The value to return if the variable is not found. Only
          None is supported.

    Return type:
    :   *Optional*[[*Var*](../../var/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")]


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/block/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.info.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Block.info[](#keysight-pwdatatools-block-info "Link to this heading")

Block.info(*varnames: str | Iterable[str] | None = None*) → DataFrame[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.info "Link to this definition")
:   Return a DataFrame containing information about the specified variable(s).

    The information on each variables includes the kind (ivar, dvar, or
    idx), role, dtype, min, max, NaN count, and masked value count.

    For complex variables, the min and max values are the minimum and
    maximum magnitudes. For string, boolean and other non-numeric types,
    the min and max values are not defined.

    Parameters:
    :   **varname** (*str or iterable of str, optional*) – Name(s) of a variable(s) on which to gather info. If `None`,
        return info for all variables. If `varnames` is a str,
        return a DataFrame with that variable’s info. If `varnames`
        is an iterable of str, return a DataFrame containing all of
        those variables’ info. Defaults to `None`.

    Return type:
    :   pandas.DataFrame


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/block/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.iter_sections.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Block.iter\_sections[](#keysight-pwdatatools-block-iter-sections "Link to this heading")

Block.iter\_sections(*varnames: str | Iterable[str] | None = None*, *\**, *auto\_pref: Literal['idxs', 'ivars'] | None = None*, *tol: float | Mapping[str, float] = nan*) → Iterator[Tuple[Tuple[Any, ...], Self]][](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.iter_sections "Link to this definition")
:   Iterate over sections of the Block.

    The Block is split into sections based upon the values of the
    implicitly or explicitly specified variable(s).

    Parameters:
    :   * **varnames** (*str or iterable of str, optional*) – Name(s) of the variable(s) on which to split the Block into
          sections. If a single str, the Block is split into sections
          based on the values of that variable. If an iterable of str,
          the Block is split into sections based on every combination
          of the values of all of those variables. If `None`, the
          Block is split into sections based on the the setting of
          `auto_pref`.
        * **auto\_pref** (*{‘idxs’, ‘ivars’}, optional*) – Only applies if `varnames` is `None`. If `'idxs'`,
          the Block is split into sections based on the values of the
          outer idx variables (as defined by [`Block.idxnames`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.idxnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.idxnames "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.idxnames")). If
          `'ivars'`, the Block is split into sections based on the
          values of the outer independent variables (as defined by
          [`Block.ivarnames`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.ivarnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.ivarnames "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.ivarnames")). If `None`, prefer the Block’s idx
          variables. If no idxs, the ivars are checked next. If the
          Block has neither idxs nor ivars, a ValueError is raised.
        * **tol** (*float or dict-like, default NaN*) – The absolute tolerance to use when splitting the Block into
          sections based upon variables with inexact dtypes. Inexact
          dtypes include all float and complex dtypes. If tol is a
          float, it is the tolerance used for all variables. If tol
          is a dict-like, the keys are variable names and the values are
          float tolerances for each variable. If any tolerance is NaN,
          the Block is split into sections based on exact value matches
          for that variable.

    Yields:
    :   * *Any* – The value(s) of the variable(s) on which the Block is split.
        * *Block* – A Block containing the observations for the current section.


---

Block.keep\_vars(*varnames: Iterable[str]*, *\**, *keep\_crucial: bool = True*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.keep_vars "Link to this definition")

# keysight.pwdatatools.Block.keep\_vars\_in\_place[](#keysight-pwdatatools-block-keep-vars-in-place "Link to this heading")

Block.keep\_vars\_in\_place(*varnames: Iterable[str]*, *\**, *keep\_crucial: bool = True*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.keep_vars_in_place "Link to this definition")
:   Keep only the specified variables (modifies Block in place).

    All kept variables are unmodified.

    Parameters:
    :   * **varnames** – The names of the variables to keep.
        * **keep\_crucial** – If `True`, keep all vars returned by the
          [`crucial_varnames()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.crucial_varnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.crucial_varnames "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.crucial_varnames") method. Otherwise, only keep them
          if they are explicitly included in varnames. Defaults to
          `True`.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Raises:
    :   **KeyError** – If any of the variable names in varnames are not in data.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/block/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set\_data[](#keysight-pwdatatools-block-set-data "Link to this heading")

Block.set\_data(*data: Mapping[str, ArrayLike] | pd.DataFrame*, *\**, *combine\_multidim\_vars: bool = True*, *dimscales\_delim: Tuple[str, str, str] = ('[', ',', ']')*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data "Link to this definition")
:   Replace the data for variable(s) in the Block, returning a new Block.

    If a variable does not exist, it is created. If it does exist,
    its data is replaced and all other attributes are preserved.

    Parameters:
    :   * **data** (*mapping of str to array-like or pandas.DataFrame*) – The new data to set in the Block.
        * **combine\_multidim\_vars** (*bool, default True*) – If `True`, combine multidimensional variables in data into
          a single variable. This also has the effect of combining the
          existing multidimensional variables in the Block. If
          `False`, do not combine multidimensional variables.
        * **dimscales\_delim** (*length 3 tuple of str, default (‘[’, ‘,’, ‘]’)*) – A tuple of str characters for embedding dimension scales into
          the varnames of flattened multidim variables. Only applies if
          `combine_multidim_vars=True`. The first character is the
          opening delimiter, the second is the delimiter between
          dimension scales, and the third is the closing delimiter. For
          example, the default delimiters would work for flattened
          multidim varnames like `'S[2,1]'` and `'PortZ[1]'`.

    Returns:
    :   A new Block with the specified data.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** –

        * If the length along axis 0 of any of the array-likes in
          data does not match the Block’s observation count.
          - If data is a pandas DataFrame and combine\_multidim\_vars
          is `True`, and the dimscales of a multidimensional variable
          in the DataFrame do not match the dimscales of the
          corresponding multidimensional variable in the Block.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/block/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data_in_place.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set\_data\_in\_place[](#keysight-pwdatatools-block-set-data-in-place "Link to this heading")

Block.set\_data\_in\_place(*data: Mapping[str, ArrayLike | Scalar] | pd.DataFrame*, *\**, *combine\_multidim\_vars: bool = True*, *dimscales\_delim: Tuple[str, str, str] = ('[', ',', ']')*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data_in_place "Link to this definition")
:   Set the data for variable(s) in the Block (in place).

    If a variable does not exist, it is created. If it does exist,
    its data is replaced and all other attributes are preserved.

    Parameters:
    :   * **data** (*mapping of str to array-like/scalar values or pandas.DataFrame*) – The new data to set in the Block.
        * **combine\_multidim\_vars** (*bool, default True*) – If `True`, combine multidimensional variables in data into
          a single variable. This also has the effect of combining the
          existing multidimensional variables in the Block. If
          `False`, do not combine multidimensional variables.
        * **dimscales\_delim** (*length 3 tuple of str, default (‘[’, ‘,’, ‘]’)*) – A tuple of str characters for embedding dimension scales into
          the varnames of flattened multidim variables. Only applies if
          `combine_multidim_vars=True`. The first character is the
          opening delimiter, the second is the delimiter between
          dimension scales, and the third is the closing delimiter. For
          example, the default delimiters would work for flattened
          multidim varnames like `'S[2,1]'` and `'PortZ[1]'`.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** –

        * If the length along axis 0 of any of the array-likes in
          data does not match the Block’s observation count.
          - If data is a pandas DataFrame and combine\_multidim\_vars
          is `True`, and the dimscales of a multidimensional variable
          in the DataFrame do not match the dimscales of the
          corresponding multidimensional variable in the Block.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/block/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_vars_in_place.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set\_vars\_in\_place[](#keysight-pwdatatools-block-set-vars-in-place "Link to this heading")

Block.set\_vars\_in\_place(*variables: [Var](../../var/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var") | Iterable[[Var](../../var/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")]*, *\**, *copy: bool = False*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_vars_in_place "Link to this definition")
:   Set variables in the Block (in place).

    This method adds new variable(s) and/or replaces existing
    variables. If a variable by the same name already exists
    in the Block, it is replaced.

    Parameters:
    :   * **variables** (*Var or iterable of Var*) – Var instance(s) to set in the Block.
        * **copy** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, always make a copy of the Var(s) before setting
          them in the Block. If `False`, only make copies when necessary.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Notes

    Copies are made when the Var’s block attribute is not None and
    copy is False. This is to ensure that the Var is not shared
    between multiple Blocks.


---
