<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/block/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.to_file.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Block.to\_file[](#keysight-pwdatatools-block-to-file "Link to this heading")

Block.to\_file(*dst: str | PathLike | BaseDataFile*, *dst\_mode: Literal['x', 'w', 'a'] = 'x'*, *\**, *dst\_format: str | None = None*, *format\_specific: FormatSpecificWriteOptions | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*, *loc: str = '/'*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.to_file "Link to this definition")
:   Write Block data (and possibly metadata) to a file.

    If the destination file dst has a known file extension that maps
    to a supported format, then the file is written in that format.
    If the destination file has a missing or unknown file extension,
    you must either 1) input a [`DataFile`](../../../fileio/datafile/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile") with a
    `format_override` as `dst`, or 2) use the `dst_format`
    parameter. Known writeable file extensions are stored in
    `options.writing.extentions`. Known writeable file formats
    are stored in `options.writing.formats`.

    Whether metadata is written depends on the format of output file.
    If the output file’s format has places to store metadata (such as
    in the native pwdatatools format and HDF5 format), then metadata
    is automatically included in the output file.

    Parameters:
    :   * **dst** (*str, os.PathLike, or DataFile*) – The destination file path.
        * **dst\_mode** (*{‘x’, ‘w’, ‘a’}, default ‘x’*) – The mode to use when opening `dst`. If `dst` does not yet
          exist, all three modes yield the same behavior. However, the 3
          modes have differing behaviors if attempting to write to a pre-
          existing file. `'w'` overwrites the existing file. `'a'`
          appends to the existing file. `'x'` results in a
          FileExistsError.
        * **dst\_format** (*str, optional*) – Sets the destination file’s format. Supported types are stored
          in `options.writing.formats`. Any value other than
          `None` overrides the format inferred from or set by `dst`.
        * **format\_specific** (*FormatSpecificWriteOptions, optional*) – Defines the format-specific options for writing `dst`. By
          default, the global option
          `options.writing.format_specific` sets format-specific
          writing options, but this parameter overrides the global
          options. If provided, it must be an instance of
          `FormatSpecificWriteOptions`. See the docs for a list
          of all classes that are format-specific write options. Some
          examples are `ADSWriteOptions`,
          `MDIFWriteOptions`, `ADSWriteInvalid`, and
          `MDIFWriteInvalid`.
        * **iffs** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing.
        * **loc** (*str, default ‘/’*) – The write location within `dst`. Only applies to file
          formats that support multiple levels of hierarchy (for
          example, native and HDF5 formats). This parameter sets the
          write location within the file’s directory-like (or tree-like)
          structure. The “root” location is ‘/’.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Raises:
    :   * **FileExistsError** – If `dst_mode='x'` and `dst` already exists.
        * **ValueError** – If `dst_mode='a'` and append mode is not supported for the
          format of `dst`.

    Examples

    The below examples assume that `block` is an instance of
    [`Block`](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block") and the following
    imports:

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import DataFile
    >>> from pathlib import Path
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` has a known file extension, you can
    simply input `dst` as a str, `pathlib.Path`, or
    `DataFile`. Known file
    extensions are stored in `options.writing.extentions`.

    ```
    >>> # str path with known ext (.ds)
    >>> block.to_file('/home/s_param.ds')
    >>> # pathlib.Path with known ext (.mdf)
    >>> block.to_file(Path('/home/noise_param.mdf'))
    >>> # DataFile with known ext (.pwdt)
    >>> block.to_file(DataFile('/home/noise_param.pwdt'))
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` has a missing or non-standard file
    extension, you can use the `dst_format` parameter. Alternatively,
    you can input an instance of
    `DataFile` as the `dst`
    argument and use the DataFile’s `format_override` parameter to
    define the destination’s format.

    ```
    >>> # no ext solved by dst_format
    >>> block.to_file('/data/s_param', dst_format='mdif')
    >>> # non-standard ext solved by dst_format
    >>> block.to_file('/data/noise_param.txt', dst_format='mdif')
    >>> # no ext solved by DataFile
    >>> block.to_file(DataFile('/data/noise_param', format_override='mdif'))
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` already exists, a FileExistsError is
    raised if `dst_mode='x'`, which is the default. A `dst_mode`
    parameter value of `'w'` will overwrite the entire existing file,
    while a value of `'a'` will append to the existing file.

    Note

    Only certain types of datafiles work for `dst_mode='a'`. A
    ValueError is raised if append mode is not supported for the format
    of `dst`.

    ```
    >>> block.to_file('/s_param.ds', dst_mode='w')  # overwrite dst if it exists
    >>> block.to_file('/noise_param.mdf', dst_mode='a') # append to dst if it exists
    >>> block.to_file('/run1.pwdt', dst_mode='a') # append to dst if it exists
    ```

    See also

    [`write_file()`](../../../fileio/write_file.md#keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.write_file "keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.write_file"), [`translate_file()`](../../../fileio/translate_file.md#keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.translate_file "keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.translate_file")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/block/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.to_pandas_dataframe.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Block.to\_pandas\_dataframe[](#keysight-pwdatatools-block-to-pandas-dataframe "Link to this heading")

Block.to\_pandas\_dataframe(*\**, *index: Literal['default', 'ivars', 'idxs'] = 'default'*, *cols\_nlevels: int = 0*, *cols\_level\_names: bool = True*, *cols\_delim: Tuple[str, str, str] = ('[', ',', ']')*, *multidim\_pref: Literal['dims', 'default\_ints'] = 'dims'*, *dims\_pref: Literal[None, 'nums', 'names'] = None*, *default\_ints: Literal[1, 0] = 1*) → DataFrame[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.to_pandas_dataframe "Link to this definition")
:   Create a pandas DataFrame from this Block.

    Parameters:
    :   * **index** (*{‘default’, ‘ivars’, ‘idxs’}, default ‘default’*) – The index to use for the rows in the returned DataFrame. If
          `'default'`, use a default pandas integer RangeIndex. If
          `'ivars'`, use the Block’s independent variables (ivars)
          to create a pandas Index or MultiIndex. If `'idxs'`, use
          the index variables to create a pandas Index or MultiIndex.
          If `'ivars'` or `'idxs'` is the chosen option but no
          ivars or idxs exist, a default integer RangeIndex is used.
        * **cols\_nlevels** (*int, default 0*) – The number of levels to use for MultiIndex cols. If `-1`,
          let the max number of dims in the Vars determine the number of
          levels. If `0`, use a flat Index for the cols (instead of
          MultiIndex). If `1`, use a MultiIndex with 1 level. If the
          value is greater than max ndim + 1, fill the extra levels with
          empty strings.
        * **cols\_level\_names** (*bool, default True*) – If `True` set the names of the levels in the returned
          MultiIndex to `'varname'`, `'i'`, `'j'`, etc. Ignored if
          the return cols Index is flat.
        * **cols\_delim** (*3-tuple of str, default (‘[’, ‘,’, ‘]’)*) – Only applies to multi-dimensional variables. A 3-tuple of str
          characters used for embedding dimscales into the varnames in a
          flat string columns index. The first character is the opening
          delimiter, the second is the delimiter between dimscales, and
          the third is the closing delimiter. For example, the default
          delimiters would create varnames like `'S[2,1]'` and
          `'PortZ[1]'`.
        * **multidim\_pref** (*{‘dims’, ‘default\_ints’}, default ‘dims’*) – Only applies to multi-dimensional variables. If `'dims'`,
          use dimscales set by dims\_pref. If `'default_ints'`, use
          the integers set by default\_ints.
        * **dims\_pref** (*{None, ‘nums’, ‘names’}, optional*) – Only applies to multi-dimensional variables. The type of
          dimension scales preferred for the DataFrame columns if the
          Vars’ dims are used. If `'nums'`, use the numeric
          dimscales. If `'names'`, use the string dimscales.
          If `None` (the default), prefer numeric dimscales if they
          exist, otherwise use string dimscales. In all cases, if the
          dimscales are not present, use the integers set by
          default\_ints.
        * **default\_ints** (*{1, 0}, default 1*) – Only applies to multi-dimensional variables. The integer-based
          indexing to use for the columns in the event that any Var’s
          dimscales are not used. If `1`, use 1-based ints for the
          index labels. If `0`, use 0-based ints.

    Returns:
    :   A pandas DataFrame with the Block’s data.

    Return type:
    :   pandas.DataFrame

    Notes

    The dtypes in the DataFrame are automatically set based on the
    dtypes of the data in the Block, taking into account whether Vars
    have nulls or not.

    Examples

    Let’s create a sample Block and convert it to a pandas DataFrame
    using the default settings. Here, we create a Block with one idx
    variable, two ivars, and three dvars. Two of the dvars are
    multi-dimensional: the `PortR` variable has two dimensions, and
    the `magS` variable has three dimensions.

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> import pandas as pd
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Block
    >>> pd.options.display.width = 200  # all DataFrame printouts use this width
    >>> data = {
    ...     "i": [100, 101, 102, 103],
    ...     "sample": [1, 2, 1, 2],
    ...     "bias": [4.1, 4.1, 5.2, 5.2],
    ...     "Pout": [30.5, 31.2, 29.4, 30.6],
    ...     "PortR": np.random.randint(30, 60, (4, 2)),
    ...     "magS": np.random.random((4, 2, 2)),
    ... }
    >>> block = Block(data, ivarnames=["sample", "bias"], idxnames=["i"])
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe()
    >>> print(df)
         i  sample  bias  Pout  PortR[1]  PortR[2]  magS[1,1]  magS[1,2]  magS[2,1]  magS[2,2]
    0  100       1   4.1  30.5        58        50   0.260715   0.646049   0.339258   0.248542
    1  101       2   4.1  31.2        53        48   0.898790   0.525265   0.895914   0.743558
    2  102       1   5.2  29.4        54        45   0.630773   0.832599   0.929879   0.238047
    3  103       2   5.2  30.6        46        54   0.226296   0.456732   0.961668   0.068220
    ```

    Each of the 1D variables are able to fit in one column, but the
    multi-dimensional variables are split into multiple columns. The
    columns for the multi-dimensional variables are created by
    embedding default integers into the varnames. The DataFrame’s
    index is a default RangeIndex.

    Let’s create a new DataFrame with a non-default index. First, let’s use
    the Block’s ivars. This creates a MultiIndex from the Block’s independent
    variables since there are two ivars.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(index="ivars")
    >>> print(df)
                   i  Pout  PortR[1]  PortR[2]  magS[1,1]  magS[1,2]  magS[2,1]  magS[2,2]
    sample bias
    1      4.1   100  30.5        58        50   0.260715   0.646049   0.339258   0.248542
    2      4.1   101  31.2        53        48   0.898790   0.525265   0.895914   0.743558
    1      5.2   102  29.4        54        45   0.630773   0.832599   0.929879   0.238047
    2      5.2   103  30.6        46        54   0.226296   0.456732   0.961668   0.068220
    ```

    The other index option is to use the Block’s idxs. This creates a
    regular Index (not a MultiIndex) from the Block’s index variables,
    since there is only one idx variable.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(index="idxs")
    >>> print(df)
         sample  bias  Pout  PortR[1]  PortR[2]  magS[1,1]  magS[1,2]  magS[2,1]  magS[2,2]
    i
    100       1   4.1  30.5        58        50   0.260715   0.646049   0.339258   0.248542
    101       2   4.1  31.2        53        48   0.898790   0.525265   0.895914   0.743558
    102       1   5.2  29.4        54        45   0.630773   0.832599   0.929879   0.238047
    103       2   5.2  30.6        46        54   0.226296   0.456732   0.961668   0.068220
    ```

    Now, let’s explore the options for the columns. In the absence
    of dimensional metadata for the multi-dimensional variables,
    the default integers are used for the column labels. There
    are two options for the default integers: 0-based and 1-based.
    Let’s use 0-based integers.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(default_ints=0)
    >>> print(df)
         i  sample  bias  Pout  PortR[0]  PortR[1]  magS[0,0]  magS[0,1]  magS[1,0]  magS[1,1]
    0  100       1   4.1  30.5        58        50   0.260715   0.646049   0.339258   0.248542
    1  101       2   4.1  31.2        53        48   0.898790   0.525265   0.895914   0.743558
    2  102       1   5.2  29.4        54        45   0.630773   0.832599   0.929879   0.238047
    3  103       2   5.2  30.6        46        54   0.226296   0.456732   0.961668   0.068220
    ```

    By default `pwdatatools` uses square brackets and commas
    as the delimiters for embedding the dimension scales into the
    column labels for multi-dimensional variables. Let’s change
    the delimiters to parentheses and semicolons.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_delim=('(', ';', ')'))
    >>> print(df)
         i  sample  bias  Pout  PortR(1)  PortR(2)  magS(1;1)  magS(1;2)  magS(2;1)  magS(2;2)
    0  100       1   4.1  30.5        58        50   0.260715   0.646049   0.339258   0.248542
    1  101       2   4.1  31.2        53        48   0.898790   0.525265   0.895914   0.743558
    2  102       1   5.2  29.4        54        45   0.630773   0.832599   0.929879   0.238047
    3  103       2   5.2  30.6        46        54   0.226296   0.456732   0.961668   0.068220
    ```

    Now, let’s add dimensional metadata to our multi-dimensional
    variables. This is done assigning an instance of [`Dims`](../../../metadata/dims/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims")
    to the [`Var.dims`](../../var/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dims.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.dims") attribute. Each instance of [`Dims`](../../../metadata/dims/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Dims "keysight.pwdatatools.Dims")
    may define numeric (“nums”) and/or string (“names”) dimension
    scales.

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Dims
    >>> block["PortR"].dims = Dims(ndim=2, i_nums=[5, 6], i_names=["Port5", "Port6"])
    >>> block["magS"].dims = Dims(
    ...     ndim=3,
    ...     i_nums=[3, 4],
    ...     i_names=["P3", "P4"],
    ...     j_nums=[1, 2],
    ...     j_names=["P1", "P2"],
    ... )
    ```

    Now, let’s create a pandas DataFarme using the default settings.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe()
    >>> print(df)
         i  sample  bias  Pout  PortR[5]  PortR[6]  magS[3,1]  magS[3,2]  magS[4,1]  magS[4,2]
    0  100       1   4.1  30.5        58        50   0.260715   0.646049   0.339258   0.248542
    1  101       2   4.1  31.2        53        48   0.898790   0.525265   0.895914   0.743558
    2  102       1   5.2  29.4        54        45   0.630773   0.832599   0.929879   0.238047
    3  103       2   5.2  30.6        46        54   0.226296   0.456732   0.961668   0.068220
    ```

    As you can see, the column labels now use the numeric dimension
    scales. We can optionally use the string dimension scales instead.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(dims_pref="names")
    >>> print(df)
         i  sample  bias  Pout  PortR['Port5']  PortR['Port6']  magS['P3','P1']  magS['P3','P2']  magS['P4','P1']  magS['P4','P2']
    0  100       1   4.1  30.5              58              50         0.260715         0.646049         0.339258         0.248542
    1  101       2   4.1  31.2              53              48         0.898790         0.525265         0.895914         0.743558
    2  102       1   5.2  29.4              54              45         0.630773         0.832599         0.929879         0.238047
    3  103       2   5.2  30.6              46              54         0.226296         0.456732         0.961668         0.068220
    ```

    We can choose to use the default integers for the column labels
    instead of the dimension scales.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(multidim_pref="default_ints")
    >>> print(df)
         i  sample  bias  Pout  PortR[1]  PortR[2]  magS[1,1]  magS[1,2]  magS[2,1]  magS[2,2]
    0  100       1   4.1  30.5        58        50   0.260715   0.646049   0.339258   0.248542
    1  101       2   4.1  31.2        53        48   0.898790   0.525265   0.895914   0.743558
    2  102       1   5.2  29.4        54        45   0.630773   0.832599   0.929879   0.238047
    3  103       2   5.2  30.6        46        54   0.226296   0.456732   0.961668   0.068220
    ```

    Instead of creating flat columns, we can create a MultiIndex.
    if we use `cols_nlevels=-1`, the columns will have as many
    levels as the maximum number of dimensions in the Vars.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_nlevels=-1)
    >>> print(df)
    varname    i sample bias  Pout PortR          magS
    i                                  5   6         3                   4
    j                                                1         2         1         2
    0        100      1  4.1  30.5    58  50  0.260715  0.646049  0.339258  0.248542
    1        101      2  4.1  31.2    53  48  0.898790  0.525265  0.895914  0.743558
    2        102      1  5.2  29.4    54  45  0.630773  0.832599  0.929879  0.238047
    3        103      2  5.2  30.6    46  54  0.226296  0.456732  0.961668  0.068220
    ```

    We can optionally turn off the level names in the columns if we
    are using a MultiIndex.

    ```
    >>> df = block.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_nlevels=-1, cols_level_names=False)
    >>> print(df)
         i sample bias  Pout PortR          magS
                                 5   6         3                   4
                                               1         2         1         2
    0  100      1  4.1  30.5    58  50  0.260715  0.646049  0.339258  0.248542
    1  101      2  4.1  31.2    53  48  0.898790  0.525265  0.895914  0.743558
    2  102      1  5.2  29.4    54  45  0.630773  0.832599  0.929879  0.238047
    3  103      2  5.2  30.6    46  54  0.226296  0.456732  0.961668  0.068220
    ```

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/block/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.update.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Block.update[](#keysight-pwdatatools-block-update "Link to this heading")

Block.update(*other=None*, *\*\*kwargs*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.update "Link to this definition")
:   Update the Block with Vars and/or data from another mapping.

    Parameters:
    :   * **other** (*mapping of str to VarLike, optional*) – If a VarLike object is an ArrayLike, it is assumed to be the
          Var’s data, and a Var is created. If a [`Var`](../../var/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var"), it is
          directly added to the Block.
        * **\*\*kwargs** – Vars and/or data to add to the Block.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If any value in other is not a Var and the varname already
        exists. In that scenario, use the [`set_data_in_place()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data_in_place.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data_in_place "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.set_data_in_place")
        method instead.

*property* Block.varnames*: Tuple[str, ...]*[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.varnames "Link to this definition")

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/block/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.with_idxs.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Block.with\_idxs[](#keysight-pwdatatools-block-with-idxs "Link to this heading")

Block.with\_idxs(*data\_varnames: str | Iterable[str] | None = None*, *idxnames: str | Iterable[str] | None = None*, *\**, *tol: float | Mapping[str, float] = nan*, *existing: Literal['raise', 'replace'] = 'raise'*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.with_idxs "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new Block with added integer index variables

    The index variables are created by iterating through the combinations
    of values of the data variable(s).

    Parameters:
    :   * **data\_varnames** (*str or iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the data variables from which to create index
          variables. If None, use the ivars.
        * **idxnames** (*str or iterable of str, optional*) – The names to use for the new index variables. If None, the
          names are created from the data\_varnames by prepending ‘i’.
        * **tol** (*float or dict-like, default NaN*) – The absolute tolerance(s) for determining unique values in the
          data variables. If a float, the same tolerance is used for all
          data variables. If a dict-like, the keys are the data variable
          names and the values are the tolerances for those variables.
          If NaN, do not use a tolerance and instead use exact
          value matches.
        * **existing** (*{‘raise’, ‘replace’}, default ‘raise’*) – What to do if the index variable names already exist in the
          Block. If ‘raise’, raise a ValueError. If ‘replace’, replace the
          existing index variables with new ones.

    Returns:
    :   A new Block with the new index variables.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> import keysight.pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> block = pwdt.Block(
    ...     {
    ...         "bias": [1.2, 1.2, 1.3, 1.3],
    ...         "freq": [1e9, 2e9, 1e9, 2e9],
    ...         "z": [1, 2, 3, 4],
    ...     }
    ... )
    >>> block_idxs = block.with_idxs(["bias", "freq"])
    >>> block_idxs.idxnames
    ('ibias', 'ifreq')
    ```

    Let’s view the the new Block’s data as a pandas DataFrame to help
    visualize the new idx variables. We use the idx variables as the
    DataFrame’s rows index.

    ```
    >>> df = block_idxs.to_pandas_dataframe(index='idxs')
    >>> print(df)
                 bias          freq  z
    ibias ifreq
    0     0       1.2  1.000000e+09  1
          1       1.2  2.000000e+09  2
    1     0       1.3  1.000000e+09  3
          1       1.3  2.000000e+09  4
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/block/index.md === -->

# Block[](#block "Link to this heading")

This page documents public attributes and methods available in the Block class. For more information and examples, see [Use the Block Class](../../../howto/use_block_class.md#use-block-class).

*class* Block(*variables: Variables = None*, *\**, *name: str | None = None*, *ivarnames: Iterable[str] | None = None*, *idxnames: Iterable[str] | None = None*, *attrs: AttrsDictLike | None = None*, *exprs: ExprsDictLike | None = None*, *copy: bool = False*, *\*\*kwargs*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "Link to this definition")
:   An object that represents a dataset or a portion of a hierarchical dataset.

    It behaves like a mutable mapping of varnames to Vars.

    Parameters:
    :   * **variables** (*DataFrame, sequence of Var, str to VarLike dict, optional*) – Several types of inputs are supported: a pandas DataFrame, a
          mapping of str variable name to ArrayLikes or Vars, or a sequence
          of Vars. Examples of ArrayLike objects are numpy ndarrays and
          MaskedArrays, Python lists, and pandas Series. If a mapping or
          sequence is provided, all data objects must have the same length
          for axis 0.
        * **name** (*str, optional*) – A str name for the Block. If not specified, the name is set to
          an empty string.
        * **ivarnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of the independent variables (ivars).
        * **idxnames** (*iterable of str, optional*) – The names of variables that act as indexes along axis 0. Axis 0 is
          also known as the “shared” axis or dimension.
        * **attrs** (*dict-like, optional*) – A dict or dict-like object of arbitrary Block-level metadata. The
          keys must be strings and the values must be one of the following:
          str, int, float, complex, bool, None, dict, list, or
          numpy.ndarray. Nested dicts and lists are allowed.
        * **exprs** (*ExprsDictLike, optional*) – A mapping of str expression name to Expr objects. The Expr objects
          represent expressions that can be applied to variables or other
          expressions.
        * **copy** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, always copy the input variables. If `False`, do
          not copy the variables unless necessary.

    Other Parameters:
    :   * **data** (*DataFrame, str to ArrayLike mapping, or None*) – A keyword-only argument that exists mostly for backwards
          compatibility. It is recommended to use variables instead of this
          keyword-only argument. You cannot specify values for both data
          and variables.
        * **validate** (*bool, default True*) – An expert-level keyword-only argument that determines whether to
          validate the input data. Only set to `False` if you are certain
          that the input data is valid.

    Examples

    Let’s initialize a Block with a dict containing some data. The keys
    must be strings (these become variable names) and the values can be
    any array-like objects (these become variable data).

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> data = {'freq': np.array([1, 2, 3]), 'Vout': [1, 2, 3]}
    >>> block = pwdt.Block(data)
    >>> block
    Block(
        <'freq', 'Vout', ... with 3 observations>,
        name='',
        ivarnames=(),
        attrs={},
    )
    ```

    Initializing a Block with a pandas DataFrame is also supported. The
    below code results in a Block that is identical to the Block we
    created with a dict.

    ```
    >>> import pandas as pd
    >>> df = pd.DataFrame(data)
    >>> block = pwdt.Block(df)
    ```

    For more control when creating a Block from a pandas DataFrame, use
    [`Block.from_pandas_dataframe()`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe"). It has additional parameters
    related to how the DataFrame is converted to a Block.

    Below, we initialize the Block with a name, the names of the
    independent variables (ivarnames), the names of index variables
    (idxnames), variable-level metadata (using instances of [`Var`](../var/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")),
    and some arbitrary Block-level metadata (as attrs).

    ```
    >>> freq = pwdt.Var(data=[1e9, 2e9, 3e9], name='freq', unit='Hz')
    >>> vout = pwdt.Var(data=[5, 6, 7], name='Vout', role='voltage', unit='V')
    >>> variables = {'ifreq': np.array([1, 2, 3]), 'freq': freq, 'Vout': vout}
    >>> attrs = {'temperature': 150, 'comments': 'This is measured data'}
    >>> block = pwdt.Block(
    ...     variables,
    ...     name='foo',
    ...     ivarnames=['freq'],
    ...     idxnames=['ifreq'],
    ...     attrs=attrs,
    ... )
    >>> print(block)
    Block(
        <'Vout', ... with 3 observations>,
        name='foo',
        ivarnames=('freq',),
        idxnames=('ifreq',),
        attrs={'temperature': ..., 'comments': ...},
    )
    ```

    See also

    [`Block.from_file()`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_file.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_file "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_file"), [`Block.from_pandas_dataframe()`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.from_pandas_dataframe")

**Attributes**

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| [`Block.attrs`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.attrs.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.attrs "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.attrs") | A dict of arbitrary attributes (metadata). |
| [`Block.dvarnames`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.dvarnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.dvarnames "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.dvarnames") | Names of the dependent variables (dvars) as a tuple of str. |
| [`Block.exprs`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.exprs.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.exprs "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.exprs") |  |
| [`Block.idxnames`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.idxnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.idxnames "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.idxnames") | Names of the index variables (idxs) as a tuple of str. |
| [`Block.ivarnames`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.ivarnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.ivarnames "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.ivarnames") | Names of the independent variables (ivars) as a tuple of str. |
| [`Block.name`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.name.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.name "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.name") | A str name for the Block. |
| [`Block.varnames`](_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Block.varnames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block.varnames "keysight.pwdatatools.Block.varnames") | Names of all variables (ivars, dvars, and idxs) as a tuple of str. |

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/group/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.__add__.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Group.\_\_add\_\_[](#keysight-pwdatatools-group-add "Link to this heading")

Group.\_\_add\_\_(*member: Member*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.__add__ "Link to this definition")
:   Add this Group to another Group or a Block and return new Group.

    Parameters:
    :   **member** – Another Group or a Block to add with this Group.

    Returns:
    :   A new Group containing this Group and the other Group or Block.

    Return type:
    :   [*Group*](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> group1 = pwdt.Group(name='g1')
    >>> block2 = pwdt.Block(name='b1')
    >>> group2 = group1 + block2
    >>> group2
    Group(members=[Group(name='g1'), Block(name='b1')])
    >>> group3 = group2 + group1
    >>> group3
    Group(members=[Group(members=[Group(name='g2'), Group(name='g1')])
    ```

# keysight.pwdatatools.Group.\_\_iadd\_\_[](#keysight-pwdatatools-group-iadd "Link to this heading")

Group.\_\_iadd\_\_(*value: Member | Iterable[Member]*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.__iadd__ "Link to this definition")

    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt

    Group(members=[Block(name='b1')])

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/group/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.__init__.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Group.\_\_init\_\_[](#keysight-pwdatatools-group-init "Link to this heading")

Group.\_\_init\_\_(*members: Iterable[Member] | None = None*, *\**, *name: str | None = None*, *attrs: AttrsDictLike | None = None*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.__init__ "Link to this definition")
:   Initialize an instance of Group.

    Parameters:
    :   * **members** (*iterable of Blocks and/or Groups, optional*) – An iterable containing objects which will become members, or
          children, of the Group. These may be Blocks or other Groups.
          Note that there are several subtypes of Block, such as
          LoadPullBlock, which are also supported.
        * **name** (*str, optional*) – A str name for the Group. If not specified, the name is set to
          an empty string.
        * **attrs** (*dict-like, optional*) – A dict or dict-like object of arbitrary Group-level metadata. The
          keys must be strings and the values must be one of the following:
          str, int, float, complex, bool, None, dict, list, or
          numpy.ndarray. Nested dicts and lists are allowed.

    Examples

    Below, assume `block1` and `block2` are instances of Block and
    `group1` is another, pre-existing instance of Group.

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> attrs = {'foo': 1, 'bar': 42}
    >>> group2 = pwdt.Group([block1, block2, group1], name='baz', attrs=attrs)
    ```

    See also

    [`read_file_as_group()`](../../../fileio/read_file_as_group.md#keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.read_file_as_group "keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.read_file_as_group"), [`Block`](../../block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block")

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/group/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.attrs.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Group.attrs[](#keysight-pwdatatools-group-attrs "Link to this heading")

*property* Group.attrs*: [AttrsDict](../../../metadata/attrsdict/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict "keysight.pwdatatools._core.properties.AttrsDict")*[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.attrs "Link to this definition")
:   A dict of arbitrary attributes (metadata).

    The attributes are stored in an [`AttrsDict`](../../../metadata/attrsdict/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict "keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict"), which is a
    type-restricted dict. The keys must be str, and the values must
    be one of the following types: str, int, float, complex, bool,
    None, `numpy.ndarray`, dict, or list. Nested dicts and
    lists are also supported.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/group/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.copy.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Group.copy[](#keysight-pwdatatools-group-copy "Link to this heading")

Group.copy(*deep: bool = True*) → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.copy "Link to this definition")
:   Make a copy of this Group, returning a new instance of Group.

    Parameters:
    :   **deep** – When `True`, all members are deepcopied, so that the new
        Group is completely independent from self. When `False`, all
        members in the new Group are the same objects in self (only
        references to the members are copied… this means changes made
        to members in the new object will also be reflected in the
        original object). Defaults to `True`.

    Returns:
    :   A new Group instance with either the same members as this
        Group, or copies (depending on setting of deep).

    Return type:
    :   [*Group*](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group")

    Notes

    The Group’s metadata stored in attrs is always deep-copied,
    regardless of the value of deep.opied regardless of
    the value of deep. Defaults to `True`.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/group/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill\_membernames[](#keysight-pwdatatools-group-fill-membernames "Link to this heading")

Group.fill\_membernames(*\**, *recursive: bool = False*, *block\_prefix: str = 'B'*, *group\_prefix: str = 'G'*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames "Link to this definition")
:   Rename members with empty names (in place).

    The new names are unique and created by appending a unique integer
    to a prefix.

    Parameters:
    :   * **recursive** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, set empty names for all members recursively
          (including members inside child Groups). If `False`, do
          not set empty names for the members inside child Groups.
        * **block\_prefix** (*str, default ‘B’*) – The prefix to use for auto-naming Blocks with empty names.
          The new blocknames are created by appending an integer to the
          prefix. For example, if `block_prefix='B'`, then the
          first auto-named Block will be named `'B0'`. The integer
          gets incremented for each auto-named Block within the same
          Group and gets reset to 0 for each new child Group.
        * **group\_prefix** (*str, default ‘G’*) – The prefix to use for auto-naming Groups with empty names.
          The new groupnames are created by appending an integer to the
          prefix. For example, if `group_prefix='G'`, then the
          first auto-named Group will be named `'G0'`. The integer
          gets incremented for each auto-named Group within the same
          Group and gets reset to 0 for each new child Group.

    Return type:
    :   *None*


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/group/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.filled_membernames.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Group.filled\_membernames[](#keysight-pwdatatools-group-filled-membernames "Link to this heading")

Group.filled\_membernames(*\**, *recursive: bool = False*, *block\_prefix: str = 'B'*, *group\_prefix: str = 'G'*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.filled_membernames "Link to this definition")
:   Context manager to temporarily fill empty membernames (in place).

    The new names are unique and created by appending a unique integer
    to a prefix.

    This is a context manager version of the [`fill_membernames()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames")
    method, and it restores the original membernames after the
    context exits.

    Parameters:
    :   * **recursive** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, set empty names for all members recursively
          (including members inside child Groups). If `False`, do
          not set empty names for the members inside child Groups.
        * **block\_prefix** (*str, default ‘B’*) – The prefix to use for auto-naming Blocks with empty names.
          The new blocknames are created by appending an integer to the
          prefix. For example, if `block_prefix='B'`, then the
          first auto-named Block will be named `'B0'`. The integer
          gets incremented for each auto-named Block within the same
          Group and gets reset to 0 for each new child Group.
        * **group\_prefix** (*str, default ‘G’*) – The prefix to use for auto-naming Groups with empty names.
          The new groupnames are created by appending an integer to the
          prefix. For example, if `group_prefix='G'`, then the
          first auto-named Group will be named `'G0'`. The integer
          gets incremented for each auto-named Group within the same
          Group and gets reset to 0 for each new child Group.

    Yields:
    :   *None*

    See also

    [`fill_membernames()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/group/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flatten.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flatten[](#keysight-pwdatatools-group-flatten "Link to this heading")

Group.flatten(*\**, *fill\_membernames: bool = True*, *hierarchical\_membernames: bool = True*, *sep: str = '.'*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flatten "Link to this definition")
:   Flatten child Groups so that only Blocks remain as members.

    Note that this method modifies the Group in-place and cannot be
    undone. Optionally rename Blocks with hierarchical names using
    the `sep` parameter.

    Parameters:
    :   * **fill\_membernames** (*bool, default True*) – If `True`, fill empty member names using the default naming
          scheme. If `False`, leave empty member names as empty
          strings. For more control over renaming members with empty
          names, see methods [`fill_membernames()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames") and the
          related context manager [`filled_membernames()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.filled_membernames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.filled_membernames "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.filled_membernames").
        * **hierarchical\_membernames** (*bool, default True*) – If `True`, use hierarchical names for all members. If
          `False`, use the original names.
        * **sep** (*str, default ‘.’*) – String separator used as a delimiter when combining
          hierarchical names. For example, if `sep='.'`, then a Block
          named ‘block1’ nested inside a Group named ‘group1’ would be
          named ‘group1.block1’. Ignored if hierarchical\_names is
          `False`.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    See also

    [`flattened()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flattened.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flattened "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flattened")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/group/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flattened.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flattened[](#keysight-pwdatatools-group-flattened "Link to this heading")

Group.flattened(*\**, *fill\_membernames: bool = True*, *hierarchical\_membernames: bool = True*, *sep: str = '.'*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flattened "Link to this definition")
:   Context maanger to temporarily flatten a Group.

    This is a context manager version of the [`flatten()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flatten.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flatten "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flatten") method
    that restores the Group to its original state when exiting the
    context.

    Parameters:
    :   * **fill\_membernames** (*bool, default True*) – If `True`, fill empty member names using the default naming
          scheme. If `False`, leave empty member names as empty
          strings. For more control over renaming members with empty
          names, see methods [`fill_membernames()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.fill_membernames") and the
          related context manager [`filled_membernames()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.filled_membernames.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.filled_membernames "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.filled_membernames").
        * **hierarchical\_membernames** (*bool, default True*) – If `True`, use hierarchical names for all members. If
          `False`, use the original names.
        * **sep** (*str, default ‘.’*) – String separator used as a delimiter when combining
          hierarchical names. For example, if `sep='.'`, then a Block
          named ‘block1’ nested inside a Group named ‘group1’ would be
          named ‘group1.block1’. Ignored if hierarchical\_names is
          `False`.

    Yields:
    :   *None*

    See also

    [`flatten()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flatten.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flatten "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.flatten")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/group/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.from_file.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Group.from\_file[](#keysight-pwdatatools-group-from-file "Link to this heading")

*classmethod* Group.from\_file(*src: str | os.PathLike | BaseDataFile*, *loc: str = '/'*, *\**, *data: bool = True*, *format\_specific: FormatSpecificReadOptions | None = None*, *idxs: IdxsOption | None = None*, *idxs\_tol: float | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*, *src\_format: str | None = None*, *varnames: Mapping[str, str] | None = None*) → [Group](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.from_file "Link to this definition")
:   Read a datafile and return a new instance of Group.

    Known readable file extensions are stored in `options.reading.extentions`.
    Known readable file formats are stored in `options.reading.formats`.

    Parameters:
    :   * **src** (*str, os.PathLike, or DataFile*) – The source file path. If it has a missing or unknown file
          extension, you must either 1) input a [`DataFile`](../../../fileio/datafile/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile") with a
          `format_override`, or 2) use the `src_format` parameter.
        * **loc** (*str, default ‘/’*) – The location within the source file to return as a Group. If
          `'/'` (the “root”), the entire source file is read.
          Otherwise, it represents the name or position of a Group in
          the source file (for example, `'/0'` or `'/Group1`).
        * **data** (*bool, default True*) – A bool that determines whether to read the data from `src`. If
          `False`, only the metadata is read. Reading only metadata can
          be useful for quickly examining the variable names and hierarchy
          of `src`.
        * **format\_specific** (*FormatSpecificReadOptions, optional*) – Defines the format-specific options for reading `src`. By
          default, the global option
          `options.reading.format_specific` sets format-specific
          reading options, but this parameter can override the global
          options. If provided, it must be an instance of
          `FormatSpecificReadOptions`. See the docs for a list of
          all classes that can act as format-specific read options. Some
          examples are `ADSReadOptions`,
          `LoadPullReadOptions`, and `LoadPullDerivedVars`.
        * **idxs** (*{‘auto’, True, False}, optional*) – If `True`, create index variables (idxs) when reading the
          source file. If `False`, do not create them. If `'auto'`,
          use the format-specific defaults. If `None`, use the global
          option `options.reading.idxs`.
        * **idxs\_tol** (*float, optional*) – The tolerance to use when generating index variables from float
          or complex data. If NaN, no tolerance is used and indexes are
          created by using exact equality. If provided, it takes precedence
          over the global option `options.reading.idxs_tol`.
        * **iffs** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing.
        * **src\_format** (*str, optional*) – Sets the source file’s format. Supported types are stored in
          `options.reading.formats`. Any value other than `None`
          overrides the format inferred from or set by `src`.
        * **varnames** (*Mapping[str, str], optional*) – A mapping of variable roles to variable names. Defaults to
          `options.reading.varnames`.

    Return type:
    :   [*Group*](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group")

    Examples

    The below examples assume the following imports:

    ```
    >>> from pathlib import Path
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    ```

    If the source file `src` has a known file extension, and you want
    to read the entire file, you can simply input `src` as a str,
    `pathlib.Path`, or `DataFile`.
    Known file extensions are stored in
    `pwdatatools.options.reading.extentions`.

    ```
    >>> # Using a str path with known ext (.ds)
    >>> g1 = pwdt.Group.from_file('/home/s_param.ds')
    >>> # Using pathlib.Path with known ext (.mdf)
    >>> g2 = pwdt.Group.from_file(Path('/home/noise_param.mdf'))
    >>> # Using DataFile with a known ext (.mdm)
    >>> g3 = pwdt.Group.from_file(pwdt.DataFile('/home/FNoise.mdm'))
    ```

    If `src` has a missing or unknown file extension, you can use the
    `src_format` parameter. Alternatively, you can input an instance of
    `DataFile` as the `src`
    argument and use DataFile’s `format_override` parameter to define
    the source’s format.

    ```
    >>> # Using the `src_format` parameter since the file extension is missing
    >>> g1 = pwdt.Group.from_file('/data/noise', src_format='mdif')
    >>> # Using the `src_format` parameter since the file extension is non-standard
    >>> g2 = pwdt.Group.from_file('/data/noise.txt', src_format='mdif')
    >>> # Using DataFile's `format_override` parameter is another option
    >>> datafile = pwdt.DataFile('/data/noise', format_override='mdif')
    >>> g3 = pwdt.Group.from_file(datafile)
    ```

    If you want to perform a partial read, use the `loc` parameter. You
    have to know the name of the part that you want to read from `src`.
    A Group will always be returned, even if the contents could be
    contained within a single Block (the Block is placed into a Group and
    the Group is returned).

    ```
    >>> # partial read of ADS dataset returns Group containing one Block
    >>> group1 = pwdt.Group.from_file('/home/s_param.ds', loc='/SP1.SP')
    >>> # partial read of a .pwdt file
    >>> group2 = pwdt.Group.from_file('/home/ac_dc_meas.pwdt', loc='/DUT5')
    ```

    See also

    [`read_file()`](../../../fileio/read_file.md#keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.read_file "keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.read_file")
    `Block.from_file()`

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/group/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member_as_block.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get\_member\_as\_block[](#keysight-pwdatatools-group-get-member-as-block "Link to this heading")

Group.get\_member\_as\_block(*name\_or\_index: str | int*, *\**, *cast: bool = True*) → [Block](../../block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member_as_block "Link to this definition")
:   Return a member as a Block, retrieving it by name or index.

    This method is preferred over `__getitem__` because it is more
    explicit in its return type. It is also more flexible because it
    can cast a Group as a Block.

    Parameters:
    :   * **name\_or\_index** (*str or int*) – Name or index of the member to retrieve as a Block.
        * **cast** (*bool, default True*) – If `True`, attempt to cast the member to a Block if it is
          not already one. Casting can be useful especially for cases
          where the member being retrieved is a Group with one Block.
          Casting fails if the member is a Group containing more than
          one Block. If `False`, make no attempts to cast and
          instead raise a ValueError if the member is not a Block.

    Returns:
    :   The member as a Block.

    Return type:
    :   [*Block*](../../block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools.Block")

    Raises:
    :   **TypeError** – If `cast=False` and the member is not a Block.
        If `cast=True` and the member is a Group containing more
        than one Block.

    Notes

    This method does *not* search recursively through subgroups for a
    matching name.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/group/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member_as_group.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get\_member\_as\_group[](#keysight-pwdatatools-group-get-member-as-group "Link to this heading")

Group.get\_member\_as\_group(*name\_or\_index: str | int*, *\**, *cast: bool = True*) → [Group](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools._api.group.Group")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member_as_group "Link to this definition")
:   Return a member as a Group, retrieving it by name or index.

    This method is preferred over `__getitem__` because it is more
    explicit in its return type. It is also more flexible because it
    can cast a Block as a Group.

    Parameters:
    :   * **name\_or\_index** (*str or int*) – Name or index of the member to retrieve as a Group.
        * **cast** (*bool, default True*) – If `True`, cast the member to a Group if it is not already
          one. For example, if the member is already a Block, create a
          new Group containing the Block.

    Returns:
    :   The member as a Group.

    Return type:
    :   [*Group*](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools.Group")

    Raises:
    :   **TypeError** – If `cast=False` and the member is not a Group.

    Notes

    This method does *not* search recursively through subgroups for a
    matching name.


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/group/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member_as_loadpullblock.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get\_member\_as\_loadpullblock[](#keysight-pwdatatools-group-get-member-as-loadpullblock "Link to this heading")

Group.get\_member\_as\_loadpullblock(*name\_or\_index: str | int*, *\**, *cast: bool = True*) → [LoadPullBlock](../../../loadpull/loadpullblock/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools._api.loadpull.main.LoadPullBlock")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.get_member_as_loadpullblock "Link to this definition")
:   Return a member as a LoadPullBlock, retrieving it by name or index.

    This method is preferred over `__getitem__` because it is more
    explicit in its return type. It is also more flexible because it
    can cast a Group that contains a single LoadPullBlock as a
    LoadPullBlock. Note that this method cannot cast a Block as a
    LoadPullBlock, because that operation would involve copying data
    (and violate expectations of “getting” a member). Instead, use
    [`LoadPullBlock.from_block()`](../../../loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_block "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_block") to create a LoadPullBlock from
    a Block.

    Parameters:
    :   * **name\_or\_index** (*str or int*) – Name or index of the member to retrieve as a LoadPullBlock.
        * **cast** (*bool, default True*) – If `True`, attempt to cast the member to a LoadPullBlock if
          it is not already one. Casting is only supported if the member
          is a Group that contains exactly one LoadPullBlock. This fails
          if the member is a different kind of Block or a Group
          containing more than one Block. If `False`, make no attempts
          to cast and instead raise a ValueError if the member is not a
          LoadPullBlock.

    Returns:
    :   The member as a LoadPullBlock.

    Return type:
    :   [*LoadPullBlock*](../../../loadpull/loadpullblock/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock")

    Raises:
    :   **TypeError** – If `cast=False` and the member is not a LoadPullBlock.
        If `cast=True` and the member is a Group containing more
        than one Block.

    Notes

    This method does *not* search recursively through subgroups for a
    matching name.

    See also

    [`LoadPullBlock.from_block()`](../../../loadpull/loadpullblock/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_block.md#keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_block "keysight.pwdatatools.LoadPullBlock.from_block")

# keysight.pwdatatools.Group.index[](#keysight-pwdatatools-group-index "Link to this heading")

Group.index(*value: str*, *start: int = 0*, *stop: int | None = None*) → int[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.index "Link to this definition")
:   Return the index of a member by name.

    This method does not search recursively through subgroups for a
    matching name. If there are multiple members with the same name,
    the index of the first match is returned.

    Parameters:
    :   * **value** (*str*) – The name of the member to match.
        * **start** (*int, default 0*) – The index to start searching from.
        * **stop** (*int, optional*) – The index to stop searching at. If not provided, the search
          continues to the end of the Group.

    Returns:
    :   The index of the member with the given name.

    Return type:
    :   *int*

    Raises:
    :   **ValueError** – If membername is not in this Group.

Group.iter\_blocks(*\**, *recursive: bool = False*) → Iterator[[Block](../../block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block")][](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.iter_blocks "Link to this definition")

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/group/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.iter_members.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Group.iter\_members[](#keysight-pwdatatools-group-iter-members "Link to this heading")

Group.iter\_members(*\**, *recursive: bool = False*) → Iterator[Member][](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.iter_members "Link to this definition")
:   Iterate over the members.

    Unlike the [`__iter__()`](keysight.pwdatatools.Group.__iter__.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.__iter__ "keysight.pwdatatools.Group.__iter__") method, this method can optionally
    iterate recursively over all members (including members inside
    child Groups).

    Parameters:
    :   **recursive** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, iterate recursively over all members (including
        members inside child Groups). If `False`, do not iterate
        over the members inside child Groups.

    Notes

    Each iteration yields a child Group or Block. If
    `recursive=True` and a child Group exists, the child Group is
    yielded, then its members are yielded one at a time. If a child
    Group has a child Group, that child Group is yielded, then its
    members are yielded one at a time, and so on.

    Return type:
    :   *Iterator*[Member]


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/group/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.to_file.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Group.to\_file[](#keysight-pwdatatools-group-to-file "Link to this heading")

Group.to\_file(*dst: str | PathLike | BaseDataFile*, *dst\_mode: Literal['x', 'w', 'a'] = 'x'*, *\**, *dst\_format: str | None = None*, *format\_specific: FormatSpecificWriteOptions | None = None*, *iffs: bool = False*, *loc: str = '/'*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.to_file "Link to this definition")
:   Write Group to a file.

    If the destination file has a missing or unknown file extension,
    you must either 1) input a [`DataFile`](../../../fileio/datafile/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile "keysight.pwdatatools.DataFile") with a
    `format_override` as `dst`, or 2) use the `dst_format`
    parameter. Known writeable file extensions are stored in
    `options.writing.extentions`. Known writeable file formats
    are stored in `options.writing.formats`.

    Whether metadata is written depends on the format of output file.
    If the output file’s format has places to store metadata (e.g.
    such as in the native pwdatatools format or HDF5 format), then
    metadata is automatically included in the output file.

    Parameters:
    :   * **dst** (*str, os.PathLike, or DataFile*) – The destination file path.
        * **dst\_mode** (*{‘x’, ‘w’, ‘a’}, default ‘x’*) – The mode to use when opening `dst`. If `dst` does not yet
          exist, all three modes yield the same behavior. However, the 3
          modes have differing behaviors if attempting to write to a pre-
          existing file. `'w'` overwrites the existing file. `'a'`
          appends to the existing file. `'x'` results in a
          FileExistsError.
        * **dst\_format** (*str, optional*) – Sets the destination file’s format. Supported types are stored
          in `options.writing.formats`. Any value other than
          `None` overrides the format inferred from or set by `dst`.
        * **format\_specific** (*FormatSpecificWriteOptions, optional*) – Defines the format-specific options for writing `dst`. By
          default, the global option
          `options.writing.format_specific` sets format-specific
          writing options, but this parameter overrides the global
          options. If provided, it must be an instance of
          `FormatSpecificWriteOptions`. See the docs for a list
          of all classes that are format-specific write options. Some
          examples are `ADSWriteOptions`,
          `MDIFWriteOptions`, `ADSWriteInvalid`, and
          `MDIFWriteInvalid`.
        * **iffs** (*bool, default False*) – If `True`, keep any intermediate files created during
          processing.
        * **loc** (*str, default ‘/’*) – The write location within `dst`. Only applies to file
          formats that support multiple levels of hierarchy (for
          example, native and HDF5 formats). This parameter sets the
          write location within the file’s directory-like (or tree-like)
          structure. The “root” location is ‘/’.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Raises:
    :   * **FileExistsError** – If `dst_mode='x'` and `dst` already exists.
        * **ValueError** – If `dst_mode='a'` and append mode is not supported for the
          format of `dst`.

    Examples

    The below examples assume that `group` is an instance of
    [`Group`](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "keysight.pwdatatools._api.group.Group") and the following
    imports:

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import DataFile
    >>> from pathlib import Path
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` has a known file extension, you can
    simply input `dst` as a str, `pathlib.Path`, or
    `DataFile`. Known file
    extensions are stored in `options.writing.extentions`.

    ```
    >>> # str path with known ext (.ds)
    >>> group.to_file('/home/s_param.ds')
    >>> # Path with known ext (.mdf)
    >>> group.to_file(Path('/home/noise_param.mdf'))
    >>>  # DataFile with known ext
    >>> group.to_file(DataFile('/home/noise_param.pwdt'))
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` has a missing or non-standard file
    extension, you can use the `dst_format` parameter. Alternatively,
    you can input an instance of
    `DataFile` as the `dst`
    argument and use the DataFile’s `format_override` parameter to
    define the destination’s format.

    ```
    >>> # no ext solved by dst_format
    >>> group.to_file('/data/s_param', dst_format='mdif')
    >>> # non-standard ext solved by dst_format
    >>> group.to_file('/data/noise_param.txt', dst_format='mdif')
    >>> # no ext solved by DataFile
    >>> group.to_file(DataFile('/data/noise_param', format_override='mdif'))
    ```

    If the destination file `dst` already exists, a FileExistsError is
    raised if `dst_mode='x'`, which is the default. A `dst_mode`
    parameter value of `'w'` will overwrite the entire existing file,
    while a value of `'a'` will append to the existing file.

    Note

    Only certain types of datafiles work for `dst_mode='a'`. A
    ValueError is raised if append mode is not supported for the format
    of `dst`.

    ```
    >>> group.to_file('/sparam.ds', dst_mode='w')  # overwrite dst, if it exists
    >>> group.to_file('/sparam.mdf', dst_mode='a') # append to dst, if it exists
    >>> group.to_file('/run1.pwdt', dst_mode='a') # append to dst, if it exists
    ```

    See also

    [`write_file()`](../../../fileio/write_file.md#keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.write_file "keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.write_file")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/group/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Group.tree.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Group.tree[](#keysight-pwdatatools-group-tree "Link to this heading")

Group.tree() → str[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group.tree "Link to this definition")
:   Return a tree view of this Group, similar to a directory tree.

    In the tree, each sub-Group is represented as a “folder” and each
    Block is represented as a “file”. This Group is treated as the
    “root” directory.

    The tree is returned as a string with newlines separating each
    level of the tree. The tree is indented to show the hierarchy.
    The string is meant to be printed to the console or shown in a
    GUI, not for programmatic use.

    Returns:
    :   Representation of the Group as a tree.

    Return type:
    :   *str*

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> group = pwdt.read_file_as_group('/home/s_param.ds')
    >>> print(group.tree())
    <[/] Group 's_param'>
    ├── <[0] Block 'Sweep1.SP1.SP' with 4 Vars and 6 observations>
    ├── <[1] Block 'aele_0.Sweep1.SP1' with 3 Vars and 6 observations>
    └── <[2] Block 'aele_1.Sweep1.SP1' with 2 Vars and 2 observations>
    ```


---

# Group[](#group "Link to this heading")

This page documents public attributes and methods available in the Group class. For more information and examples, see [Use the Group Class](../../../howto/use_group_class.md#use-group-class).

*class* Group(*members: Iterable[Member] | None = None*, *\**, *name: str | None = None*, *attrs: AttrsDictLike | None = None*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Group "Link to this definition")
:   A hierarchical dataset or a portion of a hierarchical dataset.

    It behaves like a mutable sequence of member objects. Its
    members may consist of Blocks and/or child Groups.

    Parameters:
    :   * **members** (*iterable of Blocks and/or Groups, optional*) – An iterable containing objects which will become members, or
          children, of the Group. These may be Blocks or other Groups.
          Note that there are several subtypes of Block, such as
          LoadPullBlock, which are also supported.
        * **name** (*str, optional*) – A str name for the Group. If not specified, the name is set to
          an empty string.
        * **attrs** (*dict-like, optional*) – A dict or dict-like object of arbitrary Group-level metadata. The
          keys must be strings and the values must be one of the following:
          str, int, float, complex, bool, None, dict, list, or
          numpy.ndarray. Nested dicts and lists are allowed.

    Examples

    Below, assume `block1` and `block2` are instances of Block and
    `group1` is another, pre-existing instance of Group.

    ```
    >>> from keysight import pwdatatools as pwdt
    >>> attrs = {'foo': 1, 'bar': 42}
    >>> group2 = pwdt.Group([block1, block2, group1], name='baz', attrs=attrs)
    ```

    See also

    [`read_file_as_group()`](../../fileio/read_file_as_group.md#keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.read_file_as_group "keysight.pwdatatools._api.funcs.read_file_as_group"), [`Block`](../block/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Block "keysight.pwdatatools._api.block.Block")

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/var/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.__array_ufunc__.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Var.\_\_array\_ufunc\_\_[](#keysight-pwdatatools-var-array-ufunc "Link to this heading")

Var.\_\_array\_ufunc\_\_(*ufunc*, *method*, *\*inputs*, *\*\*kwargs*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.__array_ufunc__ "Link to this definition")
:   Provide compatibility with some numpy ufuncs.

    Var instances are not returned from numpy ufuncs because it is
    unclear what meaning of the metadata would be after the ufunc is
    applied. Instead, it is assumed that only the data is of
    interest.

    Parameters:
    :   * **ufunc** (*callable*) – The ufunc to apply.
        * **method** (*str*) – The method to apply. This is always “\_\_call\_\_”.
        * **inputs** (*tuple*) – The inputs to the ufunc.
        * **kwargs** (*dict*) – The keyword arguments to the ufunc.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/var/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.__call__.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Var.\_\_call\_\_[](#keysight-pwdatatools-var-call "Link to this heading")

Var.\_\_call\_\_(*\*one\_based\_int\_indexes: int*) → [Var](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.__call__ "Link to this definition")
:   Return new Var, interpreting args as 1-based int idxs for higher dimensions.

    This is used exclusively for indexing into multidimensional Vars and is
    meant to provide a convenient way to use 1-based indexing. The indexing
    is not applied to axis 0 (only axis 1 and higher). A 1D Var is always
    returned.

    Parameters:
    :   **one\_based\_int\_indexes** (*int*) – The 1-based integer indexes to use for the higher dimensions.

    Returns:
    :   A new Var with the same name, data, attrs, and unit as this Var,
        but with a 1D data array and empty dims.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> data = np.arange(20).reshape((5, 2, 2))
    >>> var = Var(data, name='S')
    >>> var
    Var(
        <Int64 data with shape (5, 2, 2)>,
        name='S',
        dims=<empty Dims>,
        role='',
        unit=None,
        attrs={},
    )
    >>> var12 = var(1, 2)
    >>> var12
    Var(
        <Int64 data with shape (5,)>,
        name='S(1,2)',
        dims=<empty Dims>,
        role='',
        unit=None,
        attrs={},
    )
    >>> var12.to_numpy_ndarray()
    array([ 1,  5,  9, 13, 17])
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/var/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.__getitem__.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Var.\_\_getitem\_\_[](#keysight-pwdatatools-var-getitem "Link to this heading")

Var.\_\_getitem\_\_(*item*) → [Var](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.__getitem__ "Link to this definition")
:   Return a new Var from selection.

    This uses numpy’s indexing syntax. The selection is applied to the
    data array and possibly the dims.

    The dimension-related metadata (dims) is modified to reflect the
    selection. All other metadata is copied to the new Var.

    Parameters:
    :   **item** (*int, slice, or ArrayLike*) – The selection to apply. This can be a scalar, a slice, or
        ArrayLike. If ArrayLike, it must be the same length as the
        dimension being selected along. If a boolean array, it is
        interpreted as a mask. Otherwise, it is interpreted as a list
        of values to select.

    Returns:
    :   A new Var with the same name, attrs, role, and unit as this
        Var, but with a subset of the data and possibly new dims.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/var/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.__init__.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Var.\_\_init\_\_[](#keysight-pwdatatools-var-init "Link to this heading")

Var.\_\_init\_\_(*data: ArrayLike*, *dtype: [DataType](../../../public_submodules/datatypes/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.datatypes.DataType.md#keysight.pwdatatools.datatypes.DataType "keysight.pwdatatools.datatypes.DataType") | Type[[DataType](../../../public_submodules/datatypes/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.datatypes.DataType.md#keysight.pwdatatools.datatypes.DataType "keysight.pwdatatools.datatypes.DataType")] | None = None*, *\**, *name: str | None = None*, *dims: DimsLike | None = None*, *role: str | None = None*, *unit: str | None = None*, *attrs: AttrsDictLike | None = None*)[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.__init__ "Link to this definition")
:   Initialize a new Var instance with data and optional metadata.

    Parameters:
    :   * **data** (*ArrayLike*) – An array-like object containing the data values.
        * **dtype** (*DataType, optional*) – The datatype. If not specified, it is inferred from the data.
        * **name** (*str, optional*) – A str name for the variable. If not specified, the name is set
          to an empty string.
        * **dims** (*DimsLike, optional*) – Optional metadata relating to the variable’s dimensions.
        * **role** (*str, optional*) – Metadata defining the part or function this variable serves
          within a dataset (for example, ‘frequency’ or ‘gamma.load’).
        * **unit** (*str, optional*) – An optional string representing the unit of the variable.
        * **attrs** (*dict-like, optional*) – An optional dict or dict-like object with arbitrary attributes
          (metadata) for the variable. The keys must be str and the values
          must be str, int, float, complex, bool, None, dict, list, or
          numpy.ndarray. Nested dicts and lists are allowed.

    Examples

    Below we create a 1D Var. The data is required, but all other
    parameters are optional.

    ```
    >>> import numpy as np
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> data = np.array([1, 2, 3])
    >>> vout = Var(data, name='Vout', role='voltage', unit='V')
    >>> vout.info()
    kind           -
    role     voltage
    dtype      Int32
    shape       (3,)
    dims           -
    unit           V
    min            1
    max            3
    null           -
    nan            -
    attrs          -
    Name: Vout, dtype: string
    ```

    Now, let’s instantiate a 1D Var with a numpy MaskedArray to represent
    missing or invalid data points. Note that the masked value(s) become
    the null value(s) and are not included in the min and max values.

    ```
    >>> ma = np.ma.masked_array([3, 4], mask=[True, False])
    >>> v_masked = Var(ma, name='masked')
    >>> v_masked.info()
    kind         -
    role         -
    dtype    Int32
    shape     (2,)
    dims         -
    unit         -
    min          4
    max          4
    null         1
    nan          -
    attrs        -
    Name: masked, dtype: string
    ```

    Next, let’s create a 2D Var.

    ```
    >>> data = np.random.random(6).reshape((3, 2))
    >>> portz = Var(data, name='PortZ', role='impedance.port', unit='Ohm')
    ```

    Using pandas or polars can be very complementary to pwdatatools. Here,
    let’s create a pandas DataFrame in order to more easily visualize our
    2D Var. Note that 1-based integer indexes are used for the higher
    dimension (the numbers inside of the square brackets).

    Note

    There are other options for indexing into the higher dimensions
    of a variable, such as 0-based integer and string name indexes.

    ```
    >>> df = portz.to_pandas_dataframe()
    >>> print(df)
    PortZ[1]  PortZ[2]
    0  0.551544  0.467092
    1  0.813803  0.629148
    2  0.146644  0.529128
    ```

    Or, if you prefer, you can use MultiIndex columns to get rid of the delimiters.

    ```
    >>> df = portz.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_nlevels=2)
    >>> print(df)
    varname     PortZ
    i               1         2
    0        0.551544  0.467092
    1        0.813803  0.629148
    2        0.146644  0.529128
    ```

    See also

    For more information on pandas DataFrame indexing, see
    [pandas DataFrame Indexing](../../../../core_concepts/pandas_dataframe_indexing.md#pandas-dataframe-indexing) and also the pandas documentation.

    Finally, let’s create a 3D Var. This time, we have two higher
    dimensions (always named “i” and “j”). Let’s define string names to
    use as indexes along these higher dimensions instead of integers.

    ```
    >>> data = np.random.random(20).reshape((5, 2, 2))
    >>> dims = Dims(ndim=3, i_names=['P1', 'P2'], j_names=['P1', 'P2'])
    >>> s = Var(data, name='S', dims=dims, role='network_parameters.s')
    >>> df = s.to_pandas_dataframe(cols_nlevels=3)
    >>> print(df)
    varname         S
    i              P1                  P2
    j              P1        P2        P1        P2
    0        0.551284  0.070031  0.586010  0.885456
    1        0.626966  0.234664  0.932699  0.882455
    2        0.137112  0.213657  0.657413  0.388838
    3        0.410304  0.545891  0.158400  0.034383
    4        0.009548  0.688676  0.918804  0.848939
    ```

# keysight.pwdatatools.Var.attrs[](#keysight-pwdatatools-var-attrs "Link to this heading")

*property* Var.attrs*: [AttrsDict](../../../metadata/attrsdict/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict "keysight.pwdatatools._core.properties.AttrsDict")*[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.attrs "Link to this definition")
:   A dict of arbitrary attributes (metadata).

    The attributes are stored in an [`AttrsDict`](../../../metadata/attrsdict/index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict "keysight.pwdatatools.AttrsDict"), which is a
    type-restricted dict. The keys must be str, and the values must
    be one of the following types: str, int, float, complex, bool,
    None, `numpy.ndarray`, dict, or list. Nested dicts and
    lists are also supported.

    Examples

    Initialize a Var with no attrs.

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> gain = Var([10, 12, 13], name='Gain')
    ```

    Add some attrs to the empty AttrsDict.

    ```
    >>> gain.attrs['foo'] = 'bar'
    >>> gain.attrs['baz'] = 1
    >>> gain.attrs['qux'] = [1, 2, 3]
    >>> gain.attrs['quux'] = {'a': 1, 'b': 2}
    >>> gain.attrs
    AttrsDict({'foo': 'bar', 'baz': 1, 'qux': [1, 2, 3], 'quux': {'a': 1, 'b': 2}})
    ```

    Set the value of attrs.

    ```
    >>> gain.attrs = {'foo': 'bar'}
    >>> gain.attrs
    AttrsDict({'foo': 'bar'})
    ```

    Initialize a Var with attrs.

    ```
    >>> gain = Var([10, 12, 13], name='Gain', attrs={'foo': 'bar'})
    >>> gain.attrs
    AttrsDict({'foo': 'bar'})
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/var/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.block.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Var.block[](#keysight-pwdatatools-var-block "Link to this heading")

*property* Var.block*: BaseBlock | None*[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.block "Link to this definition")
:   The Block that contains this Var.

    This property is set by the Block when the Var is added to it.
    A Var may be associated with one Block. If the Var is not
    associated with a Block, then this property is None.

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/var/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.copy.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Var.copy[](#keysight-pwdatatools-var-copy "Link to this heading")

Var.copy() → Self[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.copy "Link to this definition")
:   Return a deep copy of this Var.

    Returns:
    :   A deep copy of this Var.

    Return type:
    :   [*Var*](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools.Var")

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> gain = Var([1, 2, 3], name='Gain', unit='dB', role='gain', attrs={'foo': 1})
    >>> gain2 = gain.copy()
    >>> gain2.name == gain.name
    True
    >>> gain2.attrs == gain.attrs
    True
    >>> gain2.attrs is gain.attrs
    False
    ```


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/var/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.copy_metadata_in_place.md === -->

# keysight.pwdatatools.Var.copy\_metadata\_in\_place[](#keysight-pwdatatools-var-copy-metadata-in-place "Link to this heading")

Var.copy\_metadata\_in\_place(*other: [Var](../index.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var "keysight.pwdatatools._core.variables.var.Var")*) → None[](#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.copy_metadata_in_place "Link to this definition")
:   Copy metadata from other Var and assign it to this Var.

    The name of this Var remains the same and is not copied from the
    other Var. However, all other metadata is copied, including the
    attrs, dims, role, and unit.

    Parameters:
    :   **other** (*Var*) – The other Var from which to copy the metadata.

    Return type:
    :   *None*

    Examples

    ```
    >>> from keysight.pwdatatools import Var
    >>> gain = Var(
    ...     [10, 12, 13],
    ...     name='Gain',
    ...     role='gain',
    ...     unit='dB',
    ...     attrs={'foo': 'bar'}
    ... )
    >>> gain2 = Var([10, 12, 13], name='Gain2')
    >>> gain2.copy_metadata_in_place(gain)
    >>> gain2
    Var(
        <Int64 data with shape (3,)>,
        name='Gain2',
        dims=<empty Dims>,
        role='gain',
        unit='dB',
        attrs={'foo': ...},
    )
    ```

    See also

    [`Var.rename`](keysight.pwdatatools.Var.rename.md#keysight.pwdatatools.Var.rename "keysight.pwdatatools.Var.rename")


---

<!-- === 来源: api_reference/main/var/_autosummary/keysight.pwdatatools.Var.count_observations.md === -->
